### [1] [Self-interacting $p$-forms and the shear-free anisotropic cosmological model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07288)
*Nicolas Hernandez,Ben D. Normann,Yeinzon Rodriguez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends eFLRW cosmologies to non-Abelian p-forms (p=0–4), showing that only a specific 3-form field with quadratic potential V(H²)=A+BH² balances anisotropic curvature without fine-tuning—uniquely enabling isotropic expansion without shear or vorticity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To relax the cosmological principle by requiring only isotropic expansion (no vorticity, zero shear) rather than full isotropy and homogeneity, realized via extended FLRW (eFLRW) models where anisotropic spatial curvature is balanced by anisotropic stress.

Method: Analyzes non-Abelian p-form fields (p = 0,…,4) with self-interactions in Bianchi class A spacetimes; imposes vanishing shear and isotropic expansion; solves gauge-field continuity equation coupled with eFLRW constraint requiring anisotropic stress ∝ a⁻²; examines fine-tuning conditions for generic potentials versus the specific quadratic 3-form potential.

Result: Generic non-Abelian p-forms require fine-tuned potentials to balance anisotropic curvature; the only exception is a non-Abelian 3-form H^a with V(H²) = A + B H²: an uncharged version achieves exact balance in Bianchi class A without tuning, and a charged version does so with appropriate choice of gauge connection.

Conclusion: The 3-form with quadratic potential is the unique non-Abelian p-form candidate realizing eFLRW cosmology without fine-tuning; it simultaneously mimics a cosmological constant plus curvature-like dark matter, offering a geometrically natural alternative to ΛCDM.

Abstract: The cosmological principle may require more than observations demand: what is needed is isotropic expansion without vorticity. Extended Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker (eFLRW) cosmologies realise this with a non-vanishing anisotropic spatial curvature that is balanced by an anisotropic stress while the shear vanishes. Thorsrud and others investigated such scenarios for free differential $p$-forms. Here we extend the analysis to non-Abelian $p$-forms ($p=0,\dots,4$) with self interactions. The balance requires the anisotropic stress to decay as $a^{-2}$, $a$ being the cosmological scale factor, which we combine with the gauge-field continuity equation. The general results indicate that, in the presence of a potential, fine-tuning of the same is needed in order to balance the curvature. Notably, this is not the case for a $3$-form field $\underline{H}^a$ with a potential term $V(\underline{H}^2)=A+B\,\underline{H}^2$, where $A$ and $B$ are constants: in Bianchi class A, an uncharged 3-form balances the curvature with this potential without any tuning, and a charged one does so for a suitably chosen connection. This is the only candidate we have found where the potential does not require tuning. It is also an interesting candidate as it can be interpreted as a cosmological constant in combination with a dark-matter sector mimicking curvature.

</details>


### [2] [Kalb-Ramond black bounces: throat-blind shadows and throat-sensitive neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18697)
*Faizuddin Ahmed,Ahmad Al-Badawi,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates observational signatures of a regularized black hole core (a 'bounce') in the Simpson-Visser model derived from a Lorentz-violating Kalb-Ramond background; it identifies which astrophysical observables are sensitive to the bounce throat—specifically photon ring instability, subring brightness, neutrino oscillation phase, and neutrino pair annihilation energy—and derives universal scaling laws, demonstrating separability of mass and throat via combined photon/neutrino lensing and confirming visibility in ray-traced polarized images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify astrophysical observables capable of detecting the regularized core ('bounce') in black hole spacetimes, distinguishing them from standard singular black holes.

Method: Analytical derivation of observable dependencies on the bounce throat using the Simpson-Visser metric built from a power-law hairy black hole induced by a Lorentz-violating Kalb-Ramond field; exact computation of shadow, ISCO, redshift, photon ring stability, subring demagnification, neutrino flavor evolution, and pair annihilation energy; ray-tracing of polarized images for a turbulent thin disk; cross-verification via two independent computational methods.

Result: Shadow, ISCO, orbital frequencies, and gravitational redshift are insensitive to the bounce; photon ring instability, subring brightness, neutrino oscillation phase, and neutrino pair annihilation energy depend on the throat via a universal factor; an exact universal law for subring demagnification is derived; weak-field neutrino phase and light deflection encode the throat with distinct weights, enabling mass–throat separation via multi-messenger lensing; ray-traced images show direct image hides the throat while the first subring reveals it; throat modifies neutrino flavor survival and enhances annihilation energy deposition near stellar-mass objects.

Conclusion: The regularized black hole core leaves distinct, measurable imprints in high-precision strong-field and multi-messenger observables—particularly in photon subrings and neutrino quantum interference—enabling empirical discrimination from singular black holes, provided sufficiently resolved subring imaging and neutrino timing/spectroscopy.

Abstract: Regularizing a black hole removes its singularity, but which observations could detect the regularized core? We address this question for a Simpson-Visser black bounce built on the power-law hairy black hole that a Lorentz-violating Kalb-Ramond background produces. Measured in the areal radius, the bounce leaves the metric function untouched and changes only the radial measure. This single fact sorts the observables into two classes. The shadow, the innermost stable orbit, orbital frequencies and gravitational redshifts are blind to the throat. The instability of the photon ring, the brightness of its subrings, the oscillation phase of neutrinos and the energy deposited by neutrino pair annihilation respond to it through one universal factor. We derive an exact law for the subring demagnification that holds for any metric function. We also show that the throat enters the weak-field neutrino phase and the light deflection with different weights, so that combined photon and neutrino lensing can separate the mass from the throat. Ray-traced polarized images of a turbulent thin disk confirm that the direct image hides the throat while the first subring reveals it. For neutrinos emitted near a stellar-mass compact object the throat reshapes the flavor survival pattern and enhances the annihilation energy deposition. Two independent computations reproduce each headline number.

</details>


### [3] [Metric perturbations in Einstein-Hilbert gravity: An alternative perturbative formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18698)
*Sergey Bondarenko,Raghvendra Singh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares exponential and inverse-metric-density field variables for quantizing gravity, deriving gauge-fixed quadratic operators of the Einstein–Hilbert action on arbitrary backgrounds; it shows that while both yield equivalent tree-level physics (e.g., identical 3-point amplitudes and Schwarzschild solution behavior), their gauge-fixed operators differ by a Ricci-curvature term—vanishing on Ricci-flat or Einstein backgrounds—and clarifies implications for quantum loop computations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in field variable choice (exponential vs. inverse-metric-density) for perturbative quantum gravity, particularly regarding gauge-fixing, background dependence, operator equivalence, and consistency of classical solutions and scattering amplitudes.

Method: Derives gauge-fixed quadratic and cubic operators from the 4D Einstein–Hilbert action using exponential h_μν (g_μν = η_μν + h_μν) and inverse-metric-density χ_μν (g^μν = √−η η^μν + χ_μν) variables on arbitrary background metrics; employs background-harmonic gauge transported via exact field map; computes Faddeev–Popov operators; evaluates tadpole contributions, 3-point vertices on flat space, and tests Schwarzschild solution under unrestricted and radial-restricted variations.

Result: The density-variable quadratic operator is the trace-reversed Lichnerowicz operator; the exponential one includes an extra algebraic Ricci term due to nonlinear tadpole transformation—cancelling on Ricci-flat and Einstein backgrounds; both yield identical gauge-invariant 3-point amplitudes (matching Einstein-gravity normalization); Schwarzschild satisfies background-harmonic gauge and stationarity in both variables, but radial restriction loses an equation; full quantum loops require unified measure, regulator, and source prescription.

Conclusion: Exponential and inverse-metric-density variables produce classically and tree-level-equivalent physics when consistently gauge-fixed with background-harmonic condition, but their quadratic operators differ off-shell and on non-Einstein backgrounds—highlighting the importance of variable-aware regularization and measure definition for quantum gravity.

Abstract: We derive the ordinary gauge-fixed quadratic operators of the four-dimensional Einstein--Hilbert action in exponential and inverse-metric-density variables on an arbitrary reference background. With a single nonlinear background-harmonic gauge transported through the exact field map, the density operator is the trace-reversed Lichnerowicz operator, while the exponential operator contains an additional algebraic Ricci-curvature term. This difference arises from the nonlinear transformation of the background tadpole and vanishes on Ricci-flat backgrounds; a cosmological-constant check gives the corresponding cancellation at Einstein saddles. The construction retains the exact determinant contribution and specifies the associated Faddeev--Popov operator. At the first interacting order on a flat background, we show that the two gauge-fixed cubic actions differ by a term proportional to the free field equation, and independently evaluate the same nonzero three-point coefficient in both variables, with its normalization matched to the Einstein-gravity interaction. An exact Schwarzschild configuration satisfies the same background-harmonic gauge and is stationary under unrestricted exterior variations of the gauge-fixed action. Its density and exponential reconstructions are checked together with the bulk and boundary terms; a separate radial variation illustrates the loss of an equation after imposing a restricted ansatz. These results provide an explicit comparison of established field coordinates at the classical and tree levels; quantum loop calculations additionally require a common measure, regulator and source prescription.

</details>


### [4] [Thermodynamics and interior structure of density-responsive regular black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18705)
*Martin Drobczyk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates regular black holes in density-responsive gravity (DRG), deriving an effective two-horizon metric from a Gaussian matter profile mapped via a local running prescription; it calibrates extremal mass, analyzes nonmonotonic Hawking temperature, verifies universal near-extremal scaling of the lapse diagnostic, computes cavity diagnostics and entropy loss, and confirms de Sitter-like core structure—though pressure mismatch persists and full dynamical formation remains unestablished.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable regular black hole models within density-responsive gravity (DRG) by linking scalar-sector phenomenology to geometric and thermodynamic properties, particularly addressing extremality, horizon structure, entropy evolution, and consistency with quantum-gravity-motivated features like de Sitter cores and near-extremal scaling.

Method: Algebraic mapping of Gaussian matter profile to density profile via phenomenological local running; static metric ansatz selection based on pressure consistency checks; extremal mass calibration using scalar response amplitude A; fitting of density profile ρ(r) ∝ exp[−(r/rc)^n] to determine n; thermodynamic analysis including Hawking temperature and lapse diagnostic ℤ_tr; regulated coordinate-time integral for static cavity diagnostic; area-entropy accounting across mass evolution; inverse source reconstruction for core geometry; exploratory numerical collapse with bounce factor.

Result: Extremal mass calibrated to M_min ≈ 1.006 M_P; n = 2 best fits density profile; nonmonotonic Hawking temperature peaking at T_H^max = 0.02547 M_P; universal near-extremal scaling ℤ_tr ∝ (M − M_min)^(−1/2) confirmed for n = 2,3,4,6 and Hayward/Bardeen controls; static cavity diagnostic defined but not predictive of GW echoes; entropy loss crosses 50% at M_cross/M_0 = 1/√2; final area entropy <2.6% (M_0=5M_P) or <0.03% (M_0=50M_P) of initial Bekenstein-Hawking entropy; inverse reconstruction confirms isotropic de Sitter core but pressure mismatch remains; bounce-driven collapse yields bounded configurations without full geometry formation.

Conclusion: The DRG-motivated regular black hole model yields robust, calibrated thermodynamics and universal near-extremal behavior, supporting its viability as a phenomenological framework—but fails to resolve the pressure inconsistency in full static DRG equations, and dynamical formation of the exact calibrated geometry remains unconfirmed. The results constrain entropy evolution and horizon structure but do not establish unitarity or a Page curve.

Abstract: This work studies regular black holes motivated by the scalar sector of density-responsive gravity (DRG). An algebraic prescription with phenomenological local running maps a Gaussian matter profile to a diagnostic density profile. A pressure check shows that this profile does not solve the full static DRG equations. It serves only to select an effective metric family with two horizons. The extremal mass is calibrated to $M_{\min} = \sqrt{1+A/2}\,M_{\mathrm{P}} \simeq 1.006\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$, where $A=0.024$ is the historical scalar response amplitude. A fit to $\rho(r) = \rho_0\exp[-(r/r_c)^n]$ selects $n=2$. The temperature is nonmonotonic, reaching $T_H^{\max}=0.02547\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ at $M=1.332\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ before vanishing at $M_{\min}$. The calculation verifies the generic scaling near extremality $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{tr}}\propto(M-M_{\min})^{-1/2}$ of a static lapse diagnostic for $n=2,3,4,6$ and for the Hayward and Bardeen controls. A regulated integral of coordinate time between the horizons provides a static cavity diagnostic, not a prediction of echoes of gravitational waves. The remaining area entropy equals the area entropy already lost at $M_{\mathrm{cross}}/M_0=0.707107\simeq1/\sqrt{2}$. This geometric crossing does not establish a Page curve or unitarity. The endpoint area entropy is below $2.6\%$ of the initial Bekenstein-Hawking entropy for $M_0 = 5\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ and below $0.03\%$ for $M_0 = 50\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$. An inverse source reconstruction confirms an isotropic core of de Sitter type but retains the pressure mismatch. An exploratory collapse calculation with an imposed bounce factor produces bounded configurations with $1-2m/r<0$ without establishing formation of the calibrated geometry. The robust results are the calibrated thermodynamics and generic scaling near extremality.

</details>


### [5] [Holographic Einstein rings of a covariant quantum corrected AdS black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18716)
*Zhenglong Ban,Xiaomei Liu,Rong-Jia Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic Einstein rings in a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild–AdS₄ spacetime using a massless scalar probe, finding that the deformation parameter ζ modifies photon potential and boundary time normalization while leaving key geometric quantities invariant at fixed horizon radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle finite-radius quantum corrections from boundary time normalization effects in holographic optics of black holes, using Einstein rings as a geometric probe.

Method: Ray tracing and local WKB analysis of null geodesics for a massless scalar field in a covariant quantum-corrected Schwarzschild–AdS₄ metric; quasinormal mode computation with AdS boundary conditions; comparison of coordinate vs. boundary-frame incidence angles and impact parameters.

Result: At fixed r_h, ζ leaves metric mass, coordinate surface gravity, and photon sphere radius invariant but increases photon potential maximum and asymptotic lapse A = 1 + ζ²; critical impact parameter → A⁻¹ᐟ² for large black holes; boundary-frame incidence angle → π/2; ray tracing and WKB yield consistent geometric scale; QNM spectra confirm separation of finite-radius and boundary-normalization effects.

Conclusion: The deformation parameter ζ introduces a clean split between bulk geometric effects (encoded in photon orbits) and boundary time rescaling (encoded in A), enabling precise holographic interpretation of quantum corrections via Einstein ring observables.

Abstract: We investigate holographic Einstein rings in a covariant quantum corrected Schwarzschild--AdS$_4$ spacetime using a massless scalar probe. At fixed horizon radius $r_h$, the deformation parameter $\zeta$ leaves the metric mass parameter, coordinate surface gravity, and photon sphere radius unchanged, while increasing the photon potential maximum and the asymptotic lapse coefficient $A=1+\zeta^2$. The critical coordinate impact parameter approaches $A^{-1/2}$ for large black holes, whereas the corresponding incidence angle in the normalized boundary frame approaches $\pi/2$. Ray tracing and local WKB barrier data recover the same geometric scale. Quasinormal spectra computed with AdS boundary conditions provide a comparison at finite multipole number. These results separate the finite radius effect of the deformation from the change in boundary time normalization.

</details>


### [6] [Asymptotic stability of axisymmetric perturbations of near-horizon extremal Kerr with a Schwarzian-deformed throat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18922)
*Marcela Cárdenas,Benjamín Cuevas-Tapia,Claudio Muñoz,Jessica Trespalacios,Hanne Van Den Bosch*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes asymptotic stability of axisymmetric gravitational perturbations on deformed near-horizon extremal Kerr (NHEK) geometries, where the AdS₂ factor is modified by a Schwarzian mode; it links background deformations to the KdV hierarchy, derives energy/virial estimates, establishes stability results (conserved energy for NHEK, Lyapunov behavior for nonconstant Schwarzian), identifies a novel horizon-localized monotone norm, and computes Regge–Teitelboim charges at late-time throat corners.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational stability in the near-horizon regime of (near-)extremal Kerr black holes—particularly how deformations encoded in the Schwarzian derivative affect stability, symmetry preservation, and horizon physics—motivated by the role of NHEK in the Kerr/CFT correspondence and universality of near-horizon geometry.

Method: Uses geometric analysis of Einstein’s equations linearized around a family of axisymmetric, $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})\times \mathfrak{u}(1)$-invariant near-horizon backgrounds parameterized by Schwarzian modes; employs Gelfand–Dikii polynomials as natural Killing solutions under locality constraints; reduces axisymmetric perturbations to a wave equation on nearly-AdS₂; applies energy estimates, virial identities, and Lyapunov functional analysis; constructs a radial ADM foliation to compute Regge–Teitelboim charges at late-time corners.

Result: Proves asymptotic stability for NHEK in a weighted norm (with conserved energy and integrated local energy decay); shows the energy becomes a Lyapunov functional for nonconstant Schwarzian backgrounds, yielding uniform bounds for integrable modes and mild local decay along KdV solitons; discovers a new monotone horizon-localized norm distinct from Aretakis-type norms; computes finite, well-defined Regge–Teitelboim charges at the late-time corner of the throat.

Conclusion: Gravitational stability in near-extremal Kerr throats is robust but highly sensitive to the Schwarzian deformation of the AdS₂ base: symmetry-preserving deformations governed by the KdV hierarchy induce qualitatively distinct stability behaviors—ranging from strict asymptotic decay (NHEK) to controlled Lyapunov evolution (solitonic backgrounds)—and reveal novel horizon-localized dynamics not captured by standard Aretakis analysis.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic stability of axisymmetric gravitational perturbations of the near-horizon geometries of (near-)extremal Kerr black holes and consider the family of backgrounds in which the AdS$_{2}$ factor of NHEK is deformed by a Schwarzian mode. Requiring the Killing parameter to be local in the background makes the Gelfand--Dikii polynomials natural solutions of the Killing equation, and that locality requirement brings stationary solutions of the Korteweg-de Vries equation. The hierarchy selects a family of backgrounds, which remain $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})\times \mathfrak{u}(1)$ throughout them: the first level gives NHEK and near-NHEK, the second, solitons that depart from one of these geometries and return to it. We linearize Einstein's equations around this family, where the axisymmetric sector reduces to a wave equation on the nearly-AdS$_{2}$ base, and analyze this equation by energy and virial methods. For NHEK we obtain a conserved energy, an integrated local energy estimate and asymptotic stability in a weighted norm; for a nonconstant Schwarzian the energy becomes a Lyapunov functional whose monotonicity is dictated by the sign of its derivative, yielding uniform bounds for integrable ones, and mild local decay along the KdV soliton. We also find a monotone horizon-localized norm, which is not of Aretakis type, showing that stability properties are directly sensitive to the near-horizon structure encoded in the Schwarzian background. We compute the associated Regge--Teitelboim charges at a late-time corner of the throat using a radial ADM foliation of the spacetime.

</details>


### [7] [The fate of hidden symmetries under magnetic Harrison transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19087)
*Shoulong Li,Fangkang Yu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes universal perturbative inheritance laws for conformal Killing tensors (CKTs) and massless scalar separability under magnetic Harrison transformations, showing that hidden symmetries survive perturbatively even when exact separability is lost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether and how hidden symmetries—such as those encoded in conformal Killing tensors (CKTs) and scalar field separability—are inherited by new solutions generated via solution-generating transformations, particularly magnetic Harrison transformations.

Method: Perturbative analysis directly at the level of magnetic Harrison transformations, applied to Carter-type and conformal Carter-type seed geometries (e.g., Kerr and Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson), examining CKT preservation and scalar field separability order-by-order.

Result: Universal perturbative inheritance laws for CKTs and massless scalar separability are derived; inheritance holds despite loss of exact Carter separability in magnetized Kerr and demagnetized Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson; conformal Killing-Yano structures are obstructed at the same perturbative order.

Conclusion: A hierarchy exists in the robustness of hidden symmetries under perturbative solution generation: CKTs and scalar separability persist perturbatively, while higher-rank structures like conformal Killing-Yano tensors do not—providing a framework for analyzing symmetry inheritance across broader classes of transformations and theories.

Abstract: Solution-generating transformations construct new geometries from known seeds, raising the question of whether and to what extent the resulting solutions inherit the hidden symmetries underlying the seeds' integrability and separability. We have established universal perturbative inheritance laws for CKTs and massless scalar separability directly at the level of magnetic Harrison transformations, encompassing both magnetization of Carter-type seeds and demagnetization of conformal Carter-type seeds. In both magnetized Kerr and demagnetized Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson, this perturbative inheritance coexists with the loss of exact Carter-type separability, while seed-connected conformal Killing-Yano structures are already obstructed at the same perturbative order. These results reveal a hierarchy in the perturbative survival of hidden symmetries and provide a framework for investigating their inheritance in more general theoretical settings and under other solution-generating transformations.

</details>


### [8] [Two-scale system: compactified ambient metrics and Scri/Spi/Ti](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19377)
*Xavier Bekaert,Maxim Grigoriev,Mikhail Markov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper unifies several geometric constructions—Fefferman-Graham ambient metric, Brinkmann spaces, and ultrastatic vacuum spacetimes—via a novel two-scale conformal framework built from weight-one scalars satisfying almost-Einstein equations; it yields a new ambient construction for spatial (Spi) and temporal (Ti) infinity in Ricci-flat spacetimes, where Spi/Ti and null infinity emerge naturally as boundary face and corner of the ambient space, respectively, and provides a background for analyzing asymptotic field behavior at all infinities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate geometric constructions in general relativity and conformal geometry—particularly those related to asymptotic structure—and to provide a coherent ambient framework for studying spacetime infinities (Spi, Ti, and null infinity) beyond the standard Fefferman-Graham setting.

Method: Introduces a two-scale system consisting of two weight-one conformal scalars subject to almost-Einstein equations and regularity conditions; uses conformal compactification techniques and ambient space methodology to embed and relate Fefferman-Graham, Brinkmann, and ultrastatic geometries; constructs explicit ambient spaces for Spi and Ti in Ricci-flat settings and analyzes boundary geometry.

Result: A unified conformal-geometric framework encompassing Fefferman-Graham, Brinkmann, and ultrastatic vacuum spacetimes; a new ambient construction for Spi and Ti where they appear as a boundary face and the corner coincides with null infinity; a geometric background enabling simultaneous treatment of asymptotics for fields at spatial, temporal, and null infinity.

Conclusion: The two-scale conformal system provides a natural, flexible, and geometrically rich ambient framework that extends and unifies existing constructions, revealing deeper structural relationships among spacetime infinities and offering a powerful tool for asymptotic analysis in gravitational and gauge-theoretic contexts.

Abstract: We show that certain geometric constructions, including the Fefferman-Graham ambient metric, Brinkmann spaces, and ultrastatic vacuum spacetimes, admit natural conformal compactifications and can be unified within a single framework arising naturally from conformal geometry. The framework is based on a two-scale system comprising two weight-one conformal scalars satisfying almost-Einstein equations and certain regularity conditions. As a particular instance of this unifying framework, we obtain a new ambient construction for Spi and Ti, the extended spatial and temporal infinities of Ricci-flat spacetimes. A remarkable feature of this construction is that Spi/Ti and the null infinity of the corresponding spacetimes are naturally realised as a boundary face and the corner of the two-scale ambient space, respectively. We also demonstrate how the two-scale system can serve as a background ambient geometry for studying the asymptotic behaviour of (gauge) fields at spatial, temporal, and null infinity.

</details>


### [9] [A Stable Vector Bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19402)
*Nils A. Nilsson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a geodesically complete cosmological bounce in cubic generalised Proca theory using a free canonical scalar field, ensuring stability of perturbations and adherence to the null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a nonsingular, geodesically complete cosmological bounce within a well-behaved effective field theory—specifically, cubic generalised Proca theory—while maintaining classical stability and physical consistency (e.g., null energy condition compliance).

Method: The authors construct an exact background solution where the temporal component of the vector field evolves along its algebraic constraint; they analyze linear cosmological perturbations, examining kinetic coefficients, gradient stability, and asymptotic boundedness across infinite time.

Result: A stable, geodesically complete bounce is achieved: the null energy condition holds for matter; derivative mixing is nonzero at the bounce; kinetic coefficients remain positive for all finite times and nonzero comoving wavenumbers; short- and long-wavelength perturbations are stable and bounded.

Conclusion: Cubic generalised Proca theory admits a physically viable, stable cosmological bounce with canonical scalar matter, resolving singularity issues without violating fundamental stability or energy conditions.

Abstract: We construct a geodesically complete bounce in cubic generalised Proca theory with a free canonical scalar as the matter field. The temporal vector evolves along its algebraic constraint and supports the transition from contraction to expansion while matter obeys the null energy condition. This evolution permits nonzero derivative mixing at the bounce. The kinetic coefficients are positive at every finite time for all nonzero comoving wavenumbers, and short-wavelength perturbations are stable throughout the solution. Following the linear equations to both infinite-time limits also shows that local perturbations are bounded for all nonzero comoving wavenumbers.

</details>


### [10] [Black holes in Myrzakulov $F(R,T)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19500)
*Alexandre Landry,Ratbay Myrzakulov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies vacuum black-hole solutions in metric-affine Myrzakulov F(R,T) gravity, where T is a quadratic torsion invariant—not the energy-momentum trace—using a polynomial model F(R,T) = αR + βT + γT³. It derives field equations by independent variation of metric and connection, then imposes metric compatibility and totally antisymmetric (axial) torsion. A special branch with nonzero axial torsion yields Einstein-like equations with an effective cosmological constant Λ_* = γT_*³/α. The authors prove local existence and construct smooth torsionful Schwarzschild–(A)dS solutions across a Killing horizon; they classify Ricci-symmetric connections under spherical symmetry, distinguish curvature invariants between connection families sharing same metric and T, verify identities via coordinate-level curvature checks, and analyze degeneracy in the axial quadratic form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore viable vacuum black-hole geometries in metric-affine extensions of gravity where torsion contributes directly to dynamics via a quadratic torsion invariant T, distinct from standard F(R,T) models coupling to matter's energy-momentum trace; specifically, to assess whether non-Riemannian structures (e.g., axial torsion) can yield physically consistent, horizon-regular black holes in Myrzakulov-type F(R,T) gravity.

Method: Variational principle applied independently to metric g_μν and full affine connection Γ^λ_μν in metric-affine MG-I framework; subsequent imposition of metric compatibility (∇g = 0) and totally antisymmetric (axial) torsion; analysis restricted to spherically symmetric, static, vacuum configurations; classification of Ricci-symmetric connections via differential condition (fV − U)' = 0; explicit construction of torsionful Schwarzschild–(A)dS metrics; verification of field equations and identities using component-wise curvature contractions and unrestricted variational consistency checks; identification of null directions in the axial quadratic form.

Result: 1) A special torsion branch α + β + 3γT_*² = 0 admits exact vacuum solutions satisfying Einstein equations with effective Λ_* = γT_*³/α; 2) Local existence of smooth, horizon-regular torsionful Schwarzschild–(A)dS black holes is established; 3) Two distinct families of Ricci-symmetric connections—constant W = fV − U vs. constant U—are identified: same metric and T, but generally inequivalent affine curvature invariants; 4) All key identities and field equations are confirmed via direct component computations and curvature contractions; 5) Degeneracy in the axial quadratic form is characterized via its null directions, distinguished from full metric-affine stability.

Conclusion: Metric-affine Myrzakulov F(R,T) gravity with axial torsion supports locally well-behaved black-hole solutions that reduce to Einstein–(A)dS geometry on a special torsion branch. While Ricci-symmetry does not uniquely fix the connection—even under spherical symmetry—curvature distinctions remain detectable at the affine level. Results are strictly local: no claims about global structure, physical uniqueness of torsion profiles, or dynamical stability are made.

Abstract: We investigate vacuum black-hole solutions in metric-affine Myrzakulov $F(R,T)$ gravity (MG-I), where $R$ is the affine Ricci scalar and $T$ is a quadratic torsion invariant, not the trace of the matter energy-momentum tensor. For the polynomial model $F(R,T)=\alpha R+\beta T+\gamma T^3$, with $\alpha\neq0$, we first vary the metric and all components of the independent affine connection, and only then specialize to metric-compatible geometry with totally antisymmetric torsion. Nonzero axial torsion admits an exceptional branch $\alpha+\beta+3\gamma T_*^2=0$, on which the metric satisfies the Einstein equations with effective cosmological parameter $\Lambda_*=\gamma T_*^3/\alpha$. We establish a local existence result and construct torsionful Schwarzschild-(anti-)de Sitter representatives that are smooth through a selected future Killing horizon. Within the spherically symmetric axial ansatz, we characterize Ricci-symmetric affine connections by the condition $(fV-U)'=0$ and compare a constant-$W=fV-U$ family with a constant-$U$ family. Although these connections share the same metric and torsion scalar, their affine curvature invariants generally differ. Independent coordinate-level curvature contractions and component tests of the unrestricted metric and connection variations corroborate the identities used in the construction; these checks complement the analytic variational derivation. Finally, we identify null directions of the restricted axial quadratic form and distinguish this degeneracy from a complete metric-affine stability analysis. Our conclusions are local and do not establish global extendibility, physical inequivalence of all torsion profiles, or dynamical stability.

</details>


### [11] [Quasinormal Modes and Environmental Trapping around Charged Letelier Black Holes with Perfect-Fluid Dark Matter and Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19935)
*Semra Gurtas Dogan,Abdullah Guvendi,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quasinormal modes (QNMs) of massless scalar and electromagnetic fields on charged Letelier black holes surrounded by logarithmic perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) and quintessence, for both asymptotically flat (Λ=0) and AdS (Λ<0) spacetimes; it validates WKB and Chebyshev pseudospectral methods, analyzes parameter dependencies (charge, β, quintessence, string cloud), confirms absence of growing test-field modes, and identifies long-lived trapped modes in AdS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate stability and spectral properties of charged Letelier black holes immersed in physically motivated dark matter and dark energy backgrounds—specifically logarithmic PFDM and quintessence—and to extend QNM analysis to asymptotically AdS cases where standard WKB fails due to divergent potentials.

Method: For Λ=0: sixth-order WKB with Padé resummation and time-domain integration (for validation); eikonal approximation via null geodesics (Lyapunov exponent and angular frequency). For Λ<0: Chebyshev pseudospectral method in ingoing Eddington–Finkelstein coordinates, imposing horizon regularity and Dirichlet boundary condition at AdS infinity; cross-validated against time-domain evolutions and Schwarzschild-AdS benchmarks.

Result: In Λ=0: WKB and time-domain agree within 0.06% for fundamental modes; stronger string cloud/quintessence reduce both oscillation and damping; larger charge or β>0 (negative PFDM energy density) increase both; eikonal approximation matches ℓ=2 WKB frequencies at ~1% level. In Λ<0: no WKB applicability due to potential divergence; Chebyshev method yields long-lived modes (damping rates spanning five orders of magnitude), with damping inversely correlated with barrier leakage (ratio of mode frequency to barrier top frequency); no unstable (growing) modes found in scalar/Maxwell test fields.

Conclusion: Charged Letelier black holes in logarithmic PFDM + quintessence are spectrally stable at the linear test-field level for all explored parameters; however, full nonlinear stability remains open. The AdS spectrum exhibits rich structure due to boundary reflection and photon sphere trapping, and the methodology provides a robust framework for QNMs in non-asymptotically-flat, multi-component dark sector spacetimes.

Abstract: We compute the massless scalar quasinormal spectra of charged Letelier black holes surrounded by logarithmic perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) and quintessence, for $\Lambda=0$ and for asymptotically anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetimes, together with the test Maxwell spectra for $\Lambda=0$. For $\Lambda=0$, sixth order WKB values with Padé resummation agree with independent time domain evolutions to within $0.06\%$ for the fundamental modes. Stronger string cloud and quintessence lower both the oscillation frequencies and the damping rates, while a larger charge or a larger positive PFDM parameter $\beta$ raises both. In our convention $\beta>0$ means negative PFDM energy density; negative $\beta$ gives a positive density and reverses the shift. The angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent of the unstable circular null orbit reproduce the eikonal trends and approximate the $\ell=2$ WKB frequencies at the percent level. For $\Lambda<0$ the scalar potential diverges at the AdS boundary and the single barrier WKB formula does not apply. We therefore solve the eigenvalue problem with a Chebyshev pseudospectral method in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates, with regularity at the horizon and a Dirichlet condition at the boundary, and check the modes against time domain evolutions and Schwarzschild-AdS benchmarks. The AdS spectrum contains long-lived modes trapped between the photon sphere barrier and the reflecting boundary, with damping rates spanning about five orders of magnitude. The damping correlates with the ratio of the oscillation frequency to the barrier top frequency, consistent with leakage through the barrier. No growing modes are found in the test field sectors and parameter ranges studied, which does not establish the stability of the fully coupled system.

</details>


### [12] [Casimir-like wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20168)
*Mauricio Cataldo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the most general spherically symmetric solutions of Einstein's equations for matter with energy density ρ ∝ 1/r⁴ and equation of state p_r = ωρ, identifying black holes, naked singularities, and traversable wormholes; it shows only Casimir-type (ω > 0) and phantom (ω < −1) matter support traversable wormholes, demonstrates finite exotic matter content scaling linearly with throat radius, and establishes human-traversability for throat radii ≳ 10⁸ m.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify all spherically symmetric spacetimes supported by inverse-quartic energy density matter, determine which equations of state permit traversable wormholes, assess their physical viability (exotic matter budget, traversability), and clarify the relationship between geometrically Casimir-like stress-energy and the quantum Casimir effect.

Method: Analytical integration of Einstein’s field equations for static, spherically symmetric metrics sourced by anisotropic perfect fluid with ρ ∝ r⁻⁴ and radial equation of state p_r = ωρ; application of flare-out condition, null/weak energy conditions, geodesic analysis (acceleration, tidal forces, proper transit time), embedding diagram construction, and comparison with known Casimir stress-energy structure.

Result: Exact general solution parametrized by ω and integration constants yielding black holes, naked singularities, and wormholes; necessary and sufficient condition for traversability: ω > 0 (Casimir-type) or ω < −1 (phantom); finite total exotic matter ∝ throat radius r₀; human-traversability satisfied for r₀ ≳ 10⁸ m under radial free fall; emergence of transverse pressure p_t → −ρ for ω = 3 without imposing full Casimir tensor a priori; recovery of Garattini’s Planck-scale wormhole upon fixing amplitude to quantum Casimir scale.

Conclusion: Traversable wormholes with ρ ∝ r⁻⁴ are viable classical geometries supported exclusively by Casimir-type or phantom matter; their exotic matter requirement is finite and scalable, and macroscopic (non-Planckian) traversable configurations satisfying all known physical traversability constraints exist — indicating that such wormholes are not merely quantum curiosities but potentially realizable classical spacetime structures.

Abstract: We investigate spherically symmetric spacetimes sourced by matter with energy density $\rho \propto 1/r^{4}$ and equation of state $p_r = \omega \rho$. We derive the most general solution of Einstein's equations for this class of sources, obtaining black holes, naked singularities and traversable wormholes, parametrized by the integration constants and $\omega$. We show that only two classes of matter can support traversable wormholes: Casimir-type matter with $\omega > 0$ and phantom matter with $\omega < -1$, while quintessence and conventional dark energy are excluded by the flare-out condition. For the Casimir value $\omega = 3$, the transverse pressure approaches $p_t \to -\rho$ near the central singularity, so the full algebraic structure of the Casimir stress-energy tensor, $\rho \propto -1/r^{4}$, $p_r = 3\rho$, $p_t = -\rho$, is not imposed but emerges from Einstein's equations. Treating the amplitude of the energy density as a free classical parameter, we obtain wormholes with throat radii from microscopic to astrophysical scales. These Casimir-like configurations share the functional form of the Casimir tensor but are not generated by the physical Casimir effect; Garattini's Planck-scale Casimir wormhole is recovered when the quantum Casimir energy scale is imposed. The total amount of exotic matter is finite for arbitrary $\omega$ and scales linearly with the throat radius. Analyzing the traveler's acceleration, tidal forces and transit times, we show that radial free fall satisfies all human traversability conditions for $r_0 \gtrsim 10^{8}$ m. Finally, embedding diagrams illustrate three geometric regimes depending on $\omega$.

</details>


### [13] [Boson Stars with Ayón--Beato--García nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20634)
*Long-Xing Huang,Yong-Qiang Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates spherically symmetric boson star solutions in Einstein gravity coupled to Ayón--Beato--García nonlinear electrodynamics and a massive complex scalar field; it identifies charge-dependent solution regimes—including gravitational blueshift, negative radial mass regions, and 'frozen' configurations resembling extremal black holes—with distinct geodesic behavior near a critical radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nonlinear electrodynamics modifies boson star structure and stability, particularly whether extremal black-hole-like features can emerge without horizons or singularities in self-gravitating scalar–electromagnetic systems.

Method: Numerical integration of the coupled Einstein–scalar–nonlinear-electrodynamics field equations under spherical symmetry; analysis of metric functions, ADM mass, radial mass function, scalar-field frequency dependence, and timelike geodesics.

Result: Boson star solutions exist only below a critical electric charge; low-charge configurations show gravitational blueshift and locally negative radial mass; high-charge 'frozen' solutions arise as ω → 0, exhibiting near-vanishing g^rr and -g_tt at r_ch, mimicking extremal black holes asymptotically, with strongly deflected timelike geodesics and increased coordinate traversal time.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics enables novel boson star phases—especially frozen configurations—that qualitatively resemble extremal black holes externally while remaining horizonless and regular, suggesting a smooth geometric transition between compact bosonic objects and black-hole analogs.

Abstract: In this paper, we study spherically symmetric boson star solutions in Einstein gravity minimally coupled to Ayón--Beato--García nonlinear electrodynamics and a massive complex scalar field. We find that these solutions exist only when the electric charge $q$ is below a critical value. Within this range, solutions with relatively small charges can exhibit a gravitational blueshift, and the radial mass function becomes negative in some regions while the ADM mass remains positive. For larger charges, frozen boson-star solutions emerge as the scalar-field frequency $\omega$ approaches zero. In these configurations, the metric function $1/g_{rr}$ and $-g_{tt}$ become very small near a special position $r_{\mathrm{ch}}$, and the spacetime resembles an extremal black hole from the perspective of an observer at infinity. Moreover, timelike trajectories in these frozen boson stars are strongly deflected near $r_{\mathrm{ch}}$, and particles require a longer coordinate time to traverse the same radial distance than they do in ordinary boson stars.

</details>


### [14] [Maximum entropy reconstruction of the black hole greybody factors from its Korteweg-de Vries integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21043)
*Noah Alberca,Michele Lenzi,Carlos F. Sopuerta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a maximum entropy method to reconstruct black hole greybody factors from KdV integrals—conserved quantities derived from the KdV hierarchy of BH perturbation equations—achieving high accuracy against exact (Pöschl-Teller) and semi-analytic (4th-order WKB) benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, positivity-preserving reconstruction method for BH greybody factors using the recently discovered infinite set of KdV integrals—bypassing reliance on approximate or numerical solutions where exact ones are unavailable.

Method: Applies the maximum entropy principle to solve the moment problem posed by KdV integrals: given a finite set of moments (integrals), it reconstructs the transmission probability as the unique positive distribution maximizing Shannon entropy subject to moment constraints; validated on Pöschl-Teller (exact) and Regge-Wheeler (Schwarzschild axial) potentials.

Result: The method accurately reconstructs greybody factors for the Regge-Wheeler potential, matching 4th-order WKB across multiple multipoles; demonstrates that only a modest number of KdV integrals suffices for high-fidelity recovery.

Conclusion: The maximum entropy method provides a principled, positivity-guaranteed framework for greybody factor reconstruction from KdV integrals—establishing a new analytic tool for BH perturbation theory with implications for scattering, quasinormal modes, and information-theoretic approaches to BH physics.

Abstract: The dynamics of perturbed non-rotating black holes (BHs) can be described by master wave equations with a potential barrier. Recent work has shown that these equations possess an infinite number of symmetries generated by the Korteweg-de Vries (KdV) hierarchy, leading to conserved quantities (KdV integrals) that fully determine the BH greybody factors via a moment problem. In this paper, we introduce and validate a new method to solve this moment problem using the maximum entropy principle. The method reconstructs, at each order of truncation, the least-biased transmission probability compatible with a finite set of KdV integrals, guaranteeing positivity by construction. We first validate the technique against the exactly solvable Pöschl-Teller potential. We then apply it to the Regge-Wheeler potential governing axial perturbations of a Schwarzschild BH, where no exact solution is available; the resulting reconstruction agrees closely with the fourth-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation across several multipoles. This is the first application of the maximum entropy method to the reconstruction of BH greybody factors, demonstrating that a modest number of KdV integrals is sufficient to recover them accurately.

</details>


### [15] [Detectability of Eccentric Intermediate Mass-Ratio Inspiral in the Decihertz Band](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21323)
*Devesh Giri,Sajal Mukherjee,Apratim Ganguly,Sanjit Mitra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the impact of orbital eccentricity on gravitational-wave detection and parameter estimation for intermediate mass-ratio inspirals (IMRIs) in the decihertz band (~0.01–1 Hz), showing that circular waveform templates fail significantly even at modest eccentricities (e₀ ≳ 0.03), biasing mass measurements and missing detectable eccentricity down to e₀ ~ 10⁻⁴; it concludes that eccentric waveform models are essential for DECIGO, LGWA, and IndIGO-D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether standard circular-template-based search and parameter-estimation pipelines are sufficient for detecting and characterizing IMRIs in the decihertz GW band—where dynamically formed binaries are expected to retain measurable eccentricity—and to quantify the resulting mismatches, biases, and resolvability limits.

Method: Using perturbative black-hole waveforms from FastEMRIWaveforms for a representative IMRI (M=10⁴ M⊙, q=100, 10-hr observation), the authors compute fitting factors between eccentric signals and circular templates, maximizing over total mass and mass ratio via differential evolution; they evaluate mismatch across B-DECIGO, LGWA-Si, and IndIGO-D noise curves plus flat PSD, and apply a signal-to-noise-dependent distinguishability criterion to determine minimum resolvable eccentricity.

Result: Mismatch exceeds the 3% threshold at e₀ ≈ 0.03; best-fitting circular templates bias total mass and mass ratio by several percent at e₀ = 0.1; eccentricities as low as e₀ ≈ 5×10⁻⁴ (B-DECIGO, IndIGO-D) and 5×10⁻³ (LGWA-Si) are resolvable; mismatch arises from excitation of radial Teukolsky harmonics (introducing unmodeled sidebands) and slower eccentric orbital decay at fixed semi-latus rectum.

Conclusion: Circular templates are inadequate for robust detection and unbiased parameter estimation of eccentric IMRIs in the decihertz band; future observatories (DECIGO, LGWA, IndIGO-D) require eccentric waveform models and dedicated template banks.

Abstract: The proposed decihertz gravitational-wave (GW) band $(\sim 10^{-2}-1\,\Hz)$ will observe the late inspiral of intermediate mass-ratio inspirals (IMRIs), binaries with mass ratios $10^2 \lesssim q \lesssim 10^4$ that assemble dynamically in dense stellar environments and are expected to retain measurable eccentricity. Search and parameter-estimation pipelines are predominantly built around circular templates, and we test this assumption. Using black-hole perturbation theory waveforms from \textsc{FastEMRIWaveforms} for a representative IMRI ($M=10^4\,\modot$, $q=100$, observed for its final $10\,\hr$), we compute fitting factors between eccentric signals and circular templates, maximizing over the template's total mass and mass ratio with a differential-evolution search, for the B-DECIGO, LGWA-Si, and IndIGO-D noise curves and a flat PSD. The mismatch grows with initial eccentricity $e_0$ and crosses the standard $3\%$ threshold at $e_0\simeq3\times10^{-2}$, well within the range allowed by the waveform model and expected from dynamical formation. The best-fitting circular templates also bias the total mass and mass ratio by several percent at $e_0=0.1$. Combining the mismatch with the signal-to-noise-dependent distinguishability criterion, we find that for a source at $1\,\mathrm{Gpc}$, eccentricities as small as $e_0\simeq5\times10^{-4}$ (B-DECIGO and IndIGO-D) and $5\times10^{-3}$ (LGWA-Si) are resolvable, one to two orders of magnitude below where detection efficiency degrades. The loss of match arises from the excitation of radial Teukolsky harmonics, which spread power into sidebands that circular templates cannot represent, and from the slower orbital decay of eccentric binaries at fixed initial semi-latus rectum. Robust detection and parameter estimation of IMRIs with DECIGO, LGWA, and IndIGO-D will require eccentric waveform models and template banks.

</details>


### [16] [Geometry of squeezed states. The phase-space metric, relative vacua and cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21426)
*Alejandro Corichi,Jerónimo Cortez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets quantum geometry by elevating the phase-space metric g—induced by vacuum choice in linear quantization—to a primary role, showing it encodes quantum fluctuations, defines coherent and squeezed states geometrically, governs quadratic evolution of squeezing parameters ri, and determines unitary implementability of field vacua via metric distance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically clarify the roles of symplectic and metric structures in quantum theory—particularly how vacuum selection induces a natural metric g on classical phase space, and how this metric governs quantum features (fluctuations, squeezing, unitary equivalence) more fundamentally than conventional state-space geometry.

Method: Analytical geometric quantization framework: treats compatible complex structures on phase space as equivalent to metrics g; computes quantum fluctuations as g-norms of Hamiltonian vector fields; derives squeezed-state characterization via eigenvalues of g0^{-1}g; analyzes quadratic Hamiltonian evolution via Lie derivative of g; applies metric distance in space of vacua to assess unitary implementability for QFTs, including explicit de Sitter examples.

Result: 1) Fluctuations of linear observables equal g-norms of their Hamiltonian vector fields; 2) The quantum metric on state space equals g/2ℏ along coherent states; 3) Squeezing is intrinsically a relative property between two metrics (vacua), with squeezing parameters ri given by log-eigenvalues of g0^{-1}g; 4) Quadratic evolution changes ri only via the g0-self-adjoint part of the generator, at an exact rate; 5) For QFT, unitary implementability of a vacuum relative to reference is equivalent to finite geodesic distance in the space of metrics; 6) In de Sitter space, Bunch–Davies vacuum has sinh rk = 1/(2|kη|) relative to standard reference (non-unitary), but sinh rk = 1/(2k²η²) relative to radiation-era vacuum (unitary, faster squeezing).

Conclusion: The phase-space metric g induced by vacuum choice is not auxiliary but foundational: it fully encodes quantum uncertainties, coherent/squeezed state structure, dynamics of squeezing under quadratic Hamiltonians, and the criterion for unitary equivalence of vacua—providing a unified geometric language for quantum fluctuations across quantum mechanics and quantum field theory.

Abstract: Geometry enters quantum theory in two places. The space of quantum states carries a symplectic form, which generates unitary evolution, and a metric, which governs uncertainties and transition probabilities. The classical phase space acquires a metric of its own when a vacuum is chosen: quantizing a linear system amounts to choosing a compatible complex structure, equivalently a compatible metric $g$. We take this second metric as the primary object and show that it plays, on phase space, the role that the quantum metric plays on the space of states: fluctuations of linear observables are $g$-norms of Hamiltonian vector fields, and along coherent states $g/2\hbar$ \emph{is} the quantum metric. Squeezed states then have a simple description. A squeezed vacuum is the vacuum of another metric, and squeezing is a property of a pair of metrics, a relative vacuum, whose invariants are the eigenvalues $e^{\pm2r_i}$ of $L=g_0^{-1}g$. We work out the harmonic oscillator in detail, including why a rotated squeezed state no longer minimizes the product of the displayed uncertainties. Quadratic evolution deforms the metric, and only the part of the generator that is self-adjoint with respect to the reference changes the $r_i$, at a rate we give exactly. For fields, a vacuum is unitarily implementable relative to a reference exactly when it lies at finite distance from it in the space of vacua. For a massless field in de Sitter space we revisit the two canonical charts compared by Grain and Vennin: relative to the reference of the standard squeezing formalism the Bunch--Davies state has $\sinh r_k=1/(2|k\eta|)$, and relative to the vacuum that a radiation era beginning at time $\eta$ would select it has $\sinh r_k=1/(2k^2\eta^2)$. The second comparison squeezes twice as fast and is unitarily implementable; the first is not.

</details>


### [17] [On the Asymptotic Expansion of Massive Fields in Spherically Symmetric Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21557)
*Robin Fynn Diedrichs,Iris van Gemeren*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the standard Yukawa falloff assumption for massive real scalar fields sourced by a central object, showing it is generically invalid even for minimally coupled fields; it demonstrates that the asymptotically extracted coefficient is neither coordinate-invariant nor equal to the scalar charge in the point-particle action, and resolves the discrepancy via matched asymptotic expansions (perturbative) and numerical methods (non-perturbative).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the widespread but unjustified assumption that massive scalar fields sourced by a central object always exhibit Yukawa falloff at infinity, and to clarify the physical meaning and coordinate dependence of the asymptotically measured 'scalar charge'.

Method: Perturbative matched asymptotic expansions to resolve field behavior across scales, combined with non-perturbative numerical integration to probe full-field structure beyond linear or weak-field approximations.

Result: The Yukawa falloff is generically violated; the asymptotically extracted coefficient is coordinate-dependent and distinct from the true scalar charge defined invariantly via the point-particle action.

Conclusion: The standard identification of asymptotic field amplitude with scalar charge is invalid for massive scalar fields; a rigorous separation of near-zone and far-zone physics—via matched asymptotics—is required to correctly relate field observables to fundamental charges.

Abstract: The behavior of massive real scalar fields, when sourced by a central object, is usually assumed to follow a Yukawa falloff towards radial infinity. In this paper, we show that this is generically not the case, even for minimally coupled fields. Further, while the point-particle action provides a coordinate-invariant definition of the central object's scalar charge, we show that the coefficient one usually extracts at asymptotically large radii in the case of massive scalar fields is neither coordinate invariant nor identical to the scalar charge appearing in the point-particle action. We resolve this issue perturbatively by constructing matched asymptotic expansions and investigate the non-perturbative structure using numerical methods.

</details>


### [18] [A separation criterion for chiral systems and its application to Plebański--Demiański perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21603)
*Hideo Furugori,Daisuke Yoshida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a constructive, necessary-and-sufficient criterion for local separation of first-order chiral systems coupling two complex doublets, using Gram decomposition and differential transport; it applies the framework to Einstein–Maxwell perturbations on rotating, charged, accelerating Plebański–Demiański spacetimes, yielding explicit separated radial/angular equations and spectral properties of angular operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, constructive criterion for separability of first-order chiral systems—particularly relevant for gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations in general relativity—beyond standard second-order or scalar cases, and to extend separation theory to broader classes of spacetimes including those with acceleration, NUT charge, and cosmological constant.

Method: Combines Gram decomposition of coupling sources (with separable coordinate dependence) and a differential transport test; restricts to mode-independent complex matrix transformations with unit determinant; analyzes uniqueness of Hermitian coefficient matrices given fixed differential operator and admissible Gram decomposition; constructs explicit algebraic transformation for radiative perturbations on Plebański–Demiański backgrounds; derives angular endpoint indices and studies self-adjoint realizations.

Result: A necessary-and-sufficient local separability criterion for the specified chiral system class; explicit separated radial and angular ODEs for coupled Einstein–Maxwell perturbations on accelerating, rotating, charged, NUTted, Λ-bearing Plebański–Demiański spacetimes; uniqueness of Gram decomposition under genericity assumptions; classification of angular endpoint behavior (conical deficits/excesses for Bonnor–Misner identifications); discreteness and completeness of eigenbases for self-adjoint angular operators under regularity assumptions.

Conclusion: Local separation of such chiral systems is fully characterized by the interplay of source structure (via Gram decomposition) and geometric transport; the framework successfully generalizes separation theory to highly nontrivial spacetimes and yields well-posed spectral problems for angular operators, enabling future stability and quasinormal mode analyses.

Abstract: We give a constructive criterion for local separation of first-order chiral systems coupling two complex doublets. It combines a Gram decomposition of the coupling sources with separate coordinate dependence and a differential transport test. For recoverable, nondegenerate source families, the criterion is necessary and sufficient within the specified class of mode-independent complex matrix transformations with unit determinant. For a fixed differential operator and admissible Gram decomposition, we identify conditions under which the sources uniquely determine the Hermitian coefficient matrix required for separation. As an application, we construct an explicit algebraic transformation and derive separated radial and angular equations for coupled radiative Einstein--Maxwell perturbations of rotating, charged Plebański--Demiański backgrounds with acceleration, NUT charge, and a cosmological constant, on specified local patches. Its Gram decomposition is unique under additional generic assumptions. We also derive angular endpoint indices for Bonnor and Misner identifications with conical deficits or excesses. Under the stated regularity assumptions, each specified self-adjoint angular realization at fixed real mode parameters has a discrete spectrum and a complete orthonormal eigenbasis.

</details>


### [19] [Electric-magnetic asymmetry of exact dyonic Euler-Heisenberg black holes: duality-blind shadows and magnetically controlled horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18694)
*Vahideh Memari,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how Euler-Heisenberg corrections break electric-magnetic duality in dyonic black holes, finding that exterior observables (shadow, ISCO, Lyapunov exponent) are nearly insensitive to charge asymmetry—deviations are ~10⁻⁶—while interior structure (horizon existence, extremality) is strongly charge-asymmetric: magnetic charge always enforces a horizon; electric-only configurations can be horizonless; extremality is governed by a critical curve in the charge plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which physical properties of dyonic black holes retain memory of the electric-magnetic duality breaking induced by Euler-Heisenberg vacuum polarization.

Method: Analytical perturbative expansion of the exact Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg dyonic black hole solution in the coupling constant; asymptotic analysis of metric function symmetry under electric-magnetic charge exchange; computation of horizon conditions, extremality criterion via maxima of mass-function derivative; numerical validation via two independent integrations; comparison of predicted shadow with EHT image of Sgr A*.

Result: Metric function is even in charges to first order in coupling; first odd correction appears at second order and decays as r⁻¹⁰; observable deviations (shadow radius, Lyapunov exponent, ISCO) are ≤ a few ppm; magnetic charge guarantees central singularity and horizon, while sub-critical electric configurations may be horizonless; extremality condition defines a critical curve intersecting electric axis at origin and magnetic axis at a closed-form finite value.

Conclusion: Electric-magnetic duality breaking is exponentially suppressed in the black hole exterior but fundamentally alters causal structure and extremality in the core—revealing a sharp dichotomy between robustness of astrophysical observables and fragility of horizon physics under quantum vacuum corrections.

Abstract: Maxwell theory is invariant under electric-magnetic rotations, and the Reissner-Nordström black hole depends on its charges only through the sum of their squares. The Euler-Heisenberg vacuum-polarization terms break this symmetry, and an exact dyonic Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg black hole was found recently. We ask which properties remember the breaking. Outside the photon sphere, almost none do. The metric function is even under charge exchange through first order in the coupling, the first odd term appears at second order and falls off as the tenth inverse power of the radius, and the shifts it causes in the shadow, the Lyapunov exponent, and the innermost stable circular orbit stay at a few parts per million. The core behaves differently. Any magnetic charge drives the metric function to negative infinity at the center and so forces a horizon, while an electric configuration lighter than its field energy can be horizonless. Whether an extremal limit exists is decided by one function of the charges, the maximum of the derivative of the mass function, whose critical curve reaches the origin along the electric axis and meets the magnetic axis at a closed-form value. Two independent integrations confirm every number, and the shadow is tested against the Event Horizon Telescope image of Sagittarius A*.

</details>


### [20] [Transient Information in Moving-Mirror Quantum Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18695)
*M. Baran Ökten,Ash S. Turan,Michael R. R. Good,Kuan-Nan Lin,Pisin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how transient dynamical history is encoded in quantum field correlations after a moving mirror returns to a thermal trajectory, using Carlitz–Willey ray maps and Bogoliubov analysis; it identifies even–odd grading, bandwidth-dependent signed interference, and phase/correlation scaling laws distinguishing transient from asymptotic thermal behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite dynamical history—specifically, transient mirror motion—is preserved in quantum field observables despite convergence to a common asymptotic thermal state (same Hawking temperature).

Method: Analyzes two compact deformations of the Carlitz–Willey null-ray map related by control-parameter reversal; employs Bogoliubov transformation, peeling field decomposition, complex covariance analysis, and linear/nonlinear response theory for finite-bandwidth wave packets (right-moving, Rindler/Milne); evaluates correlation scaling, interference sign, and phase dependence on bandwidth and window shape.

Result: Exact even–odd grading of field and Bogoliubov coefficients induced by map pairing; transient sign retained in first-order covariance; monochromatic quadratic response is even under reversal; finite-bandwidth packets yield signed interference with thermal reference; squared correlations scale as bandwidth^4 (right-moving) or bandwidth^3 (Rindler/Milne); cross-covariance phase scales linearly with bandwidth, maximized by flat window; finite-amplitude corrections add linear bandwidth term to Rindler/Milne response.

Conclusion: Transient mirror dynamics leave detectable imprints in signed interference patterns and precise bandwidth scalings of quantum correlations—even when asymptotic thermality (Hawking temperature) is identical—enabling retrospective inference of finite history from field measurements in retarded time.

Abstract: We study how a finite dynamical history remains encoded in a quantum field after a moving mirror returns to a common thermal trajectory. Two compact deformations of the Carlitz--Willey ray map are related by reversal of a control parameter and share the same asymptotic null line and Hawking temperature. The pairing induces an exact even--odd grading of the peeling field and Bogoliubov transformation. The complex continuum covariance retains the transient sign at first order, while the leading monochromatic quadratic response is even under reversal. Finite-bandwidth wave packets convert this distinction into signed interference with the thermal reference covariance. In linear response, the signed squared correlation is quartic in bandwidth for the selected right-moving packets and cubic for matched Rindler/Milne packets. The relative cross-covariance phase has a linear bandwidth dependence, with its leading coefficient maximized in magnitude by the flat window among normalized real symmetric windows. Finite-amplitude corrections introduce an additional linear bandwidth term in the squared Rindler/Milne response. The distinguishing packets sample the transient history in retarded time, while both trajectories share the same asymptotic Hawking temperature.

</details>


### [21] [Optical Appearances and Multiple Lensed Rings of Self-Interacting Boson Stars with a Double-Well Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18700)
*Zefang Fan,Sen Guo,Xianggao Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the optical appearance and lensing features of self-interacting boson stars—horizonless black hole mimickers—as probes of dark matter microphysics, using a novel hybrid metric reconstruction method for high-precision ray-tracing; it finds non-monotonic shadow evolution tied to scalar self-interaction balance, and shows VLBI can constrain scalar field couplings geometrically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test strong-field gravity and probe dark matter microphysics via horizon-scale imaging, using self-interacting boson stars as physically motivated, horizonless black hole alternatives whose optical signatures encode underlying scalar field interactions.

Method: Developed a robust hybrid metric reconstruction: interior 6-parameter Padé approximant matched via globally $C^2$-continuous cubic spline to exact exterior Schwarzschild spacetime, enabling stable, high-precision ray-tracing despite scalar field's slowly decaying tail; performed systematic ray-tracing of thin-disk illumination to compute shadows, photon rings, and image morphology across scalar potential parameter space ($\Lambda, \Gamma$) and central amplitude $\phi_0$.

Result: Identified strict upper bound $\phi_0 \lesssim 0.317$ for regular static solutions; discovered non-monotonic shadow size evolution driven by macroscopic mass-amplitude turnover from microphysical competition between attractive and repulsive self-interactions; observed prominent nested higher-order photon rings and separated secondary images in most compact configurations; revealed diagonal gradient in shadow size across ($\Lambda, \Gamma$) parameter space.

Conclusion: Next-generation VLBI observations can not only distinguish horizonless objects from black holes but also geometrically constrain the microphysical self-coupling parameters ($\Lambda, \Gamma$) of ultralight scalar dark matter fields via quantitative shadow and photon ring measurements.

Abstract: As horizon-scale imaging opens a new era of strong-field gravity tests, self-interacting boson stars have emerged as compelling dark matter candidates and natural black hole mimickers. We systematically investigate the optical appearances and multiple lensed rings of such horizonless objects illuminated by a geometrically thin accretion disk. To overcome the severe numerical stiffness caused by the slowly decaying scalar tail, we introduce a robust hybrid metric reconstruction framework---seamlessly bridging an interior 6-parameter Padé approximant to the exact exterior Schwarzschild spacetime via a globally $C^2$-continuous cubic spline. This ensures high-precision ray-tracing across the entire domain. For the adopted double-well potential, we identify a strict upper bound ($\phi_0 \lesssim 0.317$) for the existence of regular static solutions. Crucially, we uncover a striking non-monotonic evolution in the shadow size, driven by a macroscopic mass-amplitude turnover that is fundamentally rooted in the microphysical competition between attractive and repulsive self-interactions. Within the most compact configurations, extreme gravitational lensing generates prominent nested higher-order photon rings and distinctly separated secondary images. Furthermore, exploring the scalar potential parameter space ($\Lambda, \Gamma$) reveals a distinct diagonal gradient in the shadow size. Our findings demonstrate that next-generation VLBI observations can move beyond merely identifying horizonless objects, offering a novel geometric avenue to directly probe the microphysical couplings of dark matter scalar fields.

Abstract: We derive the Lorentzian Fourier transform of the Gaussian-smeared light-cone contribution to the scalar propagator in Krein-space quantization in terms of a Meijer $G$-function. We characterize its distributional structure, recover the massive propagator by adding the Bessel-function tail, and derive an exact expression for the massless one-loop bubble kernel using a position-space prescription.

Abstract: We investigate an interpolated Maxwell--Born--Infeld theory bridging the Maxwell and Born--Infeld limits. We construct the electrically charged black hole, whose black hole solution interpolates monotonically between the classical Born--Infeld and Reissner--Nordström solutions; singularity can be relegated, independently of the weight parameters. For general interpolated theories with a truncation threshold, we analyze the existence and multiplicity of event horizons and classify the horizon configurations, and for general Born--Infeld-type nonlinear structures we prove a universal lower bound on the order of the Kretschmann divergence, thereby excluding regular electric black holes.

*Israr Ali Khan*

Main category: gr-qc

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how slow astrophysical evolution (e.g., accretion, mergers) drives chaotic dynamics in black hole spacetimes, moving beyond static integrable models.

Result: Chaos onset is governed by nonlinear resonance overlap (not single-parameter thresholds); ε increases chaos monotonically via resonance overlap; magnetic field B induces chaos near resonance (ω_r ≈ ω_d) but suppresses it at higher B via detuning; predicted magnetic critical value B_c ≈ 0.011 matches measured 0.012 without free parameters; quintessence lowers confining potential, enabling non-resonant escape and regularity breakdown.

Conclusion: The transition to chaos in slowly evolving black hole spacetimes is fundamentally resonant and symmetry-based, with quantitative predictions from approximate Noether symmetry analysis; quintessence provides a complementary, non-resonant destabilization mechanism.

Abstract: Astrophysical black holes are never strictly static: accretion, mergers, and gravitational radiation drive a slow evolution of their geometry. We model this evolution through a periodic time-conformal deformation, $\Omega(t)=1+\epsilon\sin(\omega_d t)$, of a Reissner--Nordström black hole surrounded by quintessence, and study the resulting onset of chaos for charged test particles. The deformation converts the integrable static spacetime into a periodically driven nonlinear Hamiltonian system, so that the transition from regular to chaotic motion can be related to the breakdown of an approximate Noether symmetry. The resulting two-degree-of-freedom Hamiltonian system is solved using an adaptive high-order Runge--Kutta integrator, while the dynamical behaviour is analysed through Poincaré sections, bifurcation diagrams, the largest Lyapunov exponent, and the Smaller Alignment Index (SALI). Our results show that the onset of chaos is controlled primarily by nonlinear resonance rather than by the magnitude of any individual parameter. Chaos grows with the conformal amplitude $\epsilon$ through resonance overlap, while the magnetic field $B$ acts non-monotonically: it produces chaos near the field strength at which the radial frequency matches the drive, and suppresses it at stronger fields by detuning. The small-divisor structure of the approximate Noether symmetry predicts the magnetic threshold without free parameters ($B_c\simeq0.011$ against the measured $0.012$), and reproduces the conformal threshold once a single coupling constant is fixed. In contrast, the quintessence parameter mainly facilitates particle escape by reducing the confining potential barrier, providing a distinct non-resonant route to the breakdown of regular motion.

</details>
