Abstract: We study the polarization properties of regular compact objects motivated by quantum gravity. These geometries include static spherically symmetric non-singular black holes which undergo a continuous transition to regular particle-like spacetimes in certain regions of the parametric space. Applying a phenomenological model of a magnetized fluid ring orbiting in the equatorial plane, we search for characteristic signatures in the direct polarized images which could distinguish them from black holes in general relativity. At low inclinations the regular compact objects lead to a very similar polarization pattern as the Schwarzschild black hole. At high inclinations we observe a characteristic signature for the particle-like geometries. A second direct image of the fluid ring is formed which is absent for black holes. It may possess with three orders of magnitude higher polarization intensity and a specific polarization direction making it observationally significant. We elucidate the physical mechanism for the formation of the second direct image and pinpoint the plasma configurations which lead to amplification of its polarized emission.

### [26] [Landauer erasure and horizon information in an expanding universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18879)

Abstract: We investigate the thermodynamic cost of information erasure in Friedmann--Robertson--Walker cosmology by combining Landauer's principle with the thermodynamics of the apparent horizon. Adopting the apparent-horizon temperature as an effective cosmological temperature, we find that for standard non-phantom expansion, the minimum heat required to erase one bit decreases while the entropy-equivalent information content of the horizon increases. We further compare the Landauer heat with the Misner--Sharp energy and show that, despite their simple thermodynamic relation, they have distinct physical meanings. These results provide a simple connection between information erasure, horizon thermodynamics, and gravitational energy in an expanding universe.

</details>


### [27] [Primordial Non-Gaussianity and Correlation-Function Perturbativity in mLQC-I: The Hybrid Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18899)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether mLQC-I yields observationally viable primordial perturbations—specifically, whether strong nonlinearities near the quantum bounce produce detectable non-Gaussianity while preserving perturbative control over the power spectrum.

Result: Two-point corrections are subdominant ($\Xi < 1$): $<6\times10^{-6}$ for bounce state, decreasing from $\sim9.9\times10^{-2}$ to $\sim2.3\times10^{-4}$ for remote de Sitter state across scales. Equilateral $f_{\rm NL}$ exhibits large pointwise amplitudes: RMS $\sim10^3$--$10^4$ (bounce) and larger (remote), organized by rapid $k_t$-oscillations ($\Delta k_t \simeq 5.7\times10^{-4} m_{\rm P}$), modulated by slow triangle-shape envelope. Conclusion: Primordial non-Gaussianity in mLQC-I can be large pointwise without violating perturbative consistency of the two-point function; however, rapid oscillations lead to strong phase cancellation in CMB observables, decoupling local $f_{\rm NL}$ magnitude from current observational bounds.

Abstract: We investigate primordial non-Gaussianity and the perturbative consistency of scalar perturbations in modified loop quantum cosmology I (mLQC-I) within the hybrid approach. We construct the cubic interaction Hamiltonian in the canonical representation of the quadratic theory and derive an exact finite-momentum in-in expression for the tree-level bispectrum. We consider two initial-state prescriptions: a Gaussian state specified at the bounce and the de Sitter vacuum specified at a remote contracting surface. We assess the retained nonlinear-conversion correction through $\Xi=|\Delta P_R/P_R^{(0)}|$. For the bounce state, a conservative, cancellation-independent, finite-window absolute-integrand estimate is below $6\times10^{-6}$ at all five sampled momenta. For the remote de Sitter state, the signed correction is dominated by cancellations and decreases from $\Xi\simeq9.9\times10^{-2}$ at one tenth of the bounce-curvature scale to $\Xi\simeq2.3\times10^{-4}$ at that scale. Although the retained two-point corrections remain below unity, the bispectrum can have large pointwise amplitudes: the bounce state yields an equilateral $f_{\rm NL}$ with root-mean-square amplitude over oscillation phase of order $10^3$--$10^4$, while the remote state can yield much larger values. The dominant rapid oscillation is organized approximately by $k_t=k_1+k_2+k_3$, with $\Delta k_t\simeq5.7\times10^{-4}m_{\rm P}$, while the triangle-shape envelope varies slowly. For the illustrative bounce-to-present mapping adopted here, CMB-projection diagnostics indicate substantial phase averaging. Thus, large pointwise primordial non-Gaussianity can coexist with a small retained correction to the two-point function.

</details>


### [28] [Drift of a Charged Particle in a Black Hole Magnetosphere: the Effect of Black Hole Spin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19098)
*Ye Shen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a covariant guiding-center approximation to study gravitational drift of charged particles in black hole magnetospheres, deriving a general drift formula that reduces to Newtonian gravitoelectromagnetic results in the weak-field limit and quantifying—via a newly defined drift rate—the significant influence of black hole spin on particle drift, especially in extreme regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of a fully covariant formulation of the guiding-center approximation and gravitational drift in strong-field relativistic regimes—particularly for spinning black holes—motivates this work.

Method: A covariant guiding-center expansion is performed in curved spacetime; gravitoelectromagnetism is used to bridge Newtonian intuition; drift equations are derived analytically in Kerr geometry; a dimensionless drift rate is introduced to quantify spin-induced drift magnitude.

Abstract: Sparse plasma immersed in a strong magnetic field is common in astrophysical scenarios, for which a collisionless description is more appropriate. The guiding-center approximation serves as an effective tool for describing the motion of charged particles in a strong magnetic field, revealing the drift, a sub-leading order motion that causes particles to deviate from the magnetic field lines. It is widely accepted that the charged particles drift under the induction of gravity in Newtonian framework. However, a covariant formulation of the guiding-center approximation, as well as the drift of a charged particle induced by strong gravitational field, has yet to be fully explored. As a preliminary study, we aim to analyze the drift of a single charged particle in a black hole magnetosphere via a covariant approach. Particularly, we focus on the effects of black hole spin. We first examine the drift caused by the rotation of a massive object in the Newtonian regime using the language of gravitoelectromagnetism. A covariant formula of drift is derived, which is confirmed to reduce to the Newtonian results in the weak gravitational field and low speed limit. The impact of BH spin on the drift is analyzed in detail in Kerr spacetime specifically, for which we define the drift rate whose value quantifies the significance of the drift. Our results indicate that the impact of BH spin on the drift of a charged particle in a BH magnetosphere can be comparable to that of other factors in some extreme cases.

</details>


### [29] [Chaotic motion of spinning particles in nonlocal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19191)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether spin-induced orbital chaos in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) can serve as a sensitive probe of modified gravity—specifically revised Deser–Woodard nonlocal gravity (NLG)—by examining how background spacetime deformation alters chaotic dynamics and associated gravitational waveforms.

Method: Uses conservative test-body approximation in NLG Schwarzschild-like spacetimes parametrized by deformation ζ; applies dynamical diagnostics—Poincaré sections, rotation curves, and fast Lyapunov indicators (FLIs)—to classify orbit regularity; computes approximate mass-quadrupole waveforms; quantifies waveform distinguishability via mismatch analysis between NLG and GR waveforms, including adjustments for initial radius to compensate classification transitions.

Result: Chaotic fraction decreases monotonically with ζ, vanishing at ζ = 0.1; FLI-classified chaotic orbits produce waveforms with higher spectral flatness than regular ones; adjusting initial radius to preserve orbit classification across NLG (ζ = 10⁻⁶) and GR backgrounds reduces waveform mismatch; yet chaotic NLG waveforms still yield larger minimum mismatches than regular ones, and sensitivity to initial conditions limits practical signal recovery.

Conclusion: While NLG-induced spacetime deformation systematically suppresses spin-induced chaos and correlates with discernible spectral features in waveforms, orbital chaos does not provide a clear observational advantage for distinguishing NLG from general relativity—due to both degeneracy in mismatch reduction and fragility under initial-condition perturbations.

Abstract: We study spin-induced chaos around a static, spherically symmetric black hole in revised Deser--Woodard nonlocal gravity (NLG), using the conservative test-body approximation relevant to extreme-mass-ratio inspirals. The deformation parameter $\zeta$ describes departures from Schwarzschild spacetime. Poincaré sections, rotation curves, and fast Lyapunov indicators (FLIs) reveal changes in resonant structures and a decreasing fraction of non-plunging trajectories classified as chaotic within the sampled family, from $37.5\%$ at $\zeta=0$ to zero at $\zeta=0.1$. The FLI classifications of selected trajectories are reflected in their approximate mass-quadrupole waveforms: neighboring chaotic and regular trajectories show a marked local spectral-flatness contrast, which weakens in backgrounds where both are classified as regular. To assess whether a transition from chaotic to regular motion caused by the background deformation helps distinguish NLG from GR, we compare NLG waveforms at $\zeta=10^{-6}$ with GR waveforms generated at different initial radii. For a trajectory whose classification changes between the backgrounds, this adjustment substantially reduces the mismatch. The selected NLG waveforms from chaotic orbits retain larger minimum mismatches than those from regular orbits, although sensitivity to initial conditions may also hinder signal recovery. These results connect background-dependent orbital dynamics with radiation spectra, but do not establish that chaos offers an advantage for identifying the background geometry.

</details>


### [30] [Numerical evidence for the validity of the inverse hoop conjecture for black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19240)
*Shahar Hod*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides evidence supporting the Inverse Hoop (IH) conjecture — that the irreducible mass of a black hole is bounded above by one-quarter of its smallest engulfing ring circumference divided by π — for non-stationary black-hole configurations, specifically verifying it for the recently constructed CGP solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the validity of the Inverse Hoop conjecture beyond stationary black holes to dynamical (non-stationary) regimes, where geometric wrinkling and horizon evolution challenge simple area bounds.

Method: Analytical verification of the IH inequality ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}} \leq {\cal C}_{\text{s}}/4\pi$ on the class of non-stationary black-hole spacetimes recently constructed by Croft, García-Moreno, and Pani (CGP).

Result: All CGP non-stationary black-hole solutions satisfy the IH relation, providing concrete support for the conjecture in dynamical settings.

Conclusion: The IH conjecture holds for the tested non-stationary black-hole configurations, suggesting its robustness beyond stationarity and reinforcing the idea that horizon geometry remains constrained even under time-dependent evolution.

Abstract: The inverse hoop (IH) conjecture, ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}}\leq {\cal C}_{\text{s}}/4\pi$, originally discussed in the context of stationary black-hole configurations, suggests that the surface area of a black hole cannot undergo unconstrained geometric wrinkling (here ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}}=\sqrt{{\cal A}/16\pi}$ is the irreducible mass associated with the horizon area ${\cal A}$ of the black hole and ${\cal C}_{\text{s}}$ is the circumference length of the smallest ring that can engulf the horizon of the black hole in every direction). In the present compact paper we provide evidence supporting the validity of the IH conjecture for non-stationary black-hole configurations. In particular, we show that all the non-stationary black-hole solutions recently constructed by Croft, García-Moreno, and Pani (CGP) [arXiv:2611.13266] satisfy the IH relation.

</details>


### [31] [Relativistic precession around rotating neutron stars. I. Slow rotation: Hartle--Thorne spacetimes, particle orbits and the QPOs of atoll and Z sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19502)

It quantifies how neutron star quadrupole moments—2–4× larger than Kerr BH counterparts—suppress nodal precession, constrain achievable nodal frequencies, and bias mass/spin/inertia estimates if Kerr approximations are used. RPM fits to atoll sources yield high masses (1.88–2.15 M⊙) and reveal EOS-dependent degeneracies; for 4U 1728−34, low-frequency QPOs prefer large moment of inertia (~2.5×10⁴⁵ g cm²).   <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how neutron star internal structure (encoded in EOS-dependent quadrupole moment) affects QPO modeling via the RPM—and to quantify biases introduced by assuming Kerr (black hole) spacetimes when interpreting QPOs from neutron stars.

Method: Solved Hartle–Thorne equations to second order in angular velocity for four nuclear EOSs (WFF1, G3, FSUGarnet, IOPB-I); computed exact exterior orbital and epicyclic frequencies (no spin/quadrupole expansion); modeled hot-spot emission on eccentric, tilted geodesics; fitted RPM predictions to observed QPO pairs in atoll (4U 1636−53, 4U 1728−34) and Z (GX 5−1, GX 340+0) sources.

Result: Neutron star quadrupole lowers nodal precession by up to 25% near ISCO and caps maximum nodal frequency; RPM fits yield high NS masses (1.88–2.15 M⊙); for 4U 1728−34, low-frequency QPO prefers I ≈ 2.5×10⁴⁵ g cm²; Kerr assumption underestimates I by 22%; Z-source HBOs cannot be 2×nodal frequency under physical EOS constraints without extreme spins (>730 Hz) or maximum-mass stars.

Conclusion: Neutron star quadrupole is essential for accurate RPM-based mass, spin, and moment-of-inertia inference from QPOs; neglecting it (e.g., using Kerr metric) introduces systematic biases; current QPO data favor relatively stiff EOSs and high-mass neutron stars, but challenge standard RPM interpretations for Z-source horizontal-branch oscillations.

Abstract: We study the relativistic precession model (RPM) of quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) in the spacetimes of slowly rotating neutron stars computed from the equation of state (EOS). For four EOSs (WFF1, G3, FSUGarnet and IOPB-I) we solve the Hartle--Thorne equations to second order in the angular velocity and compute the orbital and epicyclic frequencies of the exterior metric without expanding in the spin or the quadrupole. At $1.9\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ the spin-induced quadrupole is 2--4 times that of a black hole with the same spin; it lowers the nodal precession frequency by up to 25 per cent near the innermost stable circular orbit and limits the nodal frequency that any spin can produce. An eccentric, tilted geodesic around a $1.92\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ star spinning at 363~Hz precesses at the epicyclic rates, and the power spectrum of a hot spot on it peaks at the orbital, periastron and nodal precession frequencies. Applied to the atoll sources 4U 1636$-$53 and 4U 1728$-$34, the RPM gives masses of 2.02--$2.15\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ and 1.88--$1.92\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$. For 4U 1728$-$34 the low-frequency QPO favours stars with large moments of inertia, $I\simeq2.5\times10^{45}$~g\,cm$^{2}$. Without an EOS the QPOs give $M=1.920^{+0.014}_{-0.012}\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$, $j=0.162^{+0.023}_{-0.011}$ and $\bar Q=2.2^{+1.1}_{-0.8}$; a Kerr spacetime with the same nodal frequency would underestimate the moment of inertia by 22 per cent. For the Z sources GX 5$-$1 and GX 340+0 the horizontal-branch oscillations cannot be twice the nodal precession frequency for any of the four EOSs: the fits are driven to spins of 730--745~Hz and to the maximum mass.

</details>


### [32] [Scalar charge of neutron stars and accreting black holes in Starobinsky gravity: quasi-universal relations, the trace anomaly and gravitational-wave sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19510)
*Yashmitha Kumaran,Ilídio Lopes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a master formula for the Starobinsky scalaron charge of static, spherically symmetric objects—neutron stars and accreting black holes—showing it is determined by the stress–energy trace (ρ−3p), not vacuum geometry; for neutron stars, the charge is quasi-universal in compactness, correlates tightly with tidal deformability and moment of inertia, and remains stable under self-consistent Einstein-frame structure solving; black holes have zero charge in vacuum but acquire a finite, disc-edge–dominated charge when surrounded by an accretion disc; observational constraints from NICER, GW170817, and GW230529 are used to bound the scalaron mass (i.e., range) with projections for Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the Starobinsky scalaron 'charge'—a gravitational scalar degree of freedom sourced by the trace of the stress–energy tensor—in realistic astrophysical objects, and assess its observability and theoretical consistency in f(R) = R + αR² gravity, particularly distinguishing between vacuum and matter-influenced geometries.

Method: Derives a general master formula for the scalaron charge q from the trace ρ−3p in static, spherically symmetric spacetimes; applies it to realistic neutron star interiors using six equations of state and Eardley’s sensitivity formalism; extends to accreting black holes with thin discs via analytic integration; performs self-consistent Einstein-frame stellar structure integration; confronts results with NICER radius measurements, LIGO/Virgo tidal deformability (GW170817), and dipole radiation constraint from GW230529; computes Fisher forecast for scalaron mass m_φ using next-generation detectors.

Result: Neutron star scalaron charge is quasi-universal in compactness C = GM/(c²R); pressure subtraction yields q ∝ C^k with refined coefficient matching realistic EOS to 0.007; q correlates with Λ (tidal deformability) at ~0.1% and I (moment of inertia) at ~1%; self-consistent structure changes q by <4% at fixed C; vacuum black holes have q = 0; disc-accreting black holes have q = sinh(m_φ r_d)/(m_φ r_d), dominated by disc outer edge; light propagation is insensitive to q at leading order; GW230529 dipole limit implies m_φ ≳ 10⁻¹⁹ eV, strengthened to ~10⁻¹⁸ eV with ET/CE projections.

Conclusion: The Starobinsky scalaron charge is an astrophysically measurable, matter-determined quantity—not a geometric hair—with tight empirical and theoretical links to neutron star structure and binary dynamics; its absence in vacuum black holes reaffirms no-hair theorems in this sector, while its presence in accreting systems opens novel tests of f(R) gravity with multi-messenger observations and next-generation GW detectors.

Abstract: The Starobinsky scalaron is sourced by the trace of the stress--energy, so its exterior charge is set by the matter around an object and not by the object's vacuum geometry. We derive a master formula for that charge for any static, spherically symmetric source, evaluate it first for neutron stars, where the interior is known, and then for accreting black holes, where it is not. For a star the pressure subtracts: on the interior metric with the Eardley sensitivity, and the charge is a quasi-universal function of the compactness. Six realistic equations of state confirm the relation but lie above the coefficient calibrated on polytropes; the refit reproduces their mean to 0.007. The trace $\rho-3p$ changes sign in the cores of massive stars, and the charge also tracks the tidal deformability and the moment of inertia to a few parts in 1000 and 100. Solving the Einstein-frame structure equations self-consistently moves a star along this relation and leaves its charge at fixed compactness within 4 per cent of the test-field value. We compare the charge with NICER radii and the GW170817 tidal deformability, and use the published massless dipole constraint from GW230529 to calibrate a Fisher estimate of the sensitivity of a similar binary to a finite scalaron range, projected to the Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer; the range is a free parameter, and the laboratory bound on pure $R+\alpha R^2$ is confronted explicitly. A vacuum black hole has q=0; a hole with a disc has $q=\sinh(m_\phi r_d)/(m_\phi r_d)$, controlled by the disc's outer edge, which makes it the counterpart in which the same integral is dominated by matter far from the centre; light is blind to the charge at leading order, and the correction beyond it is negligible for any self-consistent disc.

</details>


### [33] [Dymnikova black string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19682)
*L. G. Barbosa,L. C. N. Santos,C. C. Barros Jr*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a new static black string solution in Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant, supported by a Dymnikova-type anisotropic fluid; it features regular curvature invariants at the origin, satisfies energy conditions in certain regimes, and exhibits thermodynamic properties (e.g., Hawking temperature, heat capacity) sensitive to the Dymnikova scale.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a regular black string solution with physically well-behaved matter (Dymnikova-type anisotropic fluid) in anti-de Sitter spacetime, avoiding curvature singularities while generalizing known solutions like the Lemos black string.

Method: Solving Einstein's equations with negative cosmological constant and a Dymnikova-inspired anisotropic stress-energy tensor; analyzing energy conditions, horizon structure, curvature invariants (Ricci and Kretschmann scalars), and thermodynamic quantities (Hawking temperature, heat capacity).

Conclusion: The Dymnikova-type anisotropic matter yields a singularity-free black string in AdS space, with viable energy conditions and scale-dependent thermodynamics—suggesting a physically plausible regular black string model beyond vacuum or simple fluid sources.

Abstract: We obtain a new static black string solution of Einstein's equations with a negative cosmological constant sourced by a Dymnikova-type anisotropic matter distribution. At large radial distances, the geometry approaches the Lemos black string, while the behavior near the central region is characterized by an effective curvature parameter. We examine the weak and strong energy conditions, the horizon structure, and the curvature invariants. The Ricci and Kretschmann scalars remain finite at the origin. We also investigate the Hawking temperature and heat capacity, with particular attention to the dependence of the thermodynamic behavior on the Dymnikova scale.

</details>


### [34] [Multi-throat generalized Ellis--Bronnikov wormholes with asymptotic string-cloud behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19808)
*M.S. Cunha,C.R. Muniz,V.H.U. Borralho,G. Alencar*

The stress tensor satisfies pointwise energy conditions in finite belly-centered regions but violates the radial null energy condition (NEC) at throats and in asymptotic tails. Integrated NEC violation (via Nandi–Zhang–Kumar volume integral) is quadratic in deformation amplitude and distinct from bifurcation curves. Light-ring structure is fully mapped: unstable light rings reside at throats, stable ones at bellies; double-throat configurations support internally trapped null geodesics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and systematically classify regular, asymptotically conical, traversable wormhole geometries with tunable throat multiplicity, and to identify physically viable matter sources satisfying realistic field-theoretic constraints—including resolution of branch ambiguities in nonlinear electrodynamics and compatibility with energy conditions in localized regions.

Method: Analytic construction of ultrastatic wormhole metrics via local deformation of generalized Ellis–Bronnikov areal-radius profiles; derivation of dimensionless bifurcation curves for throat number transitions; reconstruction of a composite matter source using canonical scalar, shift-symmetric phantom scalar, and magnetically charged nonlinear electromagnetic field coupled to the canonical scalar; analysis of energy conditions (pointwise and integrated NEC via Nandi–Zhang–Kumar covariant volume integral); classification of light-ring stability via effective potential and radial geodesic analysis.

Result: A unified parameter-space description yielding 1–4 throats; regular, asymptotically conical geometries; explicit source reconstruction resolving NED branch obstruction; $r^{-2}$-scaling asymptotic stress approaching string-cloud equation of state; pointwise energy condition satisfaction in finite belly regions but NEC violation at throats and integrable asymptotic tails; quadratic dependence of integrated NEC violation on deformation amplitude; distinct bifurcation vs. zero-integral loci; systematic light-ring pattern: unstable at throats, stable at bellies, with internal trapping in double-throat case.

Conclusion: Throat multiplicity, source physics, integrated energy-condition content, and background-metric circular-orbit structure are coherently governed by a small set of deformation parameters; the model demonstrates that multi-throat wormholes can arise from well-defined, non-exotic (in localized regions) matter sectors when appropriately coupled, advancing the viability of topologically nontrivial spacetimes in classical GR.

Abstract: We study an ultrastatic, spherically symmetric family of traversable wormholes obtained by locally deforming a generalized Ellis--Bronnikov areal-radius profile in a spacetime with an asymptotic angular deficit. Depending on the deformation parameters, the same analytic family contains one, two, three, or four throats. The corresponding bifurcation curves are obtained in dimensionless form, and the resulting geometries are regular and asymptotically conical. We also reconstruct a source in General Relativity using a canonical scalar, a shift-symmetric phantom scalar, and a magnetic nonlinear electromagnetic field coupled to the canonical scalar. In the reference multi-throat geometry, the coupling removes a branch obstruction of an uncoupled single-valued nonlinear electrodynamics (NED); the same obstruction occurs whenever equal values of the magnetic invariant require different values of $\mathcal G_F$. For $\mathcal G(F)=F+\lambda\sqrt F$, the NED sector carries the leading asymptotic stress, which scales as $r^{-2}$ and approaches the string-cloud equation of state. The total stress tensor satisfies pointwise energy conditions in finite belly-centered regions of representative two- and four-throat solutions, while the throats violate the radial null energy condition (NEC) and the violation extends into integrable asymptotic tails. The Nandi--Zhang--Kumar covariant volume integral of $\rho+p_r$ is quadratic in the deformation amplitude; its zero is distinct from the bifurcation curves. Away from saddle-node boundaries, each throat supports a radially unstable background-metric light ring, and each intermediate belly supports a radially stable one, with an internally trapped null family in the double-throat case. These results place the topology, source reconstruction, integrated NEC content, and background-metric circular-orbit structure in a common parameter-space description.

</details>


### [35] [A hybrid log-likelihood-ratio statistic for single-detector SPIIR gravitational-wave searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19918)
*SPIIR Collaboration,Weichangfeng Guo,Sunil Choudhary,Victor Oloworaran,Linqing Wen,Jayden Doan,Qingyuan Liang,Tim Davies,Luke Davis,Irina Dvorkin,Apratim Ganguly,Shang-Jie Jin,JeongCho Kim,Yu-Chiung Lin,Ngai-Chun Lung,Alistair McLeod,Khun Sang Phukon,Andreas Wicenec*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new single-detector SPIIR pipeline for BNS and NSBH merger detection during LIGO-Virgo O3/O4a runs, featuring a hybrid log-likelihood ratio that jointly models glitches and signal SNR, calibrated per source category and trigger-rate class; it achieves significantly improved BNS sensitive distances over GstLAL (24–38% gain), competitive NSBH performance, and successfully recovers known events with robust false alarm rate (FAR) estimation using in-detector background extrapolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve detection sensitivity and reliability during periods when only one gravitational-wave detector is operational—constituting >13% of O3 and nearly 30% of O4a—by developing a dedicated, calibrated single-detector search pipeline that better handles non-Gaussian noise (glitches) while maintaining accurate FAR estimation.

Method: Developed a single-detector variant of the SPIIR pipeline using a hybrid log-likelihood ratio: (i) a glitch-consistency model (calibrated by source category and trigger-rate class) for non-Gaussian transients, and (ii) a profile likelihood ratio for SNR under Gaussian noise assumptions; FARs are assigned via empirical background estimation from the same detector and analysis chunk, with high-ranking tail extrapolation; calibration and validation performed on O3 data and GWTC-3 injections.

Result: At FAR = 0.5 per month: BNS sensitive distances are 136.5+2.9−3.0 Mpc (L1) and 120.2+3.1−3.3 Mpc (H1)—24% and 38% higher than GWTC-3 GstLAL; NSBH distances are 164.1+6.7−7.3 Mpc (L1) and 134.9+6.3−7.0 Mpc (H1), comparable to GstLAL; FAR assignments validated via background cumulative counts; known events GW190425, GW200105_162426, and GW230529_181500 recovered below threshold.

Conclusion: The single-detector SPIIR pipeline substantially enhances BNS detection reach in single-detector observing periods without compromising FAR fidelity, demonstrating the value of tailored glitch modeling and in-situ background calibration—enabling more robust and sensitive uncorrelated searches essential for expanding the gravitational-wave catalog during partial detector duty cycles.

Abstract: Periods with only one detector operating accounted for 13.3 percent of the third LIGO-Virgo observing run (O3) and 29.7 percent of the first part of the fourth observing run (O4a). We present the first dedicated single-detector implementation of the Summed Parallel Infinite Impulse Response (SPIIR) pipeline for binary neutron star (BNS) and neutron star-black hole (NSBH) merger searches. Our hybrid log-likelihood ratio combines signal-consistency models for non-Gaussian glitches with a profile likelihood ratio for the signal-to-noise ratio under a Gaussian-noise model. We calibrate the glitch model by source category and trigger-rate class. We assign false alarm rates (FARs) using the measured background from the same detector and analysis chunk, extrapolating its high-ranking tail. We check the FAR assignments by comparing observed and expected cumulative background counts for BNS in O3a and NSBH in O3b at LIGO Hanford (H1) and LIGO Livingston (L1). Using O3 data and the GWTC-3 injection set, we find that, at a threshold of one false alarm per two months, the BNS sensitive distances are $136.5^{+2.9}_{-3.0}$ Mpc in L1 and $120.2^{+3.1}_{-3.3}$ Mpc in H1. These central estimates are approximately 24 and 38 percent higher, respectively, than the GWTC-3 GstLAL values of $110.5^{+3.2}_{-3.4}$ Mpc and $87.2^{+3.6}_{-3.9}$ Mpc over the same data and injection parameter ranges. GstLAL provides the only published single-detector results in GWTC-3. For NSBH, SPIIR reaches $164.1^{+6.7}_{-7.3}$ Mpc in L1 and $134.9^{+6.3}_{-7.0}$ Mpc in H1, compared with $165.1^{+6.7}_{-7.3}$ Mpc and $156.0^{+5.9}_{-6.4}$ Mpc for GWTC-3 GstLAL, respectively. Targeted searches recover triggers associated with GW190425, GW200105_162426, and GW230529_181500 with assigned FARs below the same threshold.

</details>


### [36] [Probing superradiant cloud level transitions in stellar-mass black hole binaries with milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational wave observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20004)
*Si-Tong Peng,Anson Chen,Si-Yang Pu,Jun Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how superradiant clouds around stellar-mass black holes in binaries affect gravitational-wave signals across milli-Hertz (LISA) and ground-based bands, identifying phase corrections and merger-time shifts as probes of ultralight bosons.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect or constrain ultralight bosons via their superradiant cloud signatures in multi-band gravitational-wave observations of black hole binaries.

Result: Fine transitions imprint measurable phase corrections in LISA; Bohr transitions shift merger time detectable by ground-based detectors; cloud depletion can start before resonance; ionization accelerates (rather than delays) coalescence; posterior on initial cloud mass fraction is constrained to Mc^i/M ≲ 10⁻⁶ for a nearby binary.

Conclusion: Milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations provide complementary, sensitive probes of superradiant cloud evolution and thus ultralight bosons, motivating dedicated search strategies across detector bands.

Abstract: We investigate superradiant cloud level transitions in stellar-mass black hole binaries with milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations. Under the adopted cloud formation and survival conditions, the $\lvert322\rangle\rightarrow\lvert31-1\rangle$ fine transition can imprint phase corrections in the LISA band, while Bohr transitions occurring after the milli-Hertz band observation can be probed through shifts in the merger time. Modeling the coupled cloud-orbit evolution, we find that cloud depletion can begin before resonance and that ionization can advance rather than delay coalescence. For a representative nearby binary, a vacuum injection-recovery analysis yields a cloud-mass-fraction posterior concentrated at $M_c^i/M\lesssim10^{-6}$. Comparing the LISA-predicted coalescence time with a subsequent ground-based measurement provides a complementary test of later cloud evolution. These results motivate complementary searches for ultralight bosons with milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [37] [Canonical metrics on punctured spheres, II: compactified moduli spaces of black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20282)
*Boris Botvinnik*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the asymptotic behavior of canonical scalar-flat metrics on punctured spheres as subsets of punctures (representing black holes) collide, revealing a tree-like degeneration structure; it introduces a renormalized mass matrix extending smoothly over the Axelrod–Singer compactification, with geometric limits yielding bubble trees joined by Schwarzschild necks and minimal spheres interpreted as apparent horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the canonical scalar-flat metric and its associated mass matrix behave under collision of punctures—modeling black hole mergers—and to relate this degeneration to geometric compactifications and physical notions like horizons and mass inequalities.

Method: Analysis of asymptotics of the canonical scalar-flat metric $g_{\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}}$ and its mass matrix $\eta \in H_\ell$ as punctures coalesce; use of rooted trees to encode nested cluster collisions; renormalization by subtracting logarithmic divergences; extension to the Axelrod–Singer compactified moduli space $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell$; geometric convergence arguments with rescaling; comparison with the Riemannian Penrose inequality.

Result: The mass matrix diverges logarithmically along explicit rays $E(A)$ as clusters collapse; the renormalized mass matrix extends smoothly over strata of $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell$, decomposing per vertex of a bubble tree; rescaled metrics converge to complete scalar-flat metrics glued via Schwarzschild necks; for $n=3$, necks contain minimal spheres serving as common apparent horizons; a conjectural criterion for outermost horizon components is formulated; the mass matrix descends to a Deligne–Mumford-type quotient.

Conclusion: The degeneration geometry of multi-black-hole initial data is governed by a hierarchical, tree-structured renormalization of mass data, intrinsically linked to compactified moduli spaces and physically meaningful horizon structures—bridging geometric analysis, mathematical relativity, and moduli theory.

Abstract: Let $g_{\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}}$ denote the canonical scalar-flat metric on $S^n\setminus \mathfrak{p}_{\ell}$ introduced in Part~I, which for $n=3$ corresponds to the Brill--Lindquist initial data for $\ell$ black holes, and let $\eta\in H_{\ell}$ be its associated mass matrix. In this paper, we investigate the asymptotic behavior of the metrics and the mass matrix $\eta$ as subsets of punctures collide. Such collisions cause every mass to diverge; if a cluster $A$ of black holes collapses at a relative scale $t$, the mass matrix satisfies $\eta=E(A)\ln(1/t)+O(1)$, diverging along an explicit ray $E(A)\in H_{\ell}$. For nested families of clusters, this degeneracy structure naturally organizes into a space of rooted trees. Subtracting the divergence gives a renormalized mass matrix which extends smoothly to every stratum of the Axelrod--Singer compactified moduli space $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell= \overline C_{\ell-1}(\mathbb{R}^n)/\mathrm O(n)$ and splits naturally into one mass matrix per vertex of the bubble tree. Geometrically, at each vertex carrying punctures the suitably rescaled metrics converge to a complete scalar-flat metric with the nodes filled in, adjacent such pieces being joined by Schwarzschild necks, and the renormalized matrix acting as its mass data. For $n=3$, the necks contain minimal spheres, which are common apparent horizons of the merging holes. At vertices bearing two holes, we determine on which side of the limiting horizon each node lies, and we formulate as a conjecture the resulting criterion for a neck to be a component of the outermost horizon. We also compare the mass lower bound of Part~I with the Riemannian Penrose inequality. Because the mass matrix is invariant under rotations of the bubbles, it lives on a Deligne--Mumford-type quotient of the compactification.

</details>


### [38] [Repeated Gravitational-Wave Lensing from Binary Black Holes on Eccentric and Precessing Orbits around Spinning Supermassive Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20416)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li,Zhoujian Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates repeated gravitational lensing of gravitational waves from a stellar-mass binary black hole (BBH) orbiting a supermassive black hole (SMBH), modeling full post-Newtonian outer-orbit dynamics and moving-source waveform effects; it finds that outer-orbit evolution significantly alters lensing event timing and amplification, improves parameter constraints—especially on outer-orbit inclination and SMBH spin—and yields persistent waveform residuals even after local unlensed-model fitting, enabling complementary SMBH and orbital characterization in LISA data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how outer-orbit evolution (eccentricity, inclination, relativistic precession) in hierarchical triple systems affects repeated gravitational lensing of BBH gravitational waves by the SMBH, and to assess its impact on detectability and parameter inference for LISA.

Method: Models the outer orbit with post-Newtonian dynamics; incorporates moving-source effects and de Sitter precession into the inspiral waveform; uses an event-local point-mass lensing model to track time-varying source–lens alignment and GW frequency modulation; performs Fisher-matrix uncertainty estimation, lensing-event identification, and residual analysis over one-year LISA observations.

Result: Outer-orbit evolution changes timing/amplification of successive lensing events and can bring initially non-lensing configurations into the lensing regime; a fixed circular/nonprecessing approximation misses lensing for many systems; median fractional uncertainty reductions are largest for outer-orbit inclination and SMBH spin; lensing-induced waveform residuals persist after local unlensed-model fitting for some detectable systems.

Conclusion: Repeated gravitational lensing in evolving hierarchical BBH–SMBH systems provides complementary, physically distinct constraints on SMBH spin and outer-orbit geometry in LISA observations—beyond what standard unlensed waveforms offer.

Abstract: Gravitational waves (GWs) from a stellar-mass binary black hole (BBH) orbiting a supermassive black hole (SMBH) can experience repeated gravitational lensing as the BBH passes behind the SMBH. Our previous studies of orbit-induced GW modulation motivate this investigation of repeated lensing in evolving hierarchical triple systems. We investigate how eccentricity, inclination, and relativistic precession of the outer orbit affect successive lensing events and parameter inference. The outer orbit follows post-Newtonian dynamics, and the inspiral waveform includes moving-source effects and de Sitter precession. An event-local point-mass lensing model tracks the changing source--lens alignment and GW frequency. For a one-year LISA observation, we examine lensing-event identification, Fisher-matrix estimates of parameter uncertainties, and waveform residuals when lensing is omitted. Our results show that outer-orbit evolution changes the timing and amplification of successive lensing events and can bring initially unfavorable configurations into the lensing region. A fixed circular, nonprecessing approximation misses all lensing events for a substantial fraction of the selected lensed systems. The median fractional reductions in uncertainty are largest for the outer-orbit inclination and SMBH spin. For some systems above the adopted detection threshold, lensing-induced waveform differences persist after local adjustments to the parameters of the unlensed model. Repeated lensing can thus provide complementary constraints on SMBH properties and outer-orbit geometry in LISA observations of hierarchical BBHs.

</details>


### [39] [Harmonic branches of Einstein--ModMax--scalar field gravity: exact solutions and regularity II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20529)
*Leonel Bixano,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs harmonic solution branches for Einstein–ModMax–Scalar field gravity with non-constant electromagnetic phase Θ, derives physical fields from metric and gauge potentials, classifies known and new static/dyonic solutions (including RN, Taub–NUT, Melvin–swirling), proves theorems on phase constancy vs. charge rescaling and twist-induced obstruction to diagonal EM rescaling, and identifies smooth horizon limits versus constitutive continuation at null equatorial circles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend known Maxwell-gravity solutions to the nonlinear ModMax electrodynamics coupled to a scalar field, while allowing nontrivial electromagnetic phase structure (i.e., non-constant tanΘ = G/F), thereby uncovering new exact solutions and clarifying geometric/physical obstructions to naive generalizations.

Method: Harmonic map formulation of field equations; integration of solution branches via harmonic functions (Schwarzschild/Coulomb-type); reconstruction of physical metric components (f, ω, k), gauge potentials (A_t, A_φ), and scalar field φ; imposition of regular horizon conditions; classification via symmetry (static/stationary, axial) and coupling structure; analytical theorems on electromagnetic phase constancy and twist-induced rescaling obstruction.

Result: New explicit ModMax extensions of RN, Taub–NUT, single-charge, and static dyonic solutions with scalar-dependent couplings; a restricted stationary family with active scalar; exact ModMax Schwarzschild exterior embedded in Melvin–swirling background; identification of smooth horizon limits versus constitutive continuation at null equatorial circle; rigorous distinction between constant-phase and rescaled-charge configurations; recovery of known Maxwell limits and uncharged cases.

Conclusion: Non-constant electromagnetic phase is essential for generating physically distinct ModMax–scalar–gravity solutions beyond trivial Maxwell deformations; twist fundamentally obstructs naive diagonal rescalings of potentials; harmonic methods provide a unified framework for cataloguing both known and novel exact solutions across static and restricted stationary sectors.

Abstract: We integrate harmonic solution branches of Einstein--ModMax--Scalar field gravity, keeping the electromagnetic phase \(\tan{\Theta}=\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{F}\) non-constant. For each family we first solve the field equations $(a(\s),b(\s),c(\s))$ and reconstruct the physical functions $f,\omega,k,A_t,A_\varphi,\phi$. In the same section we then select the Schwarzschild or Coulomb harmonic function, impose the regular horizon conditions, identify the ModMax solution, and state its Maxwell limit. Two theorems distinguish a constant electromagnetic phase from an exact static charge rescaling and show how twist prevents constant diagonal rescalings of the electromagnetic potentials at fixed metric and scalar. The catalogue covers known RN and charged Taub--NUT solutions, single-charge configurations, explicit static dyons with scalar-dependent couplings, and a restricted stationary family with an active scalar. We distinguish new explicit ModMax extensions of known Maxwell profiles from the nonfrozen stationary integration. The axial LWP reconstruction recovers the known Melvin--swirling background and gives an exact ModMax Schwarzschild exterior within that background. At its horizon, smooth metric and electromagnetic limits are distinguished from the constitutive continuation at a null equatorial circle. Uncharged configurations and earlier Maxwell fields are traced back to the preceding harmonic catalogue.

</details>


### [40] [Quasinormal modes of Kerr--de Sitter black holes in semi-open systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20627)
*Ming-Fei Ji,Liang-Bi Wu,Yu-Sen Zhou,Li-Ming Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of Kerr–de Sitter black holes with a partially reflective inner boundary near the event horizon, using Heun equation reductions of Teukolsky equations; it identifies echo branches, superradiant and wall-driven instabilities, reveals divergent energy reflectivity at mΩ(r₊), and shows how boundary condition formulation (asymptotic vs. finite-radius Robin) critically affects spectral structure and stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how semi-open black hole systems—specifically Kerr–de Sitter spacetimes with an artificial reflective inner boundary—behave dynamically, particularly regarding stability, QNM spectrum morphology, and the physical interpretation of reflective boundary conditions in gravitational wave contexts.

Method: Reduces angular and radial Teukolsky equations for spin s = −2 perturbations to Heun equations; numerically computes QNMs for ℓ = m = 2 modes while varying reflectivity ℛ₀ and rotation parameter a; analyzes asymptotic amplitude-based vs. finite-radius Robin boundary conditions; derives analytic relation between Teukolsky amplitude and energy reflectivities; identifies resonance structures via connection coefficients.

Result: 1) Increasing ℛ₀ generates echo-like QNM branches approaching the real axis with regular spacing; other modes converge to Re ω = mΩ(r₊). 2) Increasing a causes bifurcation: some modes approach real axis or retain finite damping; others shift horizontally tracking mΩ(r₊). 3) Two instability mechanisms identified: superradiance and energy injection from the reflective wall. 4) Energy reflectivity diverges at mΩ(r₊) even for small Teukolsky amplitude reflectivity—signifying 'active wall' behavior. 5) Asymptotic-amplitude boundary conditions produce spurious resonances near poles of ingoing connection coefficient; finite-radius Robin condition on Sasaki–Nakamura variable eliminates them.

Conclusion: The choice and formulation of the inner boundary condition fundamentally control the QNM spectrum’s topology, stability properties, and physical interpretation in semi-open Kerr–de Sitter systems; distinguishing between amplitude and energy reflectivity is essential, and only physically motivated finite-radius conditions yield robust, pole-free spectra.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational quasinormal mode spectrum of Kerr--de Sitter black holes with a partially reflective inner boundary near the event horizon, by reducing the angular and radial Teukolsky equations to Heun equations. We vary different parameters and study the $s=-2$, $\ell=m=2$ modes numerically. As the reflectivity $\mathcal{R}_0$ increases, QNMs develop echo branches approaching the real axis with approximately regular spacing, while others approach $\operatorname{Re}\omega=m\Omega(r_+)$. As the rotation $a$ increases, some QNMs approach the real axis or retain finite damping, while others exhibit approximately horizontal shifts with real parts tracking $m\Omega(r_+)$. Two mechanisms driving the instabilities are identified in complementary regimes, namely superradiant amplification and energy injection from the reflective wall. We derive the ratio between Teukolsky amplitude and energy reflectivities, finding that the latter can diverge at $m\Omega(r_+)$ even when the former is small, which accounts for an active wall in this regime. We also find that formulating the reflective boundary condition in terms of asymptotic amplitudes gives rise to resonances near each pole of the ingoing connection coefficient, extending to arbitrarily large positive imaginary parts. An exact finite radius Robin condition imposed on the Sasaki--Nakamura variable removes these pole-born frequencies. These results show how the inner boundary prescription controls the spectrum structure and stability of semi-open Kerr--de Sitter systems.

</details>


### [41] [Simultaneous tests of post-Newtonian inspiral using GW250114 and GW250207](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20738)
*Saurabh Kumar,Parthapratim Mahapatra,Ish Mohan Gupta,M. Saleem,B. S. Sathyaprakash,K. G. Arun*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper conducts a high-dimensional test of post-Newtonian (PN) inspiral in binary black holes by simultaneously varying all eight phase coefficients up to 3.5PN order, using GW250114 and GW250207 with the IMRPhenomXPHM_SpinTaylor waveform model augmented by parametrized PN deviations; principal component analysis identifies three well-constrained linear combinations of coefficients—all consistent with general relativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform a robust, high-dimensional test of general relativity's post-Newtonian predictions during the inspiral phase of binary black hole mergers, moving beyond single-parameter or low-dimensional deviation analyses.

Method: Bayesian inference using two loud BBH events (GW250114 and GW250207) with an augmented IMRPhenomXPHM_SpinTaylor waveform that includes eight independent parametrized deviations to the PN phase coefficients; followed by principal component analysis (PCA) of the 8D deformation space to identify dominant constrained directions.

Result: Three leading principal components of the 8D phase-coefficient space are constrained beyond priors; all three are consistent with general-relativistic predictions; no statistically significant deviation from GR is found.

Conclusion: Exceptionally loud gravitational-wave events enable stringent, multi-dimensional tests of PN inspiral dynamics; the measurable subspace of PN deformations is directly probed and remains fully consistent with general relativity.

Abstract: We perform the one of the most general tests of post-Newtonian (PN) inspiral in binary black holes to date by allowing all eight phase coefficients up to 3.5PN order to vary simultaneously from their general-relativistic values. Using two of the loudest binary black hole events observed to date, GW250114 and GW250207, we carry out Bayesian inference with the IMRPhenomXPHM_SpinTaylor waveform model augmented by parametrized deviations in the post-Newtonian phasing. We then identify the combinations of phase coefficients most strongly constrained by the data through a principal component analysis of the eight-dimensional deformation space. We find that the data constrain the three leading principal components beyond their priors, and that all three are consistent with the predictions of general relativity. These results demonstrate that exceptionally loud gravitational-wave events enable robust, high-dimensional tests of inspiral dynamics and provide direct access to the measurable subspace of post-Newtonian deformations.

</details>

### [43] [Gravitational collapse in Lorentz-violating gravity and the spin-0 mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21034)
*Emma Albertini,Massimo Vaglio,Enrico Barausse*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the physical nature of the spin-0 mode in Einstein-Aether theory via fully nonlinear spherical collapse simulations, confirming it is a dynamical, energy-carrying wave with positive energy flux; it also reveals novel black-hole–avoiding dynamics at large couplings, possibly indicating naked singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve recent doubts—raised by Taherasghari and Will's post-Newtonian analysis—about whether the spin-0 mode in Einstein-Aether theory is a genuine dynamical, energy-carrying degree of freedom, given its absence in radiation-reaction energy loss calculations.

Method: Fully nonlinear, spherically symmetric numerical evolutions of scalar-field collapse in Einstein-Aether theory, isolating the spin-0 mode as the sole radiative degree of freedom; analysis of wave propagation speed, asymptotic falloff, energy flux, and final spacetime structure (e.g., horizon formation, ADM vs. horizon mass, trapped surfaces).

Result: The spin-0 mode propagates outward at its linear-theory predicted speed, decays as 1/r, carries positive energy to infinity; the static aether remnant has negative energy, causing horizon mass > ADM mass; for large coupling constants, collapse avoids black hole formation—producing steepening, untrapped shells consistent with transient white holes and possibly naked singularities.

Conclusion: The spin-0 mode is confirmed to be a physical, dynamical, energy-carrying wave, refuting claims that it lacks radiative character; moreover, strong-coupling dynamics reveal exotic gravitational collapse outcomes incompatible with standard black hole formation, challenging classical cosmic censorship in this theory.

Abstract: Einstein-Aether theory is a theory of gravity with a preferred frame, in which Lorentz symmetry is broken by a dynamical unit timelike vector field. The theory is closely related to Horava gravity, and in spherically symmetric collapse the two produce the same dynamics. Both propagate a scalar (spin-0) mode besides the tensor mode and, in Einstein-Aether theory, the vector mode. The physical nature of the spin-0 mode has recently been called into question following the post-Newtonian analysis of Taherasghari and Will, whose post-Newtonian calculation of radiation reaction in compact binaries finds no trace of its speed in the energy loss, suggesting that the mode may not be a dynamical, energy-carrying wave. We test this with fully nonlinear evolutions of scalar-field collapse in spherical symmetry, where the spin-0 mode is the only radiative degree of freedom. The collapse emits an outgoing spin-0 wave that falls off as the inverse of the radius, travels at the speed predicted by the linear theory, and carries positive energy to infinity. The static aether configuration left around the black hole has negative energy instead, so that the horizon mass exceeds the ADM mass. For large couplings, collapse does not produce a black hole: we recover the transient white holes found by Akhoury, Garfinkle and Gupta, but at higher resolution the collapsing shell keeps steepening, not hidden behind any trapped surface, which may signal a naked singularity.

</details>

*Ginés R. Pérez Teruel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes κ(R,T) gravity using Chen's local adjoint action and Dirac reduction in flat FLRW cosmology, revealing a fold singularity (not a bounce), physical stress non-conservation, dressed particle number conservation, polarizable-medium interpretation of κ, exact correspondence with loop quantum cosmology's Friedmann equation for linear dust, and an open-system quantum interpretation via Koopman–von Neumann quantization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the physical content and consistency of κ(R,T) gravity—particularly its field equations derived from a local variational principle—and to clarify its cosmological implications, singularity structure, conservation laws, and connections to loop quantum cosmology.

Method: Application of Chen's local adjoint action to derive field equations; Dirac constraint analysis in flat FLRW; Hamiltonian reduction; decomposition of effective energy-momentum source; comparison with loop quantum cosmology (LQC); Koopman–von Neumann transport quantization of the reduced dust constraint.

Result: Dirac reduction yields a Hamiltonian independent of κ(T) on regular branches; physical stress tensor is not conserved but dressed particle number is; effective source admits polarizable-medium interpretation; linear dust model reproduces LQC’s effective Friedmann equation but violates standard conservation; a fold—not a smooth bounce—occurs at finite density ρ_c/2 and curvature; smooth Hamiltonian continuation through fold is ruled out; flat-FLRW bounce excluded for κ>0, ρ>0, w>−1; fold density matches signature-change threshold in deformed-algebra LQC.

Conclusion: κ(R,T) gravity, despite sharing phenomenological features with LQC (e.g., effective Friedmann equation), exhibits fundamentally distinct dynamics—especially in conservation laws and singularity resolution—supporting an open-system interpretation where the adjoint field acts as a response variable, and the cosmological evolution terminates at a fold rather than undergoing a bounce.

Abstract: Chen's local adjoint action reproduces the field equations of $\kappa(R,T)$ from a local variational principle, and we show that it does physical work. In flat FLRW, Dirac reduction gives a Hamiltonian independent of $\kappa(T)$ on regular branches, where the coupling enters through a second-class matter constraint. The physical stress tensor is not generally conserved, but a dressed particle number is. An exact decomposition of the effective source motivates a polarizable-medium interpretation: $\kappa=0$ corresponds to total screening, the repulsive phase to overscreening, and a fold of the matter map to saturation. For linear coupling and dust, the expanding low-density branch has a past endpoint at the fold, where the volume is minimal and the density reaches $\rho_c/2$ at finite curvature. Dirac consistency rules out smooth Hamiltonian continuation through the fold. More generally, a flat-FLRW bounce is excluded for $\kappa>0$, positive density and $w>-1$. The linear dust model reproduces the effective Friedmann equation of loop quantum cosmology (LQC), but the two models obey different conservation laws. An effective pressure $-\rho^2/\rho_c$ restores the homogeneous LQC dynamics, and the fold density coincides with the signature-change threshold in the deformed-algebra approach to LQC. The reduced dust constraint admits an exactly solvable Koopman--von Neumann transport quantization and the adjoint field acts as a response variable, motivating an open-system interpretation.

</details>


### [45] [Critical curves and throat degeneracy in black bounces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21355)
*Francisco S. N. Lobo,Diogo Mesquita,Luís F. Dias da Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how static spherical areal-radius deformations affect null geodesics—particularly photon spheres and critical orbits—showing that while critical impact parameters and orbital frequencies remain invariant under such deformations, stability (Lyapunov exponent) and strong-deflection behavior scale with geometric factors like the radial Jacobian or throat curvature; it derives universal scaling laws for degenerate optical potentials and applies them to the Simpson–Visser spacetime, revealing a quartic merger at a=3M separating stable/unstable throat branches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which features of null-orbit dynamics (e.g., critical impact parameter, stability, strong deflection) are preserved versus modified under static, spherically symmetric radial deformations of spacetime geometry—specifically to disentangle coordinate-invariant orbital properties from those dependent on local geometric structure.

Method: Analytical perturbation and scaling analysis of the effective potential for null geodesics in static, spherically symmetric spacetimes with deformed areal radius r → R(r); derivation of coordinate-time Lyapunov exponents, strong-deflection coefficients, and asymptotic orbit behaviors near degenerate critical points; application to reflection-symmetric throats and the Simpson–Visser family via classification of extremal orders (m, q) yielding optical degeneracy order p = mq.

Result: 1) Critical impact parameter b_c and orbital frequency Ω_c are invariant under areal-radius deformation; 2) Coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent λ scales by radial Jacobian ∂R/∂r; 3) Logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient scales inversely; 4) At reflection-symmetric throats, stability factorizes into derivative of original optical potential and throat curvature; 5) Order-m extremum composed with order-q radius minimum yields optical degeneracy of order p = mq, with characteristic approach |x| ∝ |t|^{−2/(p−2)} and deflection Δϕ ∝ (b/b_c − 1)^{−(p−2)/(2p)}; 6) For Simpson–Visser spacetime, b_c = 3√3 M remains Schwarzschild-like for a < 3M, but instability changes with a; a quartic merger occurs at a = 3M, separating stable (a > 3M) and unstable (a < 3M) throat branches.

Conclusion: Null-orbit criticality (b_c, Ω_c) is robust under areal-radius deformations and encodes global, deformation-invariant information, whereas stability and strong-field lensing signatures are sensitive probes of local geometry—including throat curvature and higher-order degeneracies—enabling geometric diagnostics from photon sphere observations.

Abstract: Identical critical curves need not imply identical null-orbit dynamics. For a class of static spherical areal-radius deformations, we show that a surviving original photon sphere retains its critical impact parameter and orbital frequency, while its coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent is multiplied by the radial Jacobian. The logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient acquires the inverse factor. At a reflection-symmetric throat, stability factorizes into a derivative of the original optical potential and the throat curvature. More generally, an order-$m$ extremum of the original potential composed with an order-$q$ areal-radius minimum produces an optical degeneracy of order $p=mq$, with critical approach $|x|\propto|t|^{-2/(p-2)}$ and reflected strong deflection $\Delta\phi\propto(b/b_{\rm c}-1)^{-(p-2)/(2p)}$ for $p>2$. For the Simpson--Visser family with mass $M$ and bounce parameter $a$, the critical impact parameter remains Schwarzschild-like for $a<3M$, although the instability varies with $a$. The merger at $a=3M$ is quartic, separating stable and unstable throat branches. These results isolate the null-orbit information preserved by a radial deformation from that encoded in its local geometry.

</details>


### [46] [The Stability of the Higgs vacuum inside a Schwarzschild Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21356)
*Paul G. Abel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper studies Higgs field dynamics inside a Schwarzschild black hole, showing that due to timelike radial coordinate, the Higgs condensate evolves in time and remains in its broken phase until Planck-scale curvature is reached—regardless of black hole mass; inclusion of one-loop corrections modifies the solution only modestly (~10% reduction), preserving electroweak symmetry breaking throughout the interior within effective field theory validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether electroweak symmetry remains broken inside black holes, where spacetime geometry drastically alters field evolution—especially given that standard static analyses fail in the black hole interior where the radial coordinate becomes timelike.

Method: Analyzes the Higgs field equation in Schwarzschild interior with a Planck-suppressed nonminimal coupling to the Kretschmann scalar; reduces the equation near the singularity to Bessel's equation; incorporates one-loop vacuum fluctuations via a sourced Bessel equation solved exactly.

Result: Higgs condensate follows $H = v J_0(x)$ near singularity, stays frozen in broken phase until curvature reaches Planck scale; one-loop correction yields at most ~10% suppression of condensate magnitude.

Conclusion: Electroweak symmetry remains broken throughout the black hole interior within the domain of validity of effective field theory, independent of black hole mass.

Abstract: I investigate the behaviour of the Higgs field inside a Schwarzschild black hole, including a Planck-suppressed coupling between the Higgs field and the Kretschmann scalar, the leading curvature invariant in a Ricci-flat geometry. Because the radial coordinate is timelike in the interior, the Higgs condensate evolves in time rather than tracking the minimum of its potential. Near the singularity the interior field equation reduces to Bessel's equation, with solution $H=vJ_0(x)$, where $x\propto\sqrt\xi\,t_p/\tau$ and $\tau$ is the proper time to the singularity. Contrary to static analyses, the field remains frozen in its broken phase while the potential changes shape, departing from its vacuum value only when the curvature becomes Planckian, independently of the black-hole mass. Including the one-loop correction from Higgs vacuum fluctuations yields the same Bessel equation with a source term, which I solve exactly; the condensate is reduced by at most about ten per cent. The electroweak symmetry therefore remains broken throughout the interior within the regime of validity of effective field theory.

</details>


### [47] [Pulsar timing response and observational constraints on continuous-spin gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21512)
*Jing-Hong Han,Zhi-Chao Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies continuous-spin particles (CSPs) as massless, integer-helicity representations of the Poincaré group, and constrains their continuous-spin scale ρ_g using NANOGrav 15-year pulsar timing array data; it derives redshift response and overlap reduction functions for arbitrary helicity, finds only h=±1 modes affect observer four-velocity, recovers Hellings–Downs correlation for h=±2 in the GR limit, and sets stringent 95% upper limits on ρ_g (~10⁻²³ eV), while improving agreement of the inferred gravitational-wave spectral index γ_GW with the SMBHB prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test fundamental extensions of massless particle representations beyond standard helicity states (e.g., photons, gravitons) by probing continuous-spin particles—whose existence would imply infinite-helicity mixing under boosts—and to leverage nanohertz gravitational-wave astronomy (via pulsar timing arrays) as a unique low-energy probe of the continuous-spin scale ρ_g, inaccessible to high-energy experiments.

Method: Starting from the gauge-invariant worldline coupling of linearized continuous-spin gravity, the authors derive the one-way pulsar timing redshift response function and the overlap reduction function (ORF) for an isotropic stochastic background across all integer helicities h ∈ ℤ; they model an unpolarized h = ±2 background in the solar-system barycentric frame and perform Bayesian joint inference on NANOGrav 15-year data including pulsar red noise, under uniform and log-uniform priors on ρ_g.

Result: The analysis yields 95% upper limits: ρ_g^U < 7.03×10⁻²³ eV (uniform prior) and ρ_g^{log U} < 4.79×10⁻²³ eV (log-uniform prior); the inferred gravitational-wave spectral index is γ_GW = 4.89⁺⁰.⁸⁵₋₀.₉₆ (uniform prior), closer to the canonical SMBHB value γ_GW = 13/3 ≈ 4.33 than standard GR fits; vector modes (h = ±1) perturb observer four-velocity, while tensor modes (h = ±2) recover Hellings–Downs correlations as ρ_g/(ℏω) → 0.

Conclusion: Nanohertz pulsar timing provides a uniquely sensitive probe of continuous-spin gravity at ultralow ρ_g scales; current NANOGrav data strongly constrain CSP contributions to stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds, disfavoring large ρ_g; the improved spectral index suggests CSP-influenced propagation may better accommodate astrophysical SMBHB populations than pure GR, though no detection is claimed—only tighter bounds and phenomenological consistency.

Abstract: Continuous-spin particles form the most general massless integer-helicity irreducible representations of the Poincaré group, characterized by a dimensionful continuous-spin scale $\rho_g$ set by a nonzero Pauli--Lubanski invariant. A single continuous-spin representation contains an infinite tower of integer helicities $h\in\mathbb Z$ that mix under Lorentz boosts. Since continuous-spin effects are controlled by $\alpha\equiv\rho_g/(\hbar\omega)$ at gravitational-wave angular frequency $\omega$, nanohertz pulsar timing arrays are naturally sensitive to ultralow values of $\rho_g$. Starting from the gauge-invariant worldline coupling of linearized continuous-spin gravity, we derive the one-way pulsar timing redshift response and the overlap reduction function of an isotropic stochastic background for arbitrary integer helicity. We show that while photon propagation is modulated across all helicities, only the vector modes ($h=\pm1$) perturb the four-velocity of a static observer, and that the tensor modes ($h=\pm2$) smoothly recover the Hellings--Downs correlation of general relativity as $\rho_g/(\hbar\omega)\to 0$. Modeling an unpolarized $h=\pm2$ background in the solar-system barycentric frame and jointly fitting the NANOGrav 15-year data with pulsar red noise, we obtain the $95\%$ upper limits $\rho^{U}_g<7.03\times10^{-23}\,\mathrm{eV}$ and $\rho^{\log {U}}_g<4.79\times10^{-23}\,\mathrm{eV}$ under uniform and log-uniform priors, respectively. Under the uniform prior, we obtain $\gamma_{\mathrm{GW}} = 4.89^{+0.85}_{-0.96}$, which brings the spectral index closer to the canonical value $\gamma_{\rm GW}=13/3$ expected for circular SMBHBs than standard General Relativity.

</details>


### [48] [Separation of gravitational perturbations of Myers-Perry black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21591)
*Jun-Kun Zhao,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs separated solutions for linearized gravitational perturbations of 5D asymptotically flat Myers–Perry black holes with two unequal spins, leveraging Killing–Yano symmetry to derive commuting angular operators; it proves completeness of angular harmonics, enables local metric reconstruction, and recovers known results in the singly spinning limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous framework for analyzing gravitational stability and quasinormal modes of five-dimensional rotating black holes with two independent angular momenta — a case not fully addressed by prior separation-based methods.

Method: Uses gauge-invariant formalism and separation of variables; exploits the Killing–Yano tensor to construct a first-order angular operator that commutes with a second-order one; applies Carter interchange to link angular and radial equations; proves completeness of angular eigenfunctions at real frequencies; verifies local metric reconstructibility from perturbative data.

Result: Full separation of the five polarizations of linearized gravity; explicit construction of coupled angular eigenoperators; shared radial amplitude across all tidal components; completeness of angular harmonics; local metric reconstruction; recovery of Santos's results in the singly spinning subsector (m_ψ = 0, even ψ-parity).

Conclusion: The Killing–Yano symmetry fully controls the angular structure of gravitational perturbations in 5D Myers–Perry spacetimes, enabling a complete, stable, and reconstructible perturbation theory suitable for stability analysis and QNM spectroscopy.

Abstract: We study linearized gravitational perturbations of five-dimensional asymptotically flat Myers--Perry black holes with two unequal spins. We construct separated solutions for the five coupled gauge-invariant polarizations for arbitrary azimuthal mode numbers. The key observation is that the Killing--Yano symmetry induces a first-order angular operator that, together with a commuting second-order operator, determines the angular profiles of all five gravitational polarizations. The Carter interchange relates the scalar angular and radial equations, and the five tidal components of each separated solution share a single radial amplitude. We prove completeness of the angular harmonics at real frequencies and show that the resulting physical perturbations admit local metric reconstruction. In the singly spinning limit, we recover Santos's angular operators and radial equation for perturbations with $m_\psi=0$ and even parity under $\psi\to-\psi$. These results provide a framework for studying quasinormal modes and gravitational stability of five-dimensional rotating black holes with two independent spins.

</details>
