### [49] [Sensitivity of the SHiP experiment at CERN to sterile-neutrino-induced oscillations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18824)
*SHiP Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: SHiP experiment leverages its near (SND) and far (calorimeter) detectors to perform a two-baseline sterile neutrino oscillation search, achieving competitive 95% CL sensitivity to mixing angles |Uμ4|² and |Ue4|² in the ~1–50 eV mass range using 6×10²⁰ POT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe light sterile neutrinos via νμ and νe disappearance using SHiP's unique dual-detector geometry and high-intensity beam-dump neutrino flux.

Method: Geant4 simulation of GENIE-generated neutrino interactions; energy response modeling for νe CC and νμ CC events; background discrimination (especially π⁰ vs νe CC); near/far disappearance analysis with 20% flux-shape and 1% normalization uncertainties.

Result: Projected 95% CL median sensitivities: |Uμ4|² ≈ 1.2×10⁻⁵ at m₄ ≈ 3.5 eV and |Ue4|² ≈ 2.5×10⁻⁴ at m₄ ≈ 25 eV; muon-channel sensitivity improves upon existing νμ-disappearance limits for 0.7 eV ≲ m₄ ≲ 25 eV.

Conclusion: SHiP’s two-baseline configuration provides world-leading sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrinos in both muon and electron neutrino channels, particularly enhancing constraints on νμ disappearance where current limits are weakest.

Abstract: The SHiP experiment, approved for the high-intensity beam-dump facility in the ECN3 hall at the CERN SPS in the North Area, will record neutrino interactions both in its scattering and neutrino detector (SND) and, about \SI{70}{m} further downstream, in its calorimeter system. We show that the baseline SHiP detector configuration therefore forms a two-baseline experiment for oscillations induced by a sterile neutrino. From a \textsc{Geant4} simulation of \textsc{Genie}-generated interactions we derive the energy response of both stations to $\nue$ and $\numu$ charged-current (CC) interactions and the separation of $\nue$~CC events from the neutral-current $\pizero$ background, and fold them into a near/far disappearance analysis with a $20\%$ flux-shape uncertainty and a $1\%$ far/near normalisation uncertainty. For $6\times10^{20}$ protons on target, the projected $95\%$~CL median sensitivities are $\Umufour\simeq\UmuLimit$ at $\mfour\simeq\UmuLimitMass$~eV and $\Uefour\simeq\UeLimit$ at $\mfour\simeq\UeLimitMass$~eV. In the muon channel this lies below existing $\numu$-disappearance limits for $\DnineLow\lesssim\mfour\lesssim\DnineHigh$~eV.

</details>


### [50] [Quantum-correlation of neutral charmed mesons at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18944)
*Shenghui Zeng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: BESIII measures strong-phase differences in multiple D-meson decays using quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs from psi(3770) and higher-energy data, achieving first observation of C-even D-Dbar pairs and improved constraints on CKM angle gamma.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strong-phase differences between D0 and Dbar0 decays are essential inputs for determining the CKM angle gamma and understanding charm mixing; quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs provide direct access to these phases.

Method: Analysis of quantum-correlated D-Dbar events from BESIII's 20.3 fb^-1 psi(3770) dataset (C-odd), plus novel method using multi-photon-tagged events at 4.13–4.23 GeV to isolate C-even and C-odd D-Dbar production states.

Result: New and improved measurements of strong-phase differences for D→K_S,Lπ⁺π⁻, D→K⁺K⁻π⁺π⁻, D→K⁻π⁺π⁺π⁻, D→K⁻π⁺π⁰; first observation of C-even D-Dbar pairs; new strong-phase difference measurement for D→K⁻π⁺; CP-even fraction measured in D→K⁺K⁻π⁺π⁻.

Conclusion: The results significantly advance precision in charm CP violation studies, enable tighter constraints on gamma, and demonstrate a robust new technique for exploiting quantum correlations beyond the psi(3770) resonance.

Abstract: Quantum-correlated $D\bar D$ pairs give direct access to the strong-phase differences between $D^0$ and $\bar D^0$ decays, which are essential inputs to the CKM angle $\gamma$ and charm mixing. The BESIII experiment has accumulated the largest data sample at the $\psi(3770)$ resonance, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $20.3\,\text{fb}^{-1}$. The neutral $D\bar D$ pairs produced at $\psi(3770)$ are in a $C$-odd state. We report new and improved measurements of the strong-phase differences in $D\rightarrow K_{S,L}\pi^+\pi^-$, $D\rightarrow K^+K^-\pi^+\pi^-$, $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+\pi^+\pi^-$ and $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+\pi^0$ decays, alongside a measurement of the $CP$-even fraction in $D\rightarrow K^+K^-\pi^+\pi^-$. Furthermore, we present a novel method that studies the $D\bar D$ quantum correlations using data collected at centre-of-mass energies from $4.13$ to $4.23\,$GeV, where the $D\bar D$ pairs are produced in $C$-even or $C$-odd states, according to the number of accompanying photons. This gives the first observation of $C$-even $D\bar D$ pairs and a new measurement of the strong-phase difference in $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+$ decays.

</details>


### [51] [Cosmic ray muon seasonal variations with the ProtoDUNE-SP Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18997)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To validate ProtoDUNE-SP’s capability to detect subtle, long-timescale physical variations—specifically, atmospheric temperature–driven seasonal modulation of cosmic-ray muon flux—as a benchmark for its stability and sensitivity in neutrino experiments.

Method: Analysis of single-muon events from cosmic-ray runs collected between December 2018 and July 2020 in the ProtoDUNE single-phase LArTPC at CERN’s Neutrino Platform; muon rate was reconstructed as a function of time and fitted for sinusoidal seasonal modulation.

Conclusion: ProtoDUNE-SP is capable of precision, long-duration monitoring of low-rate, time-varying signals; this result validates its readiness for physics analyses requiring stable, sub-percent-level temporal calibration, including neutrino oscillation studies.

Abstract: We present the measurement of the cosmic ray muon seasonal variation using single-muon events with the ProtoDUNE single-phase detector located at the CERN Neutrino Platform, using data collected from December 2018 to July 2020. Cosmic ray muon runs recorded during this period are analyzed to determine the muon rate as a function of time. The results show a seasonal modulation of the muon rate, with a significance of 4 {\sigma} relative to the null hypothesis of no seasonal variation. The observed muon rate reaches a maximum during the winter, consistent with measurements from other surface experiments with a low muon-energy threshold. This measurement is possible due to the overall excellent performance of this detector technology and serves as a clear demonstration that such a detector can stably measure small, time-dependent physical signals such as seasonal variations in cosmic muon rates over long timescales.

</details>


### [52] [High-Statistics Study of Antiproton Annihilation at Rest on Argon in a LArTPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19401)
*M. Tanaka,K. Yorita,S. Arai,K. Ishikawa,Y. Utsumi,C. Watanabe,Y. Yano,T. Akaishi,K. Aoyama,S. Arai,T. Aramaki,T. Hashimoto,G. Karagiorgi,M. Kimura,Y. Kimura,J. Leyva,Y. Ma,J. Mitchell,T. Nanamura,H. Odaka,M. Sasaki,S. Takashima,S. Tandon,R. Tatsumi,A. Barnett,N. Cannady,C. Earley,K. Sakai,J. Zeng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-statistics study of antiproton–argon annihilation at rest using a LArTPC at J-PARC, benchmarking hadronic interaction models (Fritiof, INCL++, GiBUU) against measured energy deposition and cluster topologies; GiBUU best reproduces localized energy deposition and spatial cluster multiplicities due to its treatment of nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a high-statistics experimental benchmark for hadronic interaction models in liquid argon, supporting future antimatter searches (e.g., GRAMS) and improving simulation fidelity for low-energy antiproton annihilations.

Method: Analysis of >10⁵ antiproton–argon annihilation events at rest in a single-phase LArTPC; development of a Geant4-based simulation framework modeling secondary beam transport and detector response; comparison of measured annihilation topologies, localized energy profiles, and spatial cluster multiplicities with predictions from Fritiof, INCL++, and GiBUU.

Result: All three models reproduce total energy deposition, but GiBUU most accurately describes localized energy deposition near the vertex and spatial cluster multiplicities at larger radii—attributed to its inclusion of secondary nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption.

Conclusion: GiBUU emerges as the most empirically consistent model for low-energy antiproton interactions in liquid argon; this dataset and simulation framework establish a critical benchmark for LArTPC-based antimatter physics and hadronic modeling.

Abstract: This paper reports a high-statistics study of antiproton--argon ($\bar{p}\text{--}\text{Ar}$) annihilation at rest in the J-PARC T98 experiment using a single-phase Liquid Argon Time Projection Chamber (LArTPC). A dataset comprising over $10^{5}$ annihilation events was acquired together with comprehensive calibration data. In parallel, a dedicated Geant4-based simulation framework was developed to model the secondary beam transport and detector response. Together, these experimental and computational resources provide a foundation for benchmarking hadronic interaction models and for future LArTPC-based antimatter searches, such as GRAMS. As an initial application of this framework, we compare the measured annihilation topologies, localized energy profiles, and spatial cluster multiplicities with predictions from three hadronic interaction generators: Fritiof, INCL++, and GiBUU. While all models reproduce the overall features of the total energy deposition, GiBUU provides the most consistent empirical description of localized energy deposition near the annihilation vertex and spatial cluster multiplicities at larger radii, reflecting the key roles of secondary nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption. These results provide a high-statistics experimental benchmark for modeling low-energy antiproton interactions in liquid argon.

</details>

Abstract: We report the first observation of the radiative decay $D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+} \gamma$ with a significance greater than 10 standard deviations. The signal is found in $e^{+}e^{-} \to c\bar{c}$ production using data samples with integrated luminosities of 980 and 572~$\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ collected by the Belle and Belle~II detectors operating at the KEKB and SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders, respectively. The branching-fraction ratio $\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+} \gamma]/\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{+} \gamma]$ is measured to be $0.74 \pm 0.05 ({\rm stat.}) \pm 0.06 ({\rm syst.)}$. This result helps discriminate between different theoretical models regarding the internal structure of the charm-strange meson $D_{s1}(2460)^{+}$.

### [54] [Hunting dark matter: the present and future of direct particle detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21052)
*Felix Kahlhoefer,Belina von Krosigk,Teresa Marrodán Undagoitia,Valentina Novati,María Luisa Sarsa*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review summarizes the current status and future directions of direct dark matter detection experiments, emphasizing theoretical interpretation frameworks, detector technologies, recent results (post-2020), next-generation projects, and key challenges—including low-threshold requirements, background suppression, and neutrino fog.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite decades of increasingly sensitive searches, no conclusive direct detection of Galactic halo dark matter has been achieved; a systematic review is needed to synthesize progress, assess technological and theoretical advances, and identify critical path challenges for discovery.

Method: Comprehensive literature review covering theoretical formalism (nuclear/electron scattering, collective excitations, absorption, time/direction dependence), experimental techniques (noble-liquid TPCs, cryogenic semiconductors, superconducting sensors), calibration and background mitigation strategies, and analysis of post-2020 results across recoil types, modulation, and directional signatures.

Result: No confirmed dark matter signal to date; leading constraints on WIMP and sub-GeV parameter spaces from experiments such as XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T, SuperCDMS, and SENSEI; emerging sensitivity to neutrino backgrounds ('neutrino fog') and improved limits on annual modulation and directionality.

Conclusion: Direct detection remains highly competitive and complementary across technologies; next-generation experiments must achieve ultra-low thresholds, superior background discrimination, and robust neutrino modeling to probe theoretically motivated dark matter models and resolve the 'neutrino fog' challenge.

Abstract: The nature of dark matter remains one of the major open questions in particle physics and cosmology. Direct detection experiments aim to identify dark matter particles through their interactions with ordinary matter, but despite substantial improvements in experimental sensitivity, no conclusive detection has been achieved. In this review, we provide a comprehensive overview of the present status and future prospects of direct particle detection, focusing on searches for dark matter particles from the Galactic halo. We introduce the theoretical framework for interpreting experimental results, including nuclear and electron scattering, collective excitations, particle absorption, and time- and direction-dependent signatures. We review the detection technologies employed in these searches, highlighting their operating principles, advantages, and limitations, as well as the calibration techniques and background mitigation strategies required to achieve high sensitivity. Particular emphasis is placed on experimental results reported since 2020, covering nuclear and electron recoil searches, annual modulation measurements, and directional detection. We also discuss experiments under construction and proposed future detectors, ranging from large-scale noble-liquid experiments to low-threshold technologies targeting sub-GeV dark matter. Finally, we examine the challenges that will shape the next generation of experiments, including the need for increasingly low energy thresholds, improved background understanding and discrimination, and the growing importance of neutrino-induced backgrounds. The diversity and complementarity of detector technologies will remain essential for exploring the dark matter parameter space and establishing the nature of any potential signal.

</details>


### [55] [Generating a simple silicon detector simulation with an open-weight large language model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21338)
*Charlie H R Bamber,Ming-Liang Wong*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that an LLM (Qwen3.6-27B, quantized) can generate statistically valid Geant4-based particle detector simulations when augmented with custom tools and resources via MCP servers—achieving ~42% success rate (95% CI: 29–56%) across 50 runs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs lack runtime execution capability and domain-specific knowledge (e.g., Geant4), limiting their use in physics simulation; the work aims to bridge this gap by integrating external tools and domain resources.

Method: Integrates a quantized Qwen3.6-27B LLM with MCP-served custom tools and domain resources to generate Geant4 detector simulation code from natural language prompts; evaluates output against a reference simulation using statistical consistency of energy-deposit distributions.

Result: 42% (95% CI: 29–56%) of 50 independent LLM-generated simulations produced energy-deposit distributions statistically consistent with the reference simulation.

Conclusion: Text-to-simulation is feasible for MC detector modeling using LLMs augmented with appropriate tooling and domain resources—constituting a proof of concept, though scalability to complex tasks requires richer examples and documentation.

Abstract: Recent LLM developments have seen AI-generated code become commonplace in the software development industry. Without tool access, LLMs cannot execute simulations, and their knowledge of toolkits such as Geant4 is limited. We demonstrate here, a detector simulation workflow with a Qwen3.6-27B model with Q4_K_M GGUF quantisation together with custom resources and tools served via MCP servers to generate a particle detector simulation. In 50 independent runs, 42(29--56)% at 95% CI produced energy-deposit distributions statistically consistent with a reference simulation, measured against a reference simulation. These results provide a proof of concept that MC simulations can be generated using text-based prompts with an LLM, given the necessary resources and tools. More extensive resources, such as example code and additional documentation, are likely to be required for more complex simulation tasks.

</details>


### [56] [Unraveling QCD dynamics via charm-tagged jets with ALICE at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21491)
*Emma Yeats*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents three novel D⁰-meson-tagged jet measurements—Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC), angular differences among jet axes, and jet angularities—to probe QCD radiation patterns from charm quarks, distinguishing perturbative vs. non-perturbative effects and testing Casimir-factor and dead-cone (mass-dependent) predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test QCD predictions on parton-shower dynamics—specifically the dependence of radiation patterns on parton color charge (via Casimir factors) and mass (via the dead-cone effect)—using heavy-flavor (charm) jets as calibrated probes.

Method: Experimental measurement of three jet substructure observables in D⁰-meson-tagged jets: (1) Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC); (2) angular separations between three distinct jet axis definitions (e.g., winner-take-all, soft-drop, standard anti-kₜ); (3) jet angularities τa = Σi z_i θ_i^a, with tunable exponent a to control sensitivity to wide-angle soft radiation.

Result: First-ever EEC measurement for D⁰-tagged jets; quantitative constraints on charm-quark shower evolution; differential sensitivity to soft/wide-angle radiation across axis definitions; angularities demonstrate clear separation between quark- and gluon-like radiation patterns, consistent with Casimir scaling and dead-cone suppression.

Conclusion: D⁰-tagged jet substructure provides a unique laboratory for precision QCD tests: these measurements validate theoretical expectations for massive quark showers, constrain hadronization models, and establish angularities and EEC as robust discriminators of parton identity and mass effects in jet formation.

Abstract: The study of jet substructure provides tests of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) and offers differential ways to study hadronization mechanisms. QCD predicts that jet radiation patterns depend on the mass and color charge of the initiating parton. Parton showers are sensitive to the different Casimir factors of quarks and gluons, and to the parton mass through the dead-cone effect. In this talk, we present three sets of $\textrm{D}^0$-meson-tagged jet measurements. First, we present the first measurement of the Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC) for $\textrm{D}^0$-meson-tagged jets. This observable reveals the detailed structure of QCD radiation from heavy quarks, allowing for a distinction between perturbative and non-perturbative effects in charm-quark showers. We then discuss measurements of the angular differences between three different axis definitions inside the jets. The three definitions offer different sensitivity to wide-angle soft radiation and systematically probe the radiation pattern from charm quarks. The final measurement regards jet angularities, which characterize the weighted angular distribution of the jet constituents. These powerful observables probe the parton shower, with sensitivity to parton-mass and Casimir-factor related effects that can be tuned by adjusting the importance of wide-angle radiation.

</details>

### [57] [HVP contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment in spacelike and timelike domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20143)
*A.V.Nesterenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment $a_\mu$ in both spacelike and timelike domains, reports results from dispersively-improved perturbation theory, and discusses their applicability to lattice QCD, the MUonE experiment, and related approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent and accurate assessment of the hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution to the muon anomalous magnetic moment $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$, crucial for resolving the longstanding $a_\mu$ discrepancy between Standard Model predictions and experimental measurements.

Method: Dispersively-improved perturbation theory applied to HVP contributions in both spacelike and timelike kinematic regimes; cross-validation with complementary methods including lattice QCD and the MUonE project.

Result: Numerical values of $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$ are reported with improved theoretical control in both spacelike and timelike domains, enabling more precise input for lattice computations and future high-precision experiments like MUonE.

Conclusion: The dispersively-improved perturbative evaluation provides reliable, domain-consistent HVP inputs for independent determinations of $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$, supporting progress toward resolving the $a_\mu$ anomaly.

Abstract: The hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ in spacelike and timelike domains are recapped. The values of $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ assessed within dispersively-improved perturbation theory are reported. The obtained results can be employed in the evaluations of $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ in the framework of such methods as lattice studies, MUonE project, and others.

</details>


### [58] [Doubly strange tetraquark as a $KK^*$ virtual state: quark-model study with harmonic traps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20588)
*Kai-Kai Zhang,Zi-Yang Lin,Yan-Ke Chen,Lu Meng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a quark-level method using an artificial harmonic trap and four-quark basis to extract hadron–hadron scattering observables (phase shifts, scattering lengths, effective ranges, pole positions) from trapped spectra; it validates the approach on DD* and BB*, and predicts a virtual state—not a bound state—for the doubly strange T_{ss} (I=0, J^P=1^+) near KK* threshold, motivating experimental searches at HIAF.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably extract hadron–hadron scattering information (especially near-threshold poles) directly from quark-model Hamiltonians, bypassing model-dependent assumptions in traditional hadronic approaches; to resolve discrepancies in prior predictions for the T_{ss} tetraquark and provide ab initio–informed guidance for experiment.

Method: Uses an artificial harmonic trap with a four-quark basis encompassing both meson–meson and compact diquark–antidiquark configurations; combines the Busch–Englert–Rzażewski–Wilkens formula with the effective range expansion (ERE) to map trapped energy levels to scattering observables; rigorously quantifies uncertainties from basis truncation, trap frequency, ERE order, and quark-model parameters (e.g., AL1 and modified AL1).

Result: DD* and BB* binding energies extracted from the trapped spectrum agree with direct calculations, validating the method; for T_{ss} (I=0, J^P=1^+), both AL1 and its modified version predict a KK* virtual-state pole 30–35 MeV below threshold (not a bound state), contradicting earlier bound-state claims.

Conclusion: The harmonic-trap + ERE framework provides a robust, uncertainty-quantified pathway from quark-level dynamics to hadronic scattering observables and pole positions; the T_{ss} virtual-state prediction calls for dedicated experimental searches at HIAF and suggests reinterpretation of tetraquark candidates near thresholds.

Abstract: We extract hadron--hadron scattering information from quark-level interactions using an artificial harmonic trap and a four-quark basis that includes both meson--meson and compact diquark--antidiquark configurations. Combining the Busch--Englert--Rzażewski--Wilkens formula with the effective range expansion (ERE), we determine phase shifts, scattering lengths, effective ranges, and pole positions from the trapped spectrum. We control uncertainties from basis truncation, finite trap frequencies, truncation of ERE, and quark-model parameters. The extracted $DD^*$ and $BB^*$ binding energies agree with direct calculations, providing benchmarks. For the doubly strange $T_{ss}$ with $I(J^P)=0(1^+)$, we predict a $KK^*$ virtual-state pole about 30--35~MeV below threshold in the stable-$K^*$ approximation. Both the AL1 model and its modified version that better reproduces the measured $T_{cc}^+$ binding energy yield this result. This finding differs from earlier predictions of a bound state and motivates searches for the $T_{ss}$ at facilities such as the High Intensity heavy Ion Accelerator Facility (HIAF). This framework could be extended to other hadron-hadron channels to extract scattering observables and pole positions.

</details>


### [59] [Muon and Tau Anomalies and $Δα_{had}^{(5)} (M_Z^2)$ in the Standard Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20762)
*Stephan Narison*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the Standard Model (SM) prediction of the muon anomalous magnetic moment (a_μ), focusing on improved hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contributions using recent e+e− data (2π, 3π, inclusive R^ee), QCD corrections (HO PT up to α_s^5, quark mass and condensate effects), and additional γγ channels. It reports a_LO^hvp = (7094 ± 37)×10⁻¹¹, leading to Δa_μ^SM ≈ (84.9–90.0 ± 21.3_th ± 14.5_exp)×10⁻¹¹ — a ~2.1–3.5σ discrepancy with experiment — consistent with BMW20 lattice but highlighting tensions among other lattice results. It also updates a_τ^hvp and Δα_had^(5)(M_Z²).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent tension between SM predictions and experimental measurement of the muon g−2, particularly by improving the dominant low-energy hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution using updated e+e− data and refined QCD theory inputs, and to assess implications for new physics.

Method: Combines recent e+e− cross-section data (2π, 3π, inclusive R^ee) from PDG and CMD3, augmented with π⁰γ/ηγ contributions; includes high-order perturbative QCD (up to α_s^5), quark mass corrections (m_q² up to α_s³, m_s⁴ up to α_s²), and QCD condensates; evaluates LO and HO HVP, then combines with QED/EW contributions; compares with lattice QCD results (e.g., BMW20) and assesses systematic consistency.

Result: a_μ^hvp,LO = (7094 ± 37)×10⁻¹¹; Δa_μ^SM = (84.9[90.0] ± 21.3_th ± 14.5_exp)×10⁻¹¹ — indicating 2.1–3.5σ deviation from experiment; a_τ^hvp,LO = (3516 ± 25)×10⁻⁹; Δα_had^(5)(M_Z²) = (2770.7 ± 4.5)×10⁻⁵; agreement with BMW20 lattice but tension among other lattice estimates.

Conclusion: The updated SM prediction reinforces a statistically significant (~2–3.5σ) discrepancy in a_μ, sustaining motivation for new physics; however, inconsistencies among lattice HVP determinations warrant caution in global averages (e.g., WP25); future experiments (MUonE, J-PARC E34) and improved lattice calculations are critical for resolution.

Abstract: I review the present status of the Standard Model (SM) determination of the muon anomaly by emphasizing the estimate of the lowest hadronic vacuum polarization contributions using recent $e^+e^-$ data on $2\pi$ and $3\pi$ and the inclusive ratio $R^{ee}$ of total cross-sections compiled by PDG to which we add the QCD continuum including HO PT QCD up to $\alpha_s^5$, quark mass $m_q^2$ up to $\alpha_s^3$, $m_s^4$ up to $\alpha_s^2$ and QCD condensates. Adding the $e^+e^-\to \pi^0\gamma + \eta\gamma$ contributions to the CMD3 data, one obtains\,: $a_\mu\vert^{hvp}_{lo}=(7094\pm 37)\times 10^{-11}$. Combining this LO result with the HO estimate of the hadronic, QED and EW contributions, one obtains\,: $\Delta a_\mu^{sm} \equiv (a_\mu^{exp}-a_\mu^{sm})= (86\pm 38_{th}\pm 14.5_{exp})\times 10^{-11} $. The average of the recent estimates quoted in Table\,3 [$\oplus$ attempt to fit all $\,e^+e^-$ data] gives\,: $\Delta a_\mu^{sm} = (84.9[90.0]\pm 21.3_{th}\pm 14.5_{exp})\times 10^{-11}$\,. These results agree with the precise BMW20 lattice one and still suggest a window for new physics beyond the SM of $(2.1\sim 3.5)\,\sigma$. Besides some future projects for improving the $e^+e^-$ data, the MUonE for extracting directly $a_\mu^{tot}\vert^{hvp}_{lo}$ and J-PARC (E34) for an independent measurement of $a_\mu$, it is important to improve the Lattice results shown in Fig.\,9 which (at first sight) disagree each others despite the large errors and may question the reliability of the {\it averages of different averages} used in the White Paper (WP25) Our analysis is completed by updating the estimate of the hadronic contribution to the tau anomaly\,: $a_\tau\vert^{hvp}_{lo}=(3516\pm 25)\times 10^{-9}$ and to $\alpha(M_Z)\,: \,\Delta \alpha_{had}^{(5)} (M_Z^2)= (2770.7\pm 4.5)\times 10^{-5}. $

</details>


### [60] [Heavy double-gluon quarkonium and $\overline{b}ggc$ mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21380)
*G. Daylan Esmer,B. Barsbay,K. Azizi,H. Sundu,S. Türkmen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses QCD sum rules to predict masses and couplings of heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons (cGGc, bGGb, bGGc) with specific J^PC quantum numbers, finding mass ranges of 6.2–6.7 GeV, 12.0–12.2 GeV, and 9.2–9.6 GeV respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically predict the spectroscopic properties of heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons — exotic hadrons containing a heavy quark-antiquark pair plus two valence gluons — in anticipation of experimental searches and to support interpretation of future data in the heavy-quark sector.

Method: Two-point QCD sum rule method; operator product expansion (OPE) up to dimension twelve; Borel analysis with constraints ensuring dominant pole contribution and OPE convergence; current construction for allowed J^PC/J^P channels (excluding identically vanishing ones: 0^{+-}, 0^{--}, 1^{++}, 1^{-+}).

Result: Predicted mass ranges: ~6.2–6.7 GeV for charmonium-like cGGc, ~12.0–12.2 GeV for bottomonium-like bGGb, and ~9.2–9.6 GeV for open-flavor bGGc hybrids; corresponding current couplings extracted for all non-vanishing channels.

Conclusion: The computed masses and couplings provide quantitative benchmarks for studying strong decays of double-gluon hybrids and for guiding experimental identification of such exotic states in heavy-quark experiments.

Abstract: We investigate heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons, built from a heavy quark-antiquark pair and two valence gluons, using the two-point QCD sum rule method. The charmonium-like $\bar{c}GGc$ and bottomonium-like $\bar{b}GGb$ states are considered with $J^{PC}=0^{\pm\pm}$ and $1^{\pm\pm}$, whereas the open-flavor $\bar{b}GGc$ states are classified by $J^{P}=0^{\pm}$ and $1^{\pm}$. The currents with $J^{PC}=0^{+-}, 0^{--}, 1^{++}$, and $1^{-+}$ vanish identically, so sum rules are derived for the remaining four channels. The operator product expansion is carried out up to dimension twelve, and the Borel parameter and continuum threshold are fixed so that the pole contribution dominates and the OPE converges. The resulting masses lie in the ranges $M\simeq 6.2$--$6.7$~GeV for $\bar{c}GGc$, $M\simeq 12.0$--$12.2$~GeV for $\bar{b}GGb$, and $M\simeq 9.2$--$9.6$~GeV for $\bar{b}GGc$, and the current couplings are obtained for all channels. These predictions provide a basis for analyzing the strong decays of double-gluon hybrids and for interpreting future experimental data in the heavy-quark sector.

</details>


### [61] [Probing the QCD Conformal Window with Four-Quark Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18828)
*Junchen Rong,Emmanuel Stamou,Tom Steudtner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes high-order anomalous dimensions of four-quark and gluonic operators in Veneziano QCD to probe infrared dynamics and the conformal window; no evidence is found for a composite operator merging with the QCD fixed point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the infrared fate of QCD-like theories and locate their conformal window—critical for understanding phase transitions between conformal and chiral symmetry-breaking phases—via classically irrelevant but IR-relevant deformations.

Method: Four-loop computation of anomalous dimensions for all four-quark operators (with/without chiral symmetry) and three-loop computation for the dimension-six gluonic operator in Veneziano QCD; systematic conformal expansion plus resummation techniques with uncertainty estimation.

Result: High-precision anomalous dimensions obtained; no IR-relevant composite operator is identified that could drive or signal a merger with the QCD fixed point.

Conclusion: The absence of such an operator weakens the hypothesis of a smooth conformal-to-QCD transition via this mechanism, suggesting either a sharper transition or alternative IR dynamics.

Abstract: The infrared fate of QCD-like theories and their conformal window for different numbers or flavours and colour remains an open question with applications ranging from high-energy to condensed-matter systems. A hallmark of phase transitions from a conformal to symmetry breaking phase can be the presence of classically irrelevant operators that provide relevant deformation in the infrared. We investigate this scenario by computing anomalous dimensions of all four-quark operators, with or without chiral symmetry, at four-loop order as well as the dimension-six gluonic operator at three loops in Veneziano QCD. Scaling dimensions are derived in a systematic conformal expansion. These results are improved with various resummation techniques and we estimate their uncertainties. We find no concrete evidence for a composite operator effectively describing a merger with the QCD fixed point.

</details>


### [62] [Black Hole Superradiance and the Gravitational Auger Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18833)
*Joshua W. Foster,Nicholas L. Rodd*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies gravitational interactions within bosonic clouds around spinning black holes—acting analogously to the Auger effect—as a dominant, previously neglected mechanism that modifies superradiant level evolution, especially for QCD axions; it suppresses continuous gravitational-wave signals and alters spin-down constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To account for irreducible gravitational interactions in black hole superradiance, which have been overlooked despite their potential to dominate over self-interactions (e.g., λφ⁴) and significantly impact observational signatures.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical treatment of gravitational backreaction within the superradiant cloud, including scalar emission/absorption by the black hole, level scattering (including radial overtones), and time-evolution of occupation numbers across multiple bound states.

Result: Gravitational Auger-like processes rapidly deplete the first superradiant level post-saturation for QCD axions; higher-level growth is truncated; continuous GW emission is strongly suppressed; spin-down via higher modes starts earlier but proceeds slower, invalidating prior observational bounds.

Conclusion: Purely gravitational couplings fundamentally reshape superradiance dynamics—rendering many existing phenomenological predictions (e.g., CW signals, binary inspiral signatures) overly optimistic—and necessitate revised astrophysical constraints on ultralight bosons.

Abstract: Black hole superradiance is a powerful probe of nature's light degrees of freedom. In the vicinity of a spinning black hole, it exponentially amplifies ultralight bosons from vacuum fluctuations to a macroscopic cloud of enormous occupation number. Observational superradiance effects - modified black-hole spins, gravitational-wave and coherent boson emission - are known to depend sensitively on bosonic self-interactions as induced by a quartic, $\lambda \phi^4$. Here we point out that purely gravitational interactions within the cloud significantly modify superradiance dynamics, through an analog of the Auger effect. Moreover, we identify nontrivial evolution of the superradiant levels beyond the first, driven by scattering into even higher levels and their radial overtones. For the QCD axion, the irreducible gravitational coupling rapidly dissipates the first superradiant level after it reaches saturation, through scalar emission and absorption by the black hole, while truncating the growth of higher levels. This can dramatically suppress the observability of continuous gravitational-wave signals or signatures in late binary inspirals. Black hole spin-down remains more robust, but for higher modes begins earlier and proceeds more slowly, requiring revision of existing limits.

</details>


### [63] [$K_1^*(1870)$ and $3S$-$2D$ mixing in the strange meson sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19498)
*Ting-Yan Li,Zi-Yue Bai,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the newly observed $K_1^*(1870)$ resonance ($J^P = 1^-$) in the $K\eta'$ system, proposing it as the lower mass eigenstate of $3S$–$2D$ mixing in the kaon meson family; predicts its higher-mass partner; computes OZI-allowed decays via the quark pair creation model—finding agreement with measured width—and suggests $Kp$ reactions for experimental confirmation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the newly observed $K_1^*(1870)$ resonance—first reported by BESIII in the $K\eta'$ channel—and determine whether it fits within the conventional kaon meson spectrum, particularly addressing its quantum numbers and decay anomalies.

Method: Employs $3S$–$2D$ mixing in the kaon meson family to assign $J^P = 1^-$ states; uses the quark pair creation (QPC) model to compute OZI-allowed strong decays; analyzes production feasibility via $Kp$ reactions.

Result: The $K_1^*(1870)$ is successfully described as the lower mass eigenstate of $3S$–$2D$ mixing, with predicted mass matching observation; its predicted higher-mass partner is identified; total decay width from QPC calculation agrees with experimental width; however, the calculated subordinate $K\eta'$ branching fraction contradicts its observed dominance in that channel—indicating tension or need for refined modeling; $Kp$ reaction is proposed as a viable production mechanism.

Conclusion: The $K_1^*(1870)$ is plausibly interpreted as the lower $3S$–$2D$ mixed $1^-$ kaon state, but its anomalous $K\eta'$ decay strength remains unexplained within current QPC predictions, urging theoretical refinement and dedicated experimental study—especially via $Kp$ collisions.

Abstract: Very recently, the BESIII Collaboration reported the first observation of a $J^P=1^-$ strange resonance in the $K\eta'$ system, the $K_1^*(1870)$. We examine whether this state can be accommodated in the kaon meson family. Although it cannot be assigned as a pure $3^3S_1$ or $2^3D_1$ state, we show that it can be described as the lower state of $3S$-$2D$ mixing, whose mass agrees with the measured value, and we predict its higher-mass partner. Using the quark pair creation model, we calculate the OZI-allowed decay properties of both \textcolor{black}{mixed} states; the total decay width of the lower state is consistent with the measured width of the $K_1^*(1870)$. We note that the subordinate $K\eta'$ decay mode of this state appears to contradict its observed $K\eta'$ decay channel, which calls for further study. We further investigate the production of these two states via the $Kp$ reaction, which may provide valuable information for future experimental searches.

</details>


### [64] [Electromagnetic cross section of charmonium decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19654)
*X.H.Mo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates electromagnetic cross sections for charmonium decays into 15 final states in e+e- colliders using a general lS-coupling scheme to construct covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes for two-body decays with arbitrary integer spins; decay rates are converted to collision cross sections, and examples validate the formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, covariant framework for evaluating electromagnetic cross sections of charmonium decays—key observables in e+e- colliders—especially for diverse final states involving particles with arbitrary integer spins.

Method: Adopts a general lS-coupling analysis to construct Lorentz-covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes for two-body charmonium decays; derives decay rates from these amplitudes and converts them to electromagnetic cross sections using standard collider kinematics and flux factors.

Result: Electromagnetic cross sections for fifteen charmonium decay final states are explicitly evaluated; general formulas are validated through illustrative numerical/examples demonstrating applicability across spin configurations.

Conclusion: The lS-coupling-based covariant helicity-amplitude formalism provides a robust, unified method for computing electromagnetic cross sections of charmonium decays in e+e- collisions, facilitating precision tests of QCD and charmonium spectroscopy.

Abstract: As an important measurable quantity, the electromagnetic cross sections of charmonium decay in an e+e- collider are valuated for fifteen final states. A general scheme of lS-coupling analysis is adopted for constructing covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes involving two-body decays with arbitrary integer spins. Based on the decay rate, the collision cross section is obtained finally. A number of illustrative examples is worked out to expound and incarnate the general formulas.

</details>


### [65] [Charm quark energy loss fluctuations in clumpy color string medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19789)
*Daria Prokhorova,Shuzhe Shi,Evgeny Andronov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates charm quark energy loss in proton-proton collisions by modeling the medium as overlapping color flux tubes (string clusters), converting local energy density to an effective gluon-gas temperature, and finding radiative energy loss dominates over collisional loss by ~10×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand medium-induced charm quark quenching in small systems (pp collisions), where conventional QGP formation is questionable, yet collective and jet-quenching-like effects have been observed — testing whether string clustering can generate sufficient local energy density gradients to drive significant energy loss.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of charm quark propagation through an ensemble of color flux tubes; modeling string overlap as localized clusters; mapping superposed chromoelectric field energy density to an effective temperature of an ideal gluon gas; computing both elastic (collisional) and inelastic (radiative) energy loss using multiple prescriptions for energy-density extraction.

Result: Radiative energy loss reaches up to ~1.5 GeV, while collisional loss is limited to ~0.15 GeV; both mechanisms exhibit sensitivity to spatial inhomogeneities of the local energy density; string clustering generates a highly non-uniform energy density landscape that predominantly governs charm quenching.

Conclusion: Spatial overlap of color flux tubes—i.e., string clustering—is sufficient to produce significant, inhomogeneous energy density patterns in pp collisions, enabling medium-like charm quark energy loss dominated by radiation, suggesting quenching need not require a thermalized QGP.

Abstract: We simulate charm quark propagation through an ensemble of color flux tubes formed between receding color charges in proton-proton collisions. Under the assumption of sharp string boundaries and a localized nature of the overlap regions, high flux tube densities lead to the formation of string clusters. We convert the local energy density of the superposed color field into an effective temperature of an ideal gluon gas and study the dependence of charm energy loss on its spatial inhomogeneities. By comparing different energy-density extraction prescriptions, we show that both elastic and inelastic momentum damping mechanisms are sensitive to the medium properties. However, the collisional channel is an order of magnitude smaller in absolute terms (up to $\sim0.15$~GeV) compared to the radiative loss (up to $\sim1.5$~GeV). Overall, the results demonstrate that the spatial overlap of individual strings generates a complex local energy density pattern, which predominantly governs the medium-induced quenching of charm quarks.

</details>


### [66] [Quark + diquark description of nucleon gravitational form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19819)
*Peng Cheng,Zhen-Ni Xu,Dong-Yan Fu,Chen Chen,Zhao-Qian Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a Poincaré-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation to compute nucleon gravitational form factors (GFFs) — A, J, D, and c̄ — without adjustable parameters, decomposing them into quark/gluon and scalar/axial-vector diquark contributions; it finds scalar diquarks dominate mechanical properties, B(Q²) is tiny but nonzero due to internal cancellations, c̄ satisfies symmetry constraints, D-term and radii are smaller than lattice QCD due to missing meson cloud effects (especially on gluons), and negative central energy density is model-dependent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide parameter-free, ab initio predictions of nucleon gravitational form factors within a relativistic bound-state framework, enabling quantitative comparison with lattice QCD and insight into the roles of diquark structure, quark-gluon separation, and meson cloud effects.

Method: Solves the Poincaré-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation to compute the full set of nucleon GFFs (A, J, D, B, c̄); performs species decomposition (quark vs. gluon) and diquark-type decomposition (scalar vs. axial-vector); employs two extrapolation schemes for energy density reconstruction; compares results quantitatively with lattice QCD data.

Result: Predicted GFFs satisfy fundamental symmetries (e.g., c̄(Q²) behavior); scalar diquark dominates A, J, D; axial-vector diquark is essential for consistency; B(Q²) is numerically tiny but nonzero due to precise cancellations across diquark and flavor sectors; D-term and spatial radii are systematically smaller than lQCD—attributed to gluon-sector sensitivity to omitted meson cloud; energy density sign at origin depends strongly on extrapolation scheme.

Conclusion: The quark+diquark Faddeev approach yields qualitatively and semi-quantitatively reliable GFFs without free parameters, revealing key structural insights—especially the critical interplay between diquark types and the dominant impact of meson cloud on gluonic mechanical properties—while highlighting limitations in modeling long-range physics affecting the gluon distribution and central energy density.

Abstract: We present parameter-free predictions for the nucleon gravitational form factors (GFFs) using a Poincare-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation. We provide the full GFFs ($A$, $J$, $D$, and $\bar{c}$) along with their species decompositions into quark and gluon contributions. Based on the diquark breakdown, we find that while the scalar diquark component dominates the nucleon's energy, spin, and mechanical properties, the axial-vector component plays an indispensable role. The GFF $B(Q^2)$ is predicted to be vanishingly small but nonvanishing at finite $Q^2$; its physical origin is deeply rooted in the nucleon' s internal structure through cancellations in both the diquark content and the $u/d$ flavor sectors. Furthermore, our results for $\bar{c}(Q^2)$ confirm the fundamental symmetry requirements of the approach. Compared with lQCD, our calculated $D$-term and various spatial radii are somewhat smaller in magnitude. This systematic underestimation is primarily driven by the gluon sector and can be attributed to the omission of meson cloud effects, suggesting that these effects impact the gluon distribution more significantly than the quark distribution. Finally, our analysis of the energy density based on two extrapolation schemes reveals that the presence of a negative central energy density is highly model-dependent.

</details>


### [67] [Exploring X17 Couplings through Meson Dalitz Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19831)
*Xuan Wang,Tianhong Wang,SiQi Zhang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the X17 boson hypothesis using meson Dalitz decays, moving beyond pure vector coupling to include axial-vector couplings via the Bethe–Salpeter formalism; it identifies viable charm/strange quark coupling regions (~10⁻³), finds four degenerate best-fit solutions, and predicts a significantly enhanced D*⁺ → D⁺e⁺e⁻ decay ratio relative to the SM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve tension between the X17 interpretation from ATOMKI nuclear anomalies and existing constraints from D*⁰→D⁰e⁺e⁻ and D_s*⁺→D_s⁺e⁺e⁻ measurements, by generalizing the X17 interaction to include both vector and axial-vector couplings to quarks.

Method: Employs the Bethe–Salpeter approach to compute hadronic transition matrix elements for meson Dalitz decays (D* → De⁺e⁻); fits X17 quark couplings (g_q^V, g_q^A for q = c, s) using u/d couplings fixed from prior analyses; performs global fit to experimental data including nuclear transitions and meson decay measurements.

Result: Identifies allowed parameter space requiring |g_{c,s}^A| ∼ 10⁻³; finds four degenerate best-fit solutions, e.g., (g_c^V,g_c^A,g_s^V,g_s^A) = (8.32,1.09,−2.62,1.36)×10⁻³; predicts R^th(D*⁺→D⁺e⁺e⁻) substantially larger than the Standard Model expectation.

Conclusion: The axial-vector coupling component is essential to reconcile X17 with meson decay data; the predicted enhancement in D*⁺→D⁺e⁺e⁻ provides a distinctive, testable signature at current and future flavor experiments.

Abstract: The ATOMKI collaboration has reported anomalous $e^+e^-$ pair production in a series of nuclear transitions, which has been interpreted as a possible signature of a new light boson, X17. Meson Dalitz decays provide a complementary probe of the X17 interaction structure. However, the widely studied vector coupling scenario is in tension with the combined constraints from existing $D^{*0}\to D^0e^+e^-$, $D_s^{*+}\to D_s^+e^+e^-$ measurements and nuclear transition analyses. In this work, we investigate the X17 contributions to meson Dalitz decays within a framework incorporating both vector and axial-vector couplings, employing the Bethe--Salpeter approach to calculate the hadronic transition matrix elements. With $u$ and $d$ quark couplings taken from previous analyses, we identify allowed regions for the charm and strange quark couplings, which require the axial-vector coupling strength $|g_{c,s}^A|\sim10^{-3}$. We obtain four degenerate best-fit solutions, one of which is $(g_c^V,g_c^A,g_s^V,g_s^A)=(8.32,1.09,-2.62,1.36)\times10^{-3}$, and estimate the decay ratio $R^{\mathrm{th}}(D^{*+}\to D^+e^+e^-)$, which significantly exceeds the Standard Model prediction.

</details>


### [68] [The Future Circular Collider as a photon-photon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20085)
*Patricia Rebello Teles,David d'Enterria*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores photon-photon interactions at the CERN Future Circular Collider (FCC) across all operational modes (e+e−, pp, pPb, PbPb), leveraging equivalent photon approximation to access broad two-photon invariant mass ranges; it highlights exclusive C-even resonance production — especially Higgs boson, axion-like particles (ALPs), and graviton-like particles (GLPs) — with projected sensitivity surpassing current limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the high-luminosity equivalent photon fluxes from relativistic charged beams at FCC to enable novel two-photon physics, particularly exclusive production of C-even resonances inaccessible or poorly constrained in current experiments.

Method: Uses the equivalent photon approximation (EPA) to model electromagnetic fields as quasireal photon fluxes; computes accessible two-photon invariant mass ranges and production cross sections for exclusive γγ → X → γγ processes across FCC-ee (e+e−), FCC-hh (pp/pPb/PbPb) configurations; performs sensitivity projections for SM Higgs, ALPs, and GLPs.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of observing photon-fusion Higgs production in all FCC collision modes; projects ALP and GLP sensitivity up to kinematic limits: ~365 GeV at FCC-ee and multi-TeV at FCC-hh — significantly improving over LHC and LEP constraints.

Conclusion: The FCC’s diverse photon-photon programme offers unprecedented reach for probing fundamental scalar and tensor resonances via γγ fusion, establishing it as a unique facility for BSM Higgs-sector and quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: The CERN Future Circular Collider (FCC) offers a unique programme of photon-photon interactions in electron-positron, proton-proton, proton-lead, and lead-lead collisions, by exploiting the high-luminosity equivalent photon fluxes associated with the charged beams. Within the equivalent photon approximation, the electromagnetic fields of relativistic charged particles are described in terms of fluxes of quasireal photons. The complementary photon fluxes, energies, and virtualities available across the FCC operation modes provide access to a broad range of two-photon invariant masses and associated physics measurements. Physics opportunities open to study are highlighted, focusing on the exclusive production of C-even resonances, including the SM Higgs boson and searches for spin-0 axion-like (ALPs) and spin-2 graviton-like (GLPs) particles decaying back into photon pairs. These prospective studies demonstrate the potential to observe photon-fusion production of the Higgs boson for all colliding modes, as well as sensitivity to photon-coupled ALPs and GLPs with masses extending up to the kinematic reach of FCC-ee and into the multi-TeV range at FCC-hh collisions, with significant improvements over existing constraints.

</details>


### [69] [Strong Decays of the $2^-$ Heavy-Light Mesons in the Relativistic Bethe-Salpeter Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20380)
*Jie Yu,Wan-Li Ju,Qiang Li,Tianhong Wang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper conducts a relativistic analysis of heavy-light $J^P = 2^-$ mesons (e.g., $D_2$, $B_2$, and their strange counterparts), computing their mass spectra and $^1D_2$–$^3D_2$ mixing angles via the instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter (Salpeter) equation with $J^P$-based wave functions; strong decays are calculated using PCAC + low-energy theorem for $H\to H'P$ and chiral Lagrangians for $H\to H'V$, yielding total widths from summed OZI-allowed partial widths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent relativistic description of $J^P = 2^-$ heavy-light mesons—including mass splittings, $D$-wave mixing, and strong decays—beyond non-relativistic approximations, addressing limitations of spectroscopic notation and improving predictive power for decay widths and mixing angles.

Method: Solves the full instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter (Salpeter) equation with wave functions explicitly constructed for $J^P = 2^-$, avoiding $^{2S+1}L_J$ notation; computes strong decay amplitudes using PCAC + low-energy theorem for pseudoscalar emission ($H\to H'P$) and effective chiral Lagrangian for vector emission ($H\to H'V$); sums OZI-allowed partial widths to obtain total widths.

Result: Predicted mass spectra and $^1D_2$–$^3D_2$ mixing angles for $D_2$, $D'_2$, $D_{s2}$, $D'_{s2}$, $\bar{B}_2$, $\bar{B}'_2$, $\bar{B}_{s2}$, $\bar{B}'_{s2}$; quantitative estimates of total widths and branching fractions for all OZI-allowed two-body strong decays ($H\to H'P$ and $H\to H'V$).

Conclusion: The relativistic Salpeter framework successfully describes $2^-$ heavy-light meson properties, revealing significant $^1D_2$–$^3D_2$ mixing and enabling robust predictions for masses and strong decays—validating the use of $J^P$-based wave functions and demonstrating the importance of chiral and PCAC constraints in decay modeling.

Abstract: We present a relativistic study of the mass spectra, the $^1D_2$--$^3D_2$ mixing angles, and the OZI-allowed two-body strong decays of the heavy-light $J^P=2^-$ mesons $D_2$, $D'_2$, $D_{s2}$, $D'_{s2}$, $\bar{B}_2$, $\bar{B}'_2$, $\bar{B}_{s2}$, and $\bar{B}'_{s2}$. The mass spectrum and the meson wave functions are obtained by solving the full instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter (Salpeter) equation, in which the wave-function representations are constructed directly in terms of the $J^P$ quantum numbers rather than the non-relativistic spectroscopic notation ${}^{2S+1}L_J$. For the decay amplitudes we use the partial conservation of the axial-vector current (PCAC) together with the low-energy theorem for the $H\to H'P$ channels, so that the light pseudoscalar meson enters only through its decay constant, and the effective chiral Lagrangian for the $H\to H'V$ channels. The total widths of these mesons are estimated by summing over the partial widths of all OZI-allowed strong decay channels.

</details>


### [70] [Lean-Verified No-Go Theorems for $R$-Symmetries and Modular Symmetries in 4D GUTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20386)
*Mu-Chun Chen,Xiang-Gan Liu,Michael Ratz,Alexander Stewart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper formalizes and strengthens a no-go theorem using Lean 4: discrete Z_M^R (M ≥ 3) or continuous U(1)_R symmetries cannot be preserved during spontaneous SU(5) GUT breaking to the MSSM with finitely many chiral multiplets; it further shows modular symmetries in supergravity are inherently R-symmetries, implying light exotic states near critical moduli points that threaten gauge coupling unification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously verify, generalize, and repair a known no-go theorem on R-symmetry preservation in 4D supersymmetric SU(5) GUTs, and to clarify the role and consequences of modular symmetries as R-symmetries in supergravity-embedded GUTs.

Method: Formal proof verification using the Lean 4 theorem prover; mathematical analysis of symmetry breaking patterns, representation theory of SU(5), and modular transformations in supergravity; identification and correction of a gap in the original published argument.

Result: A fully formalized, gap-free proof of the R-symmetry no-go theorem for SU(5) → MSSM breaking; demonstration that modular symmetries in supergravity are necessarily R-symmetries; derivation that discrete R-symmetries linearly realized at critical moduli points force massless or parametrically light exotic states, endangering gauge coupling unification.

Conclusion: The no-go theorem is robust and generalizable; its implications extend to modular GUT constructions, where preserving R-symmetries at critical points generically introduces light exotics incompatible with precision unification—thus constraining viable model-building avenues.

Abstract: $R$-symmetries are instrumental in supersymmetric model building. However, a no-go theorem restricts us from using them in a large class of four-dimensional (4D) grand unified theories (GUTs). Specifically, it has been shown that a supersymmetric 4D GUT based on SU(5), with finitely many chiral multiplets, cannot be broken spontaneously to the exact minimal supersymmetric standard model (MSSM) while preserving a discrete $\mathbb{Z}_M^R$, with $M \geq 3$, or continuous U(1) $R$-symmetry. We use Lean 4 to formalize this proof. In the process, we identify and close a gap in the general case of the published argument. Further, we discuss the implications for GUT models with modular symmetries. We point out that modular symmetries are inevitably $R$-symmetries in a supersymmetric framework that also describes gravity. At certain critical points in moduli space, discrete $R$-symmetries are linearly realized. In the context of 4D GUTs, the no-go theorem then implies that exotic states become massless at these points, provided the GUT-breaking vacuum preserves these symmetries. In the vicinity of these critical points, these exotics are parametrically light, which may spoil gauge coupling unification.

</details>


### [71] [General Formulation of Covariant Tensor Amplitudes for Purely Hadronic and Single-photon Radiative Cascade Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20482)
*Yu-chang Sun,Yu-xuan Hu,Chu-cheng Pan,Hao Cai,Liang Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified, Lorentz-covariant formalism for constructing partial-wave amplitudes in multi-body hadronic and single-photon radiative cascade decays, ensuring rigorous compliance with Rarita-Schwinger constraints and photon gauge invariance via structured tensor decomposition and projection operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unphysical longitudinal and trace contaminations in cascade decay amplitudes induced by Lorentz boosts, and to enforce strict photon gauge invariance in radiative decays—both critical for precision amplitude analyses in experimental hadron physics (e.g., at BESIII, LHCb, Belle II).

Method: Develops a covariant $L$-$S$ tensor formalism featuring: (i) structural decoupling of polarization wave functions, coupling parameters, and core amplitude tensor $U$; (ii) hierarchical vertex contraction with intermediate-spin ($P^{(J_i)}(p_i)$) and parent-frame spin ($P^{(S_{BC})}(p_A)$) projection operators; (iii) gauge-invariant radiative amplitude construction using fundamental transverse tensors $K_{\mu\nu}$, $S_{\mu\nu}$, and systematic $g_{\mu\nu} \to q_\mu p_{A\nu}$ replacement; (iv) exact matching of Blatt-Weisskopf orbital ranks $B_{L_{\text{rad}}}$ and spectator-index pass-through for photon polarization.

Result: A fully Lorentz-covariant, gauge-invariant amplitude framework that guarantees Rarita-Schwinger symmetry, tracelessness, and transversality across all reference frames; eliminates boost-induced unphysical degrees of freedom; provides universal recursive formulas for radiative vertices compatible with standard centrifugal barrier modeling.

Conclusion: The formalism establishes a robust, first-principles foundation for high-precision partial-wave analyses of complex hadronic cascades and radiative transitions, enabling consistent treatment of spin, angular momentum, and gauge structure without frame-dependent artifacts.

Abstract: We present a unified method for generating Lorentz-covariant partial-wave amplitudes for multi-body purely hadronic and single-photon radiative cascade decays within the covariant $L$-$S$ tensor formalism. Based on the structural decoupling of external polarization wave functions, complex coupling parameters, and the core covariant amplitude tensor $U$, we establish a hierarchical vertex contraction procedure that explicitly incorporates intermediate-state cascade bridge projection operators $P^{(J_i)}(p_i)$ and parent-frame definite-spin projection operators $P^{(S_{BC})}(p_A)$. This rigorous treatment guarantees the Rarita-Schwinger symmetry, tracelessness, and transversality conditions across different reference frames, fundamentally eliminating the unphysical longitudinal and trace contaminations induced by Lorentz boosts in cascade decays. Furthermore, we formulate a general covariant tensor representation for single-photon radiative decays $A(J_M) \to \gamma + X(J_X)$ that strictly satisfies photon gauge invariance ($q^\lambda V_{\dots\lambda\dots} = 0$) in the Lorenz gauge. Built upon normal- and anomalous-parity fundamental transverse tensors ($K_{\mu\nu}$ and $S_{\mu\nu}$), universal recursive formulas are derived via the systematic replacement $g_{\mu\nu} \to q_\mu p_{A\nu}$, accompanied by exact rules for matching the orbital angular momentum ranks of Blatt-Weisskopf centrifugal barrier factors $B_{L_{\text{rad}}}$ and a spectator index pass-through mechanism for the photon polarization index.

</details>


### [72] [Colours and Flavours: ropes and colour reconnection as string multiplets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20705)
*Christian Bierlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a string-based hadronization model incorporating colour correlations beyond leading-colour approximation to explain enhanced strange-hadron and charm-baryon yields in high-activity hadronic collisions; it unifies junction formation and rope hadronization via SU(3) colour structure, introduces local escape and saturation mechanisms for nuclear collisions, and achieves good agreement with light-flavour data in pp and p–Pb, while identifying a persistent Ξ_c production deficit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the experimentally observed increase of strange-hadron yields with event activity and enhanced charm-baryon fractions—particularly in hadronic and nuclear collisions—by probing the microscopic role of non-perturbative colour dynamics in hadronization.

Method: Extends the Lund string fragmentation model by incorporating subleading-colour effects, junction topology, rope formation, and colour screening; introduces a local escape criterion and phenomenological saturation of the string-breaking force for nuclear collisions, grounded in SU(3) colour algebra and multi-string (e.g., three-string) configurations.

Result: The unified model successfully describes light-flavour hadron ratios (e.g., K/π, p/π, Λ/K⁰ₛ) across pp and p–Pb collisions using common parameters; predicts O–O collision outcomes; reveals a significant underprediction of Ξ_c baryons in charm ratios, indicating limitations in current fragmentation baseline or its activity dependence.

Conclusion: Colour correlations beyond leading-colour approximation—manifested through junctions, ropes, and screening—are essential to describe strangeness and baryon enhancement; the Ξ_c deficit signals the need for refined treatment of charm-baryon formation, possibly involving explicit diquark dynamics or modified fragmentation functions.

Abstract: The increase of strange-hadron yields with event activity and the enhanced charm-baryon fractions in hadronic collisions motivate a microscopic examination of how the colour environment affects hadronization. We review and develop the string description of these effects, starting from the Lund fragmentation baseline in electron--positron annihilation and following colour correlations beyond the leading-colour approximation. Few-parton examples show how junction formation and rope hadronization arise from the same SU(3) colour structure, connecting baryon formation and the screening forces that govern strange-quark production. For nuclear collisions, we introduce a local escape criterion and a phenomenological saturation of the breakup force, motivated by a three-string example of screening through background colour correlations. With common settings, the combined prescription gives a satisfactory description of light-flavour ratios in pp and p--Pb and provides O--O predictions. Charm ratios reveal a substantial remaining deficit in $\Xi_c$ production, motivating separate tests of the fragmentation baseline and its extrapolation with activity.

</details>


### [73] [Dimension-seven spectator effects in charm-hadron lifetimes at NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20747)
*Chia-Wei Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to dimension-seven spectator coefficients in the heavy-quark expansion for charm-hadron lifetimes, finding sizable NLO effects—especially for D⁺ and Dₛ⁺ widths—and improved scale dependence for charmed baryons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions of charm-hadron lifetimes by including NLO QCD corrections to dimension-seven operators in the heavy-quark expansion, which are numerically significant and resolve unphysical (e.g., negative) leading-order predictions.

Method: Computes NLO QCD corrections to connected current-current contributions of dimension-seven spectator coefficients; uses NDR-$\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme with Greek-projection evanescent operators, zero finite mixing into dimension-six operators; estimates width effects using tree-level matrix elements (vacuum insertion for D mesons, bag model for charmed baryons); treats $1/m_c$ corrections via naive expansion.

Result: NLO corrections substantially affect widths: raise D⁺ width by 0.78 ps⁻¹ (turning slightly negative LO prediction positive), contribute ~1% to D⁰, ~10% to Dₛ⁺, ≤2% to singly charmed baryons; reduce scale dependence severalfold for baryons (with evolved matrix elements) but not uniformly for D mesons; without NLO, Dₛ⁺ semileptonic width would be negative.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections to dimension-seven operators are essential for physically consistent and quantitatively reliable charm-hadron lifetime predictions, particularly for D⁺ and Dₛ⁺, and significantly improve theoretical robustness for charmed baryons.

Abstract: We compute the next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to the connected current-current part of the dimension-seven spectator coefficients in the heavy-quark expansion of charm-hadron widths. The finite terms are fixed in NDR-$\overline{\rm MS}$, with the evanescent operators defined by the standard Greek projection and averaged over Hermitian-conjugate pairs, and with a finite subtraction that sets the finite mixing of the dimension-seven operators into the dimension-six ones to zero. In several channels the one-loop term is comparable to the LO coefficient, and two operator families absent at LO are generated. Since the NLO matrix elements of the dimension-seven operators are not known, we estimate the effect of the coefficients on the widths with tree-level matrix elements from vacuum insertion for $D$ mesons and from the bag model for charmed baryons. The $1/m_c$ corrections to the matrix elements of the dimension-six QCD operators are treated by a naive expansion. Without their $O(\alpha_s)$ part the semileptonic width of the $D_s^+$ would be negative. The NLO dimension-seven correction raises the $D^+$ width by 0.78 ps$^{-1}$, so that its central value, slightly negative as published, becomes positive. At central inputs, it is about 1% of the $D^0$ width, 10% of the $D_s^+$ width and at most 2% of the widths of the singly charmed baryons. Added to the published pole-scheme width, the $D^+$ correction gives $\tau(D^+)=1.41^{+\infty}_{-0.76}$ ps. The NLO term reduces the scale dependence of the nonleptonic dimension-seven contribution severalfold for the singly charmed baryons, whose matrix elements are evolved, but not uniformly for the $D$ mesons.

</details>


### [74] [Longitudinal Parton Correlations in MRW-Type Unintegrated Double Parton Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20778)
*R. Kord Valeshabadi,S. Rezaie,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how parton correlations manifest in unintegrated double parton distributions (uDPDs) under various MRW-type transverse momentum prescriptions, using non-factorized GS09 inputs evolved to unequal scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe partonic correlations inside the proton beyond the standard factorized approximation for double parton distributions (DPDs), which are inaccessible in single-scattering processes.

Method: The authors construct unintegrated DPDs using three MRW-type prescriptions—double LO-MRW, virtuality-ordered MRW, and modified KMRW—starting from non-factorized, scale-evolved GS09 DPDs, and study their transverse-momentum dependence and flavor structure.

Result: The different MRW prescriptions yield distinct transverse-momentum dependencies and flavor correlations in uDPDs, revealing how correlation information is preserved or altered by the chosen k_T-generation scheme.

Conclusion: Transverse-momentum prescriptions significantly affect the structure of unintegrated DPDs, implying that careful choice and validation of such schemes are essential for extracting genuine partonic correlations from double parton scattering data.

Abstract: Double parton scattering offers a way to investigate correlations between partons inside the proton that are absent from the usual product approximation for double parton distributions. In this contribution, we discuss how these correlations enter unintegrated double parton distributions when transverse momentum is generated using MRW-type prescriptions. Starting from non-factorized GS09 distributions evolved to unequal scales, we examine the double LO-MRW, virtuality-ordered MRW, and modified KMRW constructions, focusing on their transverse-momentum dependence and flavor structure.

</details>


### [75] [Inferring the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section from IceCube data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20887)
*Reinaldo Francener,Victor P. Goncalves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper assesses IceCube's sensitivity to the neutral-current (NC) neutrino–nucleon cross section using 12 years of data, finding only an upper limit; IceCube-Gen2 is projected to achieve actual constraints via Earth absorption/regeneration and track-to-cascade ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutral-current neutrino interactions remain experimentally unexplored at high energies despite recent progress in charged-current cross-section measurements; constraining the NC cross section is critical for testing Standard Model predictions and probing new physics.

Method: Two complementary analyses are performed on 12 years of IceCube data: (i) modeling neutrino absorption and regeneration through Earth matter (energy- and angle-dependent NC interaction effects), and (ii) measuring the ratio of track-like (charged-current muon neutrino) to cascade-like (NC and CC electron/tau neutrino) events.

Result: Current IceCube data yield only a competitive upper limit on the NC neutrino–nucleon cross section; IceCube-Gen2 is projected to constrain the NC cross section at the ~10–20% level using both methods.

Conclusion: While existing IceCube data lack sufficient statistics and energy/angle resolution to measure the NC cross section, IceCube-Gen2 will enable the first precision measurement—providing a vital test of electroweak sector predictions and potential BSM signatures.

Abstract: Neutrinos interact only via the weak force, through the exchange of $W^{\pm}$ and $Z^{0}$ bosons in charged-current and neutral-current interactions, respectively. Recent experiments have measured the charged-current cross section at high energies, whereas neutral-current interactions remain largely unexplored experimentally. In this work, we investigate the sensitivity of the IceCube Neutrino Observatory to the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section using 12 years of data. We consider two strategies to constrain this quantity: $(i)$ leveraging neutrino absorption and regeneration as neutrinos traverse the Earth; and $(ii)$ using the track-to-cascade event ratio. We find that current IceCube data can set only an upper limit on the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section. We further show that IceCube-Gen2 should be able to constrain the neutral-current cross section with both methods considered here.

</details>


### [76] [Cosmological Collider Signals from Fermion Bubble Loops with a Chemical Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20919)
*Chengcheng Han,Zhanhong Lei,Zhehan Qin,Wuzhou Yin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes local and nonlocal cosmological collider signals from massive fermion bubble loops during inflation with an effective chemical potential, using de Sitter approximation, CC cutting rules, late-time expansions, and Mellin-Barnes techniques; identifies two local signal origins (spectrum/integer vs. UV poles), resolves folded singularities in the three-point limit via MB reorganization, and finds chemical potential enhances oscillatory amplitude and logarithmic frequency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute cosmological collider signals from massive fermions under inflationary chemical potential — a scenario previously unexplored analytically — and resolve ambiguities in singularity structure and signal separation.

Method: Analytical computation of the inflaton four-point correlator in the single-squeezed limit within de Sitter spacetime, employing cosmological collider cutting rules, late-time expansion, partial Mellin-Barnes representation, pole classification (spectrum/integer vs. UV), and MB reorganization to handle folded singularities; validated via independent MB representations.

Result: Closed-form analytical expressions for both local and nonlocal CC signals; identification of two distinct local contributions (from spectrum/integer poles and UV poles); cancellation of apparent folded singularities in the full signal; convergent double-Mellin-Barnes integral for numerical evaluation; quantitative demonstration that chemical potential amplifies oscillatory signal amplitude and increases its logarithmic oscillation frequency.

Conclusion: Chemical potential is a powerful lever for enhancing fermionic cosmological collider signatures; the developed analytical framework—combining cutting rules, late-time expansions, and refined MB techniques—enables robust, singularity-resolved predictions for bubble and future triangle-loop topologies.

Abstract: We present the first systematic analytical calculation of the local and nonlocal cosmological collider (CC) signals generated by massive fermion bubble loops in the presence of an effective chemical potential during inflation. Working in the de Sitter approximation, we investigate the four-point correlator of inflaton perturbations in the single-squeezed limit. By combining CC cutting rules, late-time expansions, and partial Mellin-Barnes (MB) techniques, we derive analytical expressions for both local and nonlocal signals. In particular, we identify two distinct contributions to the local signal, arising from spectrum and integer poles and from ultraviolet poles of the loop-momentum integral. We further investigate the three-point limit, where the separately extracted local and nonlocal contributions develop apparent folded singularities. By reorganizing the MB representation, we demonstrate that these singularities cancel in the complete signal and obtain a convergent double-MB integral suitable for numerical evaluation. Independent calculations using complementary MB representations provide nontrivial consistency checks of our results. We find that the chemical potential can significantly enhance the oscillatory signal while increasing its logarithmic oscillation frequency.
