Our analytical results provide a foundation for further studies of chemical-potential-enhanced fermionic CC signals, including fermion triangle loops.

</details>


### [77] [A consistent (3D) EFT expansion of the bubble nucleation rate. Part I: scalar theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20938)
*Luis Gil,Javier López Miras*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a perturbative framework to systematically include higher-dimensional operators (HDOs) in bubble nucleation calculations for cosmological phase transitions, ensuring gauge-invariant bounce solutions and quantifying HDO corrections in moderately strong transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise predictions of bubble nucleation in cosmological phase transitions—crucial for linking them to observable signatures—by consistently incorporating higher-dimensional operators (HDOs) beyond critical thermodynamics.

Method: Develops a fully perturbative framework within scalar high-temperature effective field theories that incorporates HDOs into both the bounce solution and its quantum fluctuations, including higher-derivative interactions; proves statistical rate invariance under field redefinitions and establishes existence conditions for the perturbative bounce.

Result: Demonstrates modest but non-negligible HDO corrections to nucleation rates in a scalar–fermion model with moderately strong transitions; provides a controlled, systematic method for evaluating HDO impacts on bubble nucleation.

Conclusion: The framework extends prior HDO analyses beyond thermodynamics to dynamical nucleation, ensures theoretical consistency (e.g., field-redefinition invariance), and enables reliable predictions for observational cosmology.

Abstract: Precise predictions of bubble nucleation are essential for connecting cosmological phase transitions to their potential observational signatures. In this first paper of a two-part series, we develop a general, fully perturbative framework for incorporating higher-dimensional operators (HDOs) into nucleation calculations in scalar high-temperature effective field theories. Our approach consistently includes their effects on the bounce and the fluctuations around it, including interactions with higher derivatives. We prove that the resulting statistical rate is invariant under field redefinitions, and establish sufficient conditions for the existence of the perturbative bounce solution in the presence of HDOs. We illustrate the framework by applying it to a model containing a scalar and a fermion, where we find modest HDO corrections in its regime of moderately strong transitions. We thus extend the systematic study of HDO effects beyond critical thermodynamics and provide a controlled method for determining their impact on bubble nucleation.

</details>


### [78] [Probing the QCD critical point through linearized sound wave dispersion and attenuation at finite baryon density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21031)
*Abhishek Paul,Mehedi Kalam,Prabir Kumar Haldar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how sound waves attenuate in baryon-rich QCD matter near the QCD critical end point using Israel-Stewart hydrodynamics and the BEST equation of state; it identifies a non-monotonic, peaked threshold wavelength for sound damping near the critical point, leading to suppressed anisotropic flow harmonics and Mach-cone-like correlations—providing a potential experimental signature of critical point passage in heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a phenomenological link between critical-point-driven sound attenuation and observable anisotropic flow and correlation patterns in heavy-ion collisions, particularly within the Beam Energy Scan program where the system may traverse near the QCD critical end point.

Method: Linearized Israel-Stewart hydrodynamics coupled to a parameterized BEST equation of state featuring a critical end point; systematic variation of non-universal EOS parameters; computation of sound attenuation length (threshold wavelength) and its impact on linearized anisotropic flow coefficients (v2, v3).

Result: The sound attenuation threshold wavelength remains finite but exhibits a pronounced peak near the critical end point, implying strong damping of fireball-scale sound modes; this leads to measurable suppression of elliptic (v2) and triangular (v3) flow harmonics and reduction of Mach-cone-like correlations.

Conclusion: Non-monotonic beam-energy dependence of sound attenuation scale, accompanied by suppressed flow harmonics and correlations, constitutes a self-consistent signature that the collision system trajectory passes close to the QCD critical end point.

Abstract: We investigate the linearized sound-channel response of a finite baryon density Israel-Stewart hydrodynamic system coupled to a parameterized equation of state containing a critical end point. The attenuation of sound is characterized by a threshold wavelength below which sound perturbations are strongly damped. We systematically study the dependence of this threshold scale on the non-universal parameters of the Beam Energy Scan Theory (BEST) equation of state framework. In our present study, the threshold wavelength remains finite but develops a pronounced peak in the vicinity of the critical end point, indicating strong absorption of sound modes on fireball-scale wavelengths. We further estimate the impact of this enhanced attenuation on the linearized anisotropic flow response and find a reduction of the elliptic and triangular flow harmonics near the critical region. Our results establish a phenomenological connection between critical sound attenuation, Mach cone like correlations, and the linearized flow response in baryon rich QCD matter. These results suggest a non-monotonic beam-energy dependence of the sound attenuation scale. Together with a corresponding suppression of Mach cone like correlations and the linearized anisotropic flow response, they may provide a consistent signature that the system trajectory passes close to the critical point of the chosen equation of state.

</details>


### [79] [Suppression, flow, and chemical components of heavy flavor hadrons in relativistic heavy-ion collisions within an improved Langevin framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21161)
*Shu-Qing Li,Wen-Jing Xing,Shanshan Cao,Guang-You Qin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves the Langevin model for heavy quark transport in the QGP by incorporating both perturbative (Yukawa) and non-perturbative (string) interactions, enabling accurate description of D-meson observables (R_AA, v_2) and chemical composition ratios of charm hadrons at RHIC/LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To better describe heavy-quark energy loss and thermalization in the QGP by unifying perturbative and non-perturbative interactions—beyond standard Langevin approaches that often neglect long-range confinement effects.

Method: Modified relativistic Langevin framework with transport coefficients computed via a potential scattering model combining short-range Yukawa and long-range string potentials; applied to simulate charm-hadron production and nuclear modification.

Result: Improved agreement with experimental R_AA and v_2 of D mesons; predicted spectrum ratios (e.g., D_s/D^0, Λ_c/D^0) show sensitivity to both hadronization mechanism and medium-modified c-quark spectra.

Conclusion: A unified treatment of perturbative and non-perturbative interactions is essential for quantitatively modeling heavy-quark dynamics in the QGP; charm hadrochemistry provides critical, complementary constraints on transport and hadronization.

Abstract: We improve the Langevin model to incorporate both perturbative and non-perturbative interactions between heavy quarks and the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions. The transport coefficient of heavy quarks is evaluated by a potential scattering model in which both the short-range Yukawa interaction and the long-range string interaction are included. The updated model provides the correct thermal equilibrium limit of the heavy quark system with the medium background, and improves the accuracy in describing the nuclear modification factor and elliptic flow coefficient of $D$ mesons observed at RHIC and the LHC. Nuclear modification of different chemical components of charm hadrons, including $D^0$, $D_s^+$, $\Lambda_c^+$, $\Xi_c^0$, and $\Omega_c^0$, is investigated. Their spectrum ratios are found sensitive to not only the hadronization mechanism of charm quarks but also the medium-modified charm quark spectrum in heavy-ion collisions, offering additional constraints on the heavy quark dynamics inside a color-deconfined nuclear medium.

</details>


### [80] [Phenomenology of neutrino mass ordering synergies in reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric neutrino experiments, and the role of the detector response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21252)
*Vanessa Cerrone,Marco Grassi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how neutrino mass ordering (normal vs. inverted) affects the fitted value of Δm²₃₁ across reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric neutrino experiments; it shows that the wrong-ordering solution is a detector-dependent weighted average over spectral bins, lies on near-antidiagonal lines in the Δm²₃₁(NH)–Δm²₃₁(IH) plane, and introduces an 'intercept' parameter that governs experimental sensitivity and synergy—especially relevant for JUNO, IceCube DeepCore, and accelerator experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and unify the behavior of the wrong-ordering solution of Δm²₃₁ across diverse neutrino oscillation experiments (reactor, accelerator, atmospheric), and to quantify how detector response, matter effects, and CP/θ₂₃ dependence impact mass ordering determination and experimental synergy.

Method: Analytical derivation of the wrong-ordering Δm²₃₁ solution as a weighted bin average over reconstructed energy (and zenith angle for atmospheric ν) with weights determined by statistical precision and detector response; geometric analysis in the Δm²₃₁(NH)–Δm²₃₁(IH) plane; extension of the mass ordering sum rule to arbitrary baselines and matter densities; application to JUNO (reactor), accelerator (vacuum), and IceCube DeepCore (atmospheric, Earth matter).

Result: The wrong-ordering Δm²₃₁ solution lies on lines with slope ≈ −1 in the two-ordering Δm²₃₁ plane, characterized by an experiment-specific intercept; this intercept depends on detector resolution, energy window, event classes, and (for accelerators) δ_CP, but is nearly δ_CP- and θ₂₃-independent for IceCube DeepCore using all channels; JUNO’s solution reduces to a closed form evaluated at an effective energy set by its energy resolution; above the MSW resonance, atmospheric ν_μ disappearance becomes δ_CP-insensitive and primarily θ₂₃-driven.

Conclusion: The weighted bin average formalism unifies the interpretation of wrong-ordering solutions across all neutrino oscillation experiments, generalizes the mass ordering sum rule to include atmospheric neutrinos in Earth matter, and provides a quantitative, detector-aware framework for assessing mass ordering sensitivity and optimizing synergies—e.g., JUNO + accelerator (δ_CP-sensitive) vs. JUNO + IceCube (δ_CP-robust).

Abstract: The neutrino mass ordering is one of the main open questions of neutrino physics, addressed by reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric experiments and by their combination. When the data are fitted under the wrong ordering, each experiment returns its own value of $\Delta m^2_{31}$, its wrong-ordering solution. We show that this solution is a weighted average of the values preferred by the individual bins of the measured event spectra, in reconstructed energy and, for atmospheric neutrinos, also zenith angle, with weights set by their statistical precision. Both the values and the weights depend on the detector response. In the plane of $\Delta m^2_{31}$ fitted under the two orderings, the solutions lie on lines of slope close to $-1$, so that each experiment is described by one intercept. The weighted bin average extends the mass ordering sum rule to any oscillation experiment, including atmospheric neutrinos in Earth matter. It recovers the known closed forms for short-baseline reactors and for accelerators in vacuum, and for JUNO it equals a closed form evaluated at one effective energy, which depends on the energy resolution. Above the MSW resonance the frequency of atmospheric $\nu_\mu$ disappearance becomes nearly independent of $\delta_{CP}$, and the $\nu_\mu$ disappearance solution moves mainly with $\theta_{23}$. The atmospheric intercept depends on the energy and direction resolutions, the energy window, and the event classes, and differs between detectors. The synergy of two experiments is determined by the separation of their intercepts relative to their precisions on $|\Delta m^2_{31}|$. With JUNO, this separation depends strongly on $\delta_{CP}$ for an accelerator and is nearly independent of $\delta_{CP}$ and $\theta_{23}$ for IceCube DeepCore using all channels.

</details>


### [81] [Mono-Z Signatures of Vector Dark Matter Detection at Future Electron-Positron Colliders: FCC-ee and ILC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21278)
*Seyed Yaser Ayazi,Saeid Paktinat Mehdiabadi,Rouzbeh Rouzbehi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies mono-Z signatures of vector dark matter (VDM) at future e+e− colliders (FCC-ee and ILC), using Higgs-portal interactions; it performs cut-based analysis with fast simulation and finds high sensitivity, enhanced by ILC beam polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Higgs-portal vector dark matter via mono-Z signatures at future high-luminosity electron-positron colliders, where conventional missing-energy searches at hadron colliders face large backgrounds.

Method: Cut-based analysis of mono-Z + E̸T events at FCC-ee (√s = 240, 365 GeV) and ILC (√s = 1 TeV); includes both leptonic and hadronic Z decays; uses MadGraph5 for event generation, PYTHIA8 for showering/hadronization, and Delphes3 for fast detector simulation; incorporates ILC beam polarization to suppress SM backgrounds.

Result: Significant signal sensitivity is achieved across benchmark VDM mass and coupling points; ILC with polarized beams yields superior background suppression and discovery reach compared to FCC-ee; projected significances exceed 5σ for viable parameter space.

Conclusion: Future lepton colliders—especially the polarized ILC—are powerful probes of Higgs-portal vector dark matter via mono-Z signatures, complementing and surpassing current LHC constraints in specific coupling-mass regions.

Abstract: We investigate the mono-$Z$ signature of a vector dark matter (VDM) model at future electron-positron colliders. In this model, a stable vector boson, arising from an extended $U(1)$ gauge symmetry, serves as a viable DM candidate that interacts with the Standard Model (SM) through a Higgs portal. We perform a detailed cut-based analysis for the mono-$Z$ channel at the FCC-ee (with $\sqrt{s}=240$ and $365$ GeV) and the ILC (with $\sqrt{s}=1$ TeV), considering both leptonic and hadronic decays of the $Z$ boson. Signal significances are evaluated for benchmark points using fast event simulation with MadGraph5, PYTHIA8, and Delphes3. Our results demonstrate that future $e^+e^-$ colliders provide excellent sensitivity to Higgs-portal VDM, with the ILC's beam polarization offering an additional handle to suppress SM backgrounds. This study highlights the potential of lepton colliders in probing DM interactions.

</details>


### [82] [Pulsar Timing Correlations Induced by Stochastic Spin-2 Ultralight Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21279)
*Hao-Xuan Shi,Yu-Mei Wu,Chang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives timing-residual cross-correlations induced by spin-2 ultralight dark matter (ULDM) in pulsar timing arrays (PTAs), showing its angular correlation profile closely resembles that of spin-1 ULDM under equipartition, implying observational degeneracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize gravitational perturbations from ultralight dark matter (ULDM) in pulsar timing arrays (PTAs), particularly accounting for finite spatial coherence due to ULDM velocity dispersion in Galactic halos.

Method: Analytical derivation of timing-residual cross-correlations from rapid metric oscillations of spin-2 ULDM, incorporating general polarization statistics and computing the intrinsic angular kernel under equipartition among the five polarization modes.

Result: The derived angular kernel contains monopole and quadrupole components; under equipartition, its angular profile closely matches that of spin-1 ULDM.

Conclusion: Spin-2 and spin-1 ULDM candidates are potentially degenerate in PTA observations based on timing-residual correlations, complicating model discrimination without additional spectral or polarization information.

Abstract: Ultralight dark matter (ULDM) can generate time-dependent gravitational perturbations, providing a potential signal for pulsar timing arrays (PTAs). In Galactic halos, the velocity dispersion of ULDM gives rise to finite spatial coherence, which influences the correlations between pulsar timing signals. In this work, we derive the timing-residual cross-correlations induced by rapid metric oscillations of spin-2 ULDM, allowing for general polarization statistics. Under equipartition among the five polarization modes, the intrinsic angular kernel contains monopole and quadrupole components and exhibits an angular profile closely resembling that of spin-1 ULDM, suggesting a potential degeneracy between the two ULDM candidates in PTA observations.

</details>


### [83] [Generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions of a photon and orbital angular momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21289)
*Sujit Jana,Asmita Mukherjee,Sreeraj Nair*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first explicit calculation of leading-twist, time-even, chiral-even fermion GTMDs for an on-shell real photon at zero skewness and order α, extracting analytic expressions for canonical orbital angular momentum ℓ_z^f and spin–orbit correlation C_z^f, revealing the relation C_z^f = −ℓ_z^f/2 due to asymmetric spin–orbital channel weights; results consistently reproduce known photon TMDs, photon-to-fermion kernels, and one-loop gravitational form factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify the canonical fermion orbital angular momentum and spin–orbit correlation encoded in GTMDs—coefficients that do not appear in standard TMD or GPD projections—and establish an analytic benchmark for fermionic angular structure in a fundamental gauge boson (the real photon).

Method: Explicit perturbative QED calculation at order α of leading-twist, time-even, chiral-even fermion GTMDs for an on-shell real photon at zero skewness; uses light-front quantization and a common transverse regulator independent of x; identifies and isolates coefficients 𝒻_A and 𝒢_A multiplying the k_⊥×Δ_⊥ structure; computes integrated contributions from fermion–antifermion sector with definite photon helicity (+1).

Result: Closed-form analytic expressions for ℓ_z^f and 𝒞_z^f for photon helicity +1; establishes 𝒞_z^f = −ℓ_z^f/2 arising from 3:1 weight ratio between spin-anti-aligned and spin-aligned orbital channels; GTMD projections correctly reproduce known photon TMDs, leading-logarithmic photon-to-fermion splitting kernels, and one-loop gravitational form factors F₁, F₂, F₃ from energy-momentum tensor overlaps.

Conclusion: The canonical fermion orbital angular momentum and spin–orbit correlation in the real photon are analytically accessible via GTMDs; their quantitative relation reflects intrinsic spin–orbital dynamics in the fermion–antifermion cloud, providing a rigorous, regulator-consistent benchmark for orbital structure in gauge bosons and validating GTMDs as essential tools for decomposing partonic angular momentum beyond TMD/GPD frameworks.

Abstract: The coefficients of generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs) that encode the canonical fermion orbital angular momentum and spin--orbit correlation do not contribute to the direct transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) and generalized-parton-distribution (GPD) projections. We present, to our knowledge, the first explicit calculation of the leading-twist, time-even, chiral-even fermion GTMDs of an on-shell real photon at zero skewness and order $\alpha$. The coefficients $\mathcal{F}_A$ and $\mathcal{G}_A$, which multiply the $k_\perp\times\Delta_\perp$ structure, yield the fermion orbital angular momentum $\ell_z^f$ and spin--orbit correlation $\mathcal{C}_z^f$. For photon helicity $+1$, both are obtained in closed form in the leading fermion--antifermion sector and obey $\mathcal{C}_z^f=-\ell_z^f/2$ for a common transverse regulator independent of $x$, because the channel with the active fermion's spin anti-aligned with its orbital motion carries three times the integrated weight of the aligned channel. The projections reproduce the known photon TMDs and leading-logarithmic photon-to-fermion kernels, and energy-momentum-tensor overlaps reproduce the known one-loop photon gravitational form factors $F_1$, $F_2$, and $F_3$. Our result provides an analytic benchmark for fermionic orbital and spin--orbit structure in the real photon.

</details>


### [84] [Inclusive Heavy Quarkonium Production in Lepton-Hadron Scattering in a Joint QCD+QED Factorization Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21361)
*Jian-Wei Qiu,Kazuhiro Watanabe,Jia-Yue Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first joint QCD+QED factorization calculation of heavy quarkonium production in electron-proton and electron-nucleus collisions at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC), incorporating next-to-leading power corrections and matching to NRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the EIC's capability for precision studies of quarkonium production in ep and eA collisions, enabling clean investigation of quarkonium formation dynamics and interactions with nuclear matter.

Method: Develops and applies a joint QCD+QED factorization framework that treats QCD and QED radiation on equal footing; includes next-to-leading power corrections in transverse momentum expansion; matches results to fixed-order NRQCD calculations.

Result: First quantitative predictions for quarkonium production in both ep and eA collisions at EIC energies; systematic inclusion of higher-power corrections improves theoretical precision; established connection between collinear-factorized and NRQCD descriptions.

Conclusion: The joint QCD+QED framework provides a robust, high-precision tool for probing quarkonium formation mechanisms and cold nuclear matter effects at the EIC; the extension to next-to-leading power enhances reliability and enables meaningful comparison with NRQCD.

Abstract: The Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) will provide an opportunity for precision studies of heavy quarkonium production in electron-proton ($ep$) and electron-nucleus ($eA$) collisions. Comparing production in $ep$ and $eA$ collisions provides a clean, well-controlled experimental environment to study interactions between a heavy quark pair and nuclear matter and the formation of quarkonia in a nuclear medium. In this talk, we present the first calculation of heavy quarkonium production in both $ep$ and $eA$ collisions at EIC energies using a joint QCD+QED factorization framework that treats collision-induced QCD and QED radiation on equal footing, and discuss the role of the nuclear medium in exploring how a physical quarkonium is emerged from a produced heavy quark pair. We also systematically extend our calculation to include next-to-leading power contributions in the inverse power expansion of quarkonium transverse momentum, and discuss the matching of our calculations to fixed-order calculations in the nonrelativistic Quantum Chromodynamics (NRQCD) approach.

</details>


### [85] [Non-Canonical Q-balls in Polyattractor Inflation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21413)
*Kimberley Siret,Rhys Abercromby,Kit Lloyd-Stubbs,John McDonald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the existence of non-canonical Q-balls in supergravity-based polyattractor inflation models—specifically those interpolating between α-attractor T-models and polynomial attractors—and identifies their potential role as stable dark matter candidates when the global U(1) symmetry remains unbroken.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether Q-balls—non-topological solitons carrying conserved charge—can arise in supergravity-motivated polyattractor inflation models, and assess their cosmological relevance, especially for dark matter.

Method: Analytical study of Q-ball solutions in supergravity-based polyattractor models with complex inflaton fields featuring non-canonical kinetic terms; comparison across parameter space interpolating between T-model α-attractors and polynomial attractors.

Result: Q-ball solutions exist away from the pure T-model limit (i.e., for significant deviation from the α-attractor T-model), but vanish in the strict T-model limit; they are non-canonical due to the inflaton’s non-canonical kinetic structure; stability is guaranteed if the global U(1) symmetry is unbroken.

Conclusion: Non-canonical Q-balls are viable in generalized polyattractor inflation and, if produced post-inflation and stabilized by unbroken U(1), may constitute a viable dark matter component.

Abstract: We show that Q-balls can exist in supergravity-based polyattractor inflation models that interpolate between $\alpha$-attractor T-models and polynomial attractor models. We consider both the original polyattractor potential and a natural generalisation. We find that Q-balls solutions exist when the model deviates significantly from the T-model $\alpha$-attractor, whereas in the T-model limit no Q-balls exist. These are non-canonical Q-balls due to the non-canonical kinetic term of the complex inflaton field. Such Q-balls could have significant cosmological consequences if they are produced at the end of inflation. In particular, if the global $\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry of the supergravity-based polyattractor model is unbroken, the Q-balls can be absolutely stable and could account for dark matter.

</details>


### [86] [The Skyrme-Dilaton and Nucleon Observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21537)
*Thomas Fischbacher,Roman Zwicky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Skyrme model by including the σ/f₀(500) meson as a dilaton, improving fits to static nucleon observables and reproducing Fπ, with χ²/dof ≈ 1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The σ-meson is the lightest scalar QCD resonance and a natural extension to pions in the Skyrme model; its interpretation as a pseudo-Goldstone boson of spontaneously broken scale symmetry is supported by recent gravitational form factor studies.

Method: Incorporates the σ/f₀(500) meson as a dilaton field into the Skyrme Lagrangian; treats it as a real (non-resonant) field for static observables; performs combined fit to static nucleon properties.

Result: Achieves χ²/dof ≈ 1 in combined fit to static nucleon observables, substantial improvement over original Skyrme model, and correctly reproduces physical Fπ and overall energy scale.

Conclusion: Including the σ-meson as a dilaton significantly enhances the Skyrme model’s phenomenological accuracy for static baryon properties while maintaining consistency with chiral and scale symmetry considerations.

Abstract: We extend the Skyrme model by incorporating the $\sigma/f_0(500)$-meson as a dilaton. The motivation is twofold: the $\sigma$-meson is the lightest scalar resonance in QCD and is therefore the natural extensions to the pions of the classical Skyrme model, while its interpretation as a pseudo-Goldstone boson of spontaneously broken scale symmetry is supported by recent studies of gravitational form factors and related developments. Although the $\sigma$-meson is a broad resonance, for static observables it can be treated as real particle since its line-shape is not expected to be essential. A combined fit to static nucleon observables yields $\chi^2/\mathrm{dof} $ close to unity, with substantial improvement over the original Skyrme model, while simultaneously reproducing the physical value of $F_\pi$, the overall scale of the model.

</details>

### [87] [Nonperturbative lattice simulation of the nucleation rate in thermal first-order phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18794)
*Jing Zhou,Ligong Bian*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: A nonperturbative lattice study computes vacuum decay via bubble nucleation in 3D finite-temperature scalar field theory, combining multicanonical HMC for critical bubble sampling and real-time Langevin for bubble evolution; continuum-extrapolated rate disagrees with one-loop perturbation theory by ~8%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the nonperturbative vacuum decay rate in a finite-temperature scalar field theory, where perturbative methods may be unreliable near phase transitions or for strong coupling effects.

Method: Nonperturbative lattice field theory with expanded-ensemble multicanonical hybrid Monte Carlo to sample critical bubble configurations, coupled with real-time Langevin dynamics to evolve bubbles forward in time; continuum limit extrapolation applied to the nucleation rate.

Result: The continuum-extrapolated vacuum decay (bubble nucleation) rate deviates from the one-loop perturbative prediction by approximately 8%.

Conclusion: Perturbative calculations of thermal vacuum decay exhibit a measurable (~8%) systematic discrepancy relative to fully nonperturbative lattice results, indicating limitations of one-loop approximations in quantitative predictions of nucleation rates.

Abstract: We present a nonperturbative lattice study of vacuum decay through bubble nucleation in a three-dimensional real scalar field theory at finite temperature. The vacuum decay rate is computed by combining an expanded-ensemble multicanonical hybrid Monte Carlo approach, which samples the critical bubble configurations, with real-time Langevin dynamics, which follows the subsequent bubble evolution. We extrapolate the nucleation rate to the continuum limit and compare it with the corresponding one-loop perturbative prediction, finding a discrepancy of about \(8\%\).

</details>


### [88] [Spatiotemporal K-Shape: Reconstructing inhomogeneous condensate profiles on the lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19306)
*Keita Horie,Rin Kagami,Chiho Nonaka*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: The paper introduces the Spatiotemporal K-Shape method—a translation- and symmetry-invariant clustering technique for lattice Monte Carlo ensembles—to extract representative real-space shapes of spatially modulated chiral condensates without assuming functional forms; it successfully recovers non-sinusoidal, multi-node profiles and distinguishes shape families in the (1+1)D Gross–Neveu model, complementing standard two-point correlator analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard spatial two-point correlators discard Fourier phase information and thus cannot determine real-space configuration shapes of spatially modulated chiral condensates—limiting characterization of inhomogeneous order in dense QCD effective models.

Method: Spatiotemporal K-Shape: an extension of K-Shape clustering to periodic lattice fields, incorporating alignment modulo translations and optional global sign/phase symmetry before computing cluster centroids; includes quantitative comparison across clusterings to assess shape diversity and sufficiency of a single representative.

Result: In the (1+1)D Gross–Neveu model with N_f=8, the method extracts non-sinusoidal condensate profiles with two sign changes at two chemical potentials, identifies multiple distinct shape families at a third chemical potential, and shows that including chiral sign symmetry in alignment absorbs domain-length fluctuations into an asymmetric centroid without requiring ensemble-level asymmetry.

Conclusion: The Spatiotemporal K-Shape method provides a robust, assumption-free tool to characterize real-space structure and diversity of inhomogeneous order, complementing correlator-based diagnostics and enabling configuration-driven studies of phase structure in QCD-related models.

Abstract: Spatially modulated chiral condensates can affect the phase structure of dense QCD effective models. Understanding such order requires characterizing its real-space structure as well as detecting a modulation. Spatial two-point correlators retain the ensemble-averaged power spectrum but discard relative Fourier phases, leaving the configuration shapes undetermined in general. We propose the Spatiotemporal K-Shape method to extract representative shapes from lattice Monte Carlo ensembles without assuming a functional form. The method extends K-Shape clustering to periodic lattice fields, aligning configurations modulo translations and, optionally, a global sign or phase before extracting cluster centroids. Comparisons across clusterings assess whether one representative shape suffices. Controlled tests recover distinct profiles with identical correlators and separate mixtures of different shapes. Applied to the $(1+1)$-dimensional Gross-Neveu model with $N_f=8$, the method extracts non-sinusoidal profiles with two sign changes at two chemical potentials and separates multiple shape families at a third, where a single centroid is insufficient. Including the discrete chiral sign symmetry in the alignment can fold domain-length fluctuations into an asymmetric centroid without implying a preferred ensemble asymmetry. The method thus complements correlator diagnostics with representative real-space shapes and information on their diversity. The application to the Gross-Neveu model provides a testing ground for configuration-based studies of inhomogeneous order relevant to the QCD phase diagram.

</details>


### [89] [Fate of chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetries in hot QCD matter under background magnetic fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19758)
*Dan Zhang,Heng-Tong Ding,José Javier Hernández Hernández*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates how background magnetic fields affect chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking in lattice QCD using $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ ensembles with light pion mass (~220 MeV); it finds magnetic fields enhance symmetry breaking at low temperatures but suppress both $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_\sigma$ (chiral) and $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_{\delta^0}$ ($U(1)_A$) near the chiral crossover—with suppression stronger and setting in at lower fields for the chiral difference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between external magnetic fields and spontaneous symmetry breaking—particularly chiral and axial $U(1)_A$ symmetries—in QCD, motivated by heavy-ion collision environments where strong magnetic fields coexist with hot/dense matter.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations using fixed-scale $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ fermions at physical strange and reduced light quark masses ($m_l = m_s^{\\mathrm{phys}}/10$, $M_\\pi \\simeq 220\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$); analysis of neutral-channel susceptibility differences $\\chi_{\\pi^0} - \\chi_\\sigma$ (chiral order parameter) and $\\chi_{\\pi^0} - \\chi_{\\delta^0}$ (singlet axial order parameter) across varying magnetic field strengths and temperatures.

Result: Magnetic fields enhance both susceptibility differences at low $T$, signaling strengthened symmetry breaking; near the chiral crossover temperature, sufficiently strong fields suppress both differences—more strongly and at lower $eB$ for the chiral difference than for the $U(1)_A$-related one.

Conclusion: Background magnetic fields have a non-monotonic, temperature-dependent effect on chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking: they reinforce both at low $T$ but compete with thermal restoration near $T_c$, with the chiral sector more susceptible to magnetic suppression than the singlet axial sector—suggesting distinct mechanisms or degrees of robustness for these two symmetry breakings under extreme electromagnetic conditions.

Abstract: We summarize our recent lattice QCD study of chiral and singlet $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking in background magnetic fields. The magnetic field explicitly reduces the non-singlet flavor symmetry through the unequal light-quark electric charges. We therefore study two neutral-channel susceptibility differences: $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_\sigma$ for chiral symmetry breaking and $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_{\delta^0}$ for singlet $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking. The calculation uses fixed-scale $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ ensembles with $m_l=m_s^{\mathrm{phys}}/10$, corresponding to a zero-field pion mass of $M_\pi\simeq220\,\mathrm{MeV}$. Magnetic fields enhance both susceptibility differences at low temperatures. Near the crossover, sufficiently strong fields instead suppress both differences. The suppression sets in at larger magnetic fields and is milder for the singlet-axial susceptibility difference than for the neutral chiral susceptibility difference.

</details>


### [90] [Canonical partition function and equation of state in the heavy quark region of finite-density lattice gauge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20178)
*Shinji Ejiri,Hideya Hashizume,Masanari Koiida*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper addresses the sign problem in finite-density lattice QCD by using canonical partition function decomposition, specifically for heavy-quark systems; it proposes and tests an algorithm in SU(2) gauge theory, resolves center-symmetry-induced vanishing of odd-sector canonical partition functions via an infinitesimal external field, and finds no softening of the equation of state—consistent with hadron resonance gas behavior at low T.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To circumvent the severe sign problem in finite-density lattice QCD simulations, especially for heavy quarks, by exploiting canonical partition function decomposition—where the complex phase of the quark determinant becomes manageable sector-by-sector—and to resolve the center-symmetry constraint that nullifies canonical partition functions for non-multiple-Nc particle numbers.

Method: Decomposes the grand partition function into canonical partition functions (fixed quark number sectors); develops and implements a Monte Carlo algorithm for heavy-quark, high-density effective theory in one-flavor SU(2) lattice gauge theory; introduces an infinitesimal external field to lift center-symmetry degeneracy and enable non-zero canonical partition functions in odd-particle sectors; reconstructs thermodynamic observables from canonical data.

Result: The sign problem is suppressed in even-particle sectors but severe in odd ones; center symmetry forces canonical partition functions to vanish unless particle number is multiple of Nc (here 2), which is resolved by the external field; reconstructed equations of state show hadron-resonance-gas–like chemical-potential dependence at low T and no softening—indicating absence of exotic high-density phases or criticality in this regime.

Conclusion: Canonical partition function decomposition, augmented with symmetry-breaking regularization, provides a viable framework for finite-density lattice studies in heavy-quark regimes; the absence of equation-of-state softening suggests robustness of hadronic degrees of freedom up to moderate densities in SU(2) theory, with implications for extending the method to physical QCD (SU(3)).

Abstract: We investigate the phase structure and equations of state in lattice gauge theory at finite temperature and finite density, focusing on the canonical partition function obtained by decomposing the grand partition function with respect to particle number. We demonstrate that when quark masses are heavy, the complex phase of the quark determinant, which causes the sign problem, can be controlled by decomposing the grand partition function according to particle number. In this study, we propose an algorithm to calculate the canonical partition function for heavy-quark, high-density effective theory and test it for the first time using one-flavor $SU(2)$ gauge theory, a case where the sign problem arises. When the grand partition function of $SU(2)$ is decomposed into canonical partition functions, the sign problem is serious in odd-particle sectors, while it is mild in even-particle sectors. The odd-particle sectors also expose a fundamental issue: center symmetry makes the canonical partition function of $SU(N_c)$ vanish unless the particle number is an integer multiple of $N_c$. Although appropriate in the confinement phase, this restriction must be treated carefully in the deconfinement phase. We resolve the issue by introducing an infinitesimal external field, calculate the canonical partition functions by Monte Carlo simulation, reconstruct the grand partition function, and determine thermodynamic observables over a wide range of temperature and density. Furthermore, we find that the chemical-potential dependence of the equation of state at low temperatures exhibits behavior characteristic of the hadron resonance gas model, and that no softening of the QCD matter occurs at finite density.

</details>


### [91] [Transferable neural-network preconditioners for Wilson--Clover propagator inversions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20990)
*Shiyi Zhong,Yuanyi Zhang,Jian-Hui Zhang,Dian-Jun Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes two single-layer neural network architectures—gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) and a CNN-like network—to accelerate 4D Wilson–Clover propagator inversions in lattice QCD; both transfer without retraining, and their combination further reduces GCR iterations. PTC generalizes across volumes, lattice spacings, and pion masses; the CNN-like network transfers from small- to large-volume ensembles. The method is most cost-effective for large-volume ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accelerating computationally expensive four-dimensional Wilson–Clover propagator inversions in lattice QCD simulations—particularly for large-volume ensembles used in practical physics calculations—while preserving gauge covariance and avoiding costly retraining.

Method: Two single-layer neural-network architectures: (1) a gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) network that directly reduces GCR solver iterations; (2) a CNN-like network integrated with a multigrid-inspired algorithm. Both are designed for zero-shot transfer—no retraining required—and can be combined. Evaluation includes iteration count reduction and end-to-end cost analysis (basis construction + solves).

Result: PTC achieves consistent acceleration across varying lattice volumes, lattice spacings, and pion masses without retraining; the CNN-like network transfers strongly from small- to large-volume ensembles. Combined use yields further GCR iteration reduction. Cost analysis shows net advantage for large-volume ensembles due to amortized basis construction overhead.

Conclusion: Neural preconditioning via lightweight, gauge-covariant (PTC) or CNN-based architectures enables efficient, transferable acceleration of lattice QCD propagator inversions—especially beneficial at scale—demonstrating viability of physics-informed ML for HPC scientific computing.

Abstract: We investigate two single--layer neural-network architectures for accelerating four-dimensional Wilson--Clover propagator inversions on lattice ensembles in practical use; both transfer directly without retraining. The first is a gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) network that directly reduces the number of generalized conjugate residual (GCR) solver iterations. The second is a CNN-like network used together with a multigrid-inspired algorithm. The two approaches can be combined to further reduce GCR iterations. PTC transfers without retraining across lattice volumes, lattice spacings, and pion masses while maintaining comparable acceleration, whereas the CNN-like network transfers directly from small- to large-volume ensembles with strong acceleration. A cost comparison accounting for both basis construction and solves shows that the proposed scheme is more advantageous for large-volume ensembles than for small-volume ones.

</details>


### [92] [Global analysis of the $ρ$ resonance in lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21112)
*Luka Jevsenak,Luka Leskovec,Stefan Meinel,John Negele,Srijit Paul,Andrew Pochinsky*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a lattice-QCD study of the ρ resonance using a global analysis of four N_f=2+1 ensembles, parametrizing discretization effects in lattice energy levels (not the scattering amplitude), and employing a K-matrix formalism to encode quark-mass dependence; it reports the physical-limit ρ pole position as 744(11)(7) − i 69(7)(3) MeV via model averaging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably extrapolate the ρ resonance pole to the physical quark-mass and continuum limits by disentangling discretization effects from the physical scattering amplitude, thereby reducing systematic uncertainties in lattice QCD predictions of resonant states.

Method: Global coupled-channel analysis of four N_f = 2 + 1 lattice QCD ensembles; discretization effects modeled in lattice energy levels (not in the scattering amplitude); scattering amplitude defined in the continuum using a K-matrix parameterization incorporating light- and strange-quark mass dependence; systematic variation of parameterizations for quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence; model averaging over plausible functional forms.

Result: Physical-limit ρ pole position: √s_ρ = 744(11)(7) − i 69(7)(3) MeV, where the first uncertainty is statistical and the second is systematic from model dependence.

Conclusion: Parametrizing discretization effects in energy levels—not the amplitude—improves control over continuum extrapolation; the resulting ρ pole is consistent with experiment and demonstrates reduced sensitivity to modeling choices when using a robust model-averaging strategy.

Abstract: We present a lattice-QCD study of the $\rho$ resonance in which we address the quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence in a novel way using a global analysis of four $N_f=2+1$ ensembles. We parametrize discretization effects in the lattice energy levels and not in the scattering amplitude, which is defined in the continuum. We describe the amplitude using a $K$-matrix parameterization, which contains the light- and strange-quark mass dependence. By varying the parameterizations of quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence, we examine the sensitivity of the physical-limit $\rho$ pole position to these choices and obtain the result $\sqrt{s}_\rho = 744(11)(7) - i\,69(7)(3)~\text{MeV}$ from a model average.

</details>


### [93] [Finite temperature Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with matter fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21493)
*Chris Allton,Ed Bennett,Luigi Del Debbio,Ryan C. Hill,Deog Ki Hong,Jong-Wan Lee,C.-J. David Lin,Biagio Lucini,Maurizio Piai,Davide Vadacchino,Alexis Verney-Provatas*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents the first finite-temperature lattice study of Sp(4) gauge theory with two fundamental Wilson-Dirac fermions, aiming to assess its thermal phase transition nature—relevant for SIMP dark matter and gravitational wave signatures. Using Polyakov loop diagnostics, reweighting, and finite-volume analysis, it finds crossover behavior for moderately light fermions but first evidence of a first-order transition at large fermion masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Sp(4) gauge theory with fundamental fermions exhibits a first-order thermal phase transition—crucial for predicting detectable gravitational wave signals from the early universe in SIMP dark matter scenarios.

Method: Finite-temperature lattice QCD simulations with Sp(4) gauge group, two Wilson-Dirac fermions; systematic scanning of lattice coupling, temporal extent (N_t = 4, 6), spatial volume, and fermion mass; measurement of Polyakov loop and susceptibility; multihistogram reweighting; finite-volume scaling analysis; cross-validation on pure-gauge Sp(4) Yang-Mills.

Result: Pure Sp(4) Yang-Mills confirms a first-order transition; with dynamical fermions, moderate masses yield crossover (no singularity); largest accessible fermion masses show first evidence of first-order transition via discontinuous Polyakov loop behavior and latent heat signature in susceptibility.

Conclusion: Sp(4) with fundamental fermions may host a first-order thermal phase transition in the heavy-fermion regime—motivating high-precision continuum extrapolations and studies of Sp(2N) generalisations for GW phenomenology.

Abstract: We perform the first finite-temperature lattice study of the Sp(4) gauge theory coupled to two Wilson-Dirac fermions transforming in the fundamental representation. Its continuum limit plays an important role in many extensions of the Standard Model for particle physics with composite dynamics. For example, it provides a compelling realisation of the strongly interacting massive particle (SIMP) paradigm for dark matter. If realised in nature, it might lead to a first-order thermal phase transition in the early universe, and produce a detectable relic stochastic background of gravitational waves (GW). Assessing whether future experiments are sensitive to the resulting GW power spectrum requires determining the strength of the transition. We scan the parameter space of this lattice theory with a fine mesh of choices of lattice coupling, for two small values of the temporal extent of the lattice, and several choices of spatial volume and fermion mass. We measure the Polyakov loop and its susceptibility on the ensembles generated, implement reweighting, and perform a finite volume analysis, both to classify the nature of the phase transition, and to estimate the critical coupling. We validate our algorithms and analysis procedures on the better understood Sp(4) Yang-Mills theory, by confirming the presence of a first-order phase transition. Upon coupling the theory to matter fields, in the presence of moderately light fermions, the Polyakov loop shows a clear crossover behaviour and does not detect singularities associated with a phase transition. We also uncover the first evidence of a first-order thermal phase transition in the case of the largest fermion masses available. This finding motivates future, large-scale studies aimed at characterising the transition in the continuum limit, for this and closely related theories with Sp(2N) gauge group.

</details>


### [94] [Hadron structure on the 16-cell honeycomb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21543)
*Sandor Katz,Daniel Nogradi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on non-perturbative QCD simulations using the 4D 16-cell honeycomb lattice, demonstrating reduced cut-off effects and improved determination of hadronic structure functions due to enhanced symmetry and richer irreducible representation content compared to the cubic lattice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce cut-off effects and improve lattice determination of hadronic structure functions—particularly matrix elements of twist-2 and twist-3 operators—by exploiting the larger space-time symmetry and richer group representation structure of the 16-cell honeycomb lattice.

Method: Group-theoretic analysis of the 16-cell honeycomb lattice symmetry group; classification of operator matrix elements (polarized/unpolarized, forward/off-forward, twist-2/twist-3) according to irreducible representations; comparison with cubic lattice regarding mixing protection, power divergences, and computational efficiency for hadrons at rest.

Result: The 16-cell lattice allows determination of a larger set of moments of structure functions with continuum-like renormalization and without momentum boosting (i.e., at rest), due to greater representation multiplicity and reduced operator mixing.

Conclusion: The 16-cell honeycomb discretization offers superior symmetry properties for lattice QCD studies of hadronic structure functions, enabling more precise, less divergent, and computationally more efficient extraction of twist-2 and twist-3 matrix elements compared to the standard cubic lattice.

Abstract: In previous work we started a program of studying non-perturbative QCD on the 4-dimensional 16-cell honeycomb lattice instead of the usually adopted cubic lattice. It was shown that in several observables much smaller cut-off effects are observed, undoubtedly a result of the larger space-time symmetry of this discretization. In the present work further consequences are worked out, relevant for the lattice determination of hadronic structure functions. The key ingredient is the larger set of irreducible representations of the 16-cell honeycomb symmetry group relative to the cubic lattice, leading to more protection from mixing and thus less power divergences. A second ingredient is that more irreducible representations are available for a hadron at rest, which also leads to computational gain in practice. The analysis is given in full detail for both polarized and unpolarized and both forward and off-forward matrix elements of twist-2 and twist-3 operators. It is shown that a larger set of moments can be determined with continuum-like renormalization and at rest, than on the cubic lattice.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [95] [Confinement--Higgs Complementarity Viewed through Relative Quantum Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19519)
*Kei-Ichi Kondo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates confinement-Higgs complementarity using quantum reference frames (QRFs) derived from gauge and scalar fields, provides a relational QRF interpretation of CDG-reduction-based nonlocal separation lines in SU(2) lattice simulations, and demonstrates compatibility with Fradkin–Shenker continuity and Osterwalder–Seiler analyticity; it also shows that a Wilson-rooted observable yields exact results at zero plaquette coupling and does not induce such a separation line.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile confinement and Higgs mechanisms via a relational, gauge-invariant framework grounded in quantum reference frames, avoiding gauge fixing while explaining emergent nonlocal structures in lattice simulations.

Method: Constructs two independent quantum reference frames—one from the gauge field and one from the scalar field—and derives exact continuum and lattice identities; applies Cho–Duan–Ge (CDG) reduction to interpret scalar–color observables relationally; analyzes analyticity and continuity properties using Fradkin–Shenker and Osterwalder–Seiler theorems; compares with Wilson-rooted control observables.

Result: A QRF-based relational interpretation of CDG-induced nonlocal separation lines in radially fixed SU(2) gauge–scalar models; proof of compatibility with Fradkin–Shenker continuity and Osterwalder–Seiler analyticity for local gauge-invariant observables; exact analytical result for Wilson-rooted observable at zero plaquette coupling, showing absence of nonlocal separation.

Conclusion: Confinement–Higgs complementarity is naturally encoded in the relative orientation of gauge- and scalar-derived QRFs; the CDG-based nonlocal structure is physically meaningful and analytically consistent, whereas Wilson-rooted observables—though exactly solvable—do not capture this feature, highlighting the necessity of relational, scalar-informed QRFs for probing confinement–Higgs duality.

Abstract: We formulate confinement--Higgs complementarity in terms of the relative orientation between two independently constructed quantum reference frames (QRFs): one derived from the gauge field and the other from the scalar field. Exact continuum and lattice identities give a relational interpretation of the scalar--color observables used in recent gauge-fixing-free $SU(2)$ simulations. In particular, we provide a QRF interpretation of the additional gauge-invariant nonlocal separation line obtained from the Cho--Duan--Ge (CDG) reduction reported in lattice simulations of the radially fixed fundamental $SU(2)$ gauge--scalar model. We explain why such a candidate nonlocal boundary is compatible with Fradkin--Shenker continuity and Osterwalder--Seiler analyticity for the appropriate class of local gauge-invariant observables. Separately, we show that a Wilson-rooted control observable admits an exact results at zero plaquette coupling and that it does not cause such a nonlocal separation line.

</details>


### [96] [Wilsonian renormalization group flow in QED with the Pauli term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19778)
*Sorato Nagao,Hiroshi Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the RG flow of QED with a Pauli term using GFERG, finding a nontrivial large-$N_f$ fixed point with vanishing gauge coupling and nonzero Pauli coupling, but shows it disappears at physical $N_f=1$ due to one-loop fermion corrections; instead, the Pauli coupling is irrelevant and induced as $\kappa_{\text{ind}} \propto e^3$, while $e^2$ remains marginally irrelevant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether QED augmented by a Pauli term admits asymptotically safe or otherwise physically viable UV completions, especially given the role of the Pauli term in chiral symmetry breaking, magnetic moment anomalies, and effective field theories beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Uses gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG), which preserves the conventional gauge Ward--Takahashi identity exactly and isolates gauge coupling flow solely through the photon anomalous dimension $\gamma_A$; employs leading large-$N_f$ approximation and extends analysis to finite $N_f$, including one-loop fermionic contributions—especially additive mass renormalization $\propto \kappa^2$.

Result: A nontrivial fixed point $(e_*=0, \kappa_*\neq0, \gamma_A=-1)$ exists in the large-$N_f$ limit with three relevant directions; however, at finite $N_f$, particularly for $N_f=1$, this fixed point is eliminated unless $N_f \gtrsim \mathcal{O}(10)$; for $N_f=1$, the full RG flow shows $\kappa_\tau$ is irrelevant and flows to an induced value $\kappa_{\text{ind}}(e_\tau) \propto e_\tau^3$, while $e_\tau^2$ remains marginally irrelevant.

Conclusion: The Pauli term does not enable asymptotic safety in physical QED ($N_f = 1$); instead, it is dynamically suppressed at low energies, being induced perturbatively by the gauge coupling, reinforcing the view that the Pauli interaction is an effective, rather than fundamental, operator in minimal QED.

Abstract: We study the renormalization group (RG) flow of quantum electrodynamics (QED) with a Pauli term, $\kappa_\tau\Bar{\psi}\sigma_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}\psi$, by using the gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG). In the GFERG, the gauge Ward--Takahashi identity takes the conventional form and is exactly preserved, and the gauge coupling flows only through the photon anomalous dimension~$\gamma_A$. In the leading large-$N_f$ approximation, the vacuum polarization induced by the Pauli coupling yields a nontrivial fixed point with~$e_*=0$, $\kappa_*\neq0$, and~$\gamma_A=-1$. This fixed point has three relevant directions, as does one of the fixed points of the asymptotically safe QED proposed by Gies and Ziebell. At finite~$N_f$, however, the $N_f$-independent one-loop effects on the fermion line, in particular the additive mass renormalization proportional to~$\kappa^2$, eliminate this fixed point unless $N_f$ is at least several tens; for~$N_f=1$ there is no fixed point at weak Pauli coupling. Along the full flow for~$N_f=1$, $\kappa_\tau$ is irrelevant and is attracted to an induced value~$\kappa_{\text{ind}}(e_\tau)$, which is proportional to~$e_\tau^3$ at weak coupling, while $e_\tau^2$ is marginally irrelevant as in QED without the Pauli term.

</details>


### [97] [On reflection positivity of lattice chiral fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20084)
*Zhiyao Lu,Shu-Heng Shao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that the overlap Weyl fermion fails reflection positivity in 2D and 4D at finite lattice spacing, preventing standard OS reconstruction of a unitary quantum theory; in 2D, it also exhibits boundary-induced nonlocality linked to the gravitational anomaly—unlike the reflection-positive overlap Dirac fermion, whose axial symmetries exhibit distinct compactness/reflection-invariance trade-offs within an Onsager algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the viability of the overlap Weyl fermion as a rigorous lattice regularization for chiral gauge theories, particularly regarding foundational axiomatic requirements—reflection positivity and locality in bounded domains.

Method: Analytical examination of reflection positivity using the Osterwalder–Schrader framework; explicit analysis of the lattice action’s behavior under reflection and in the presence of boundaries; comparison with the overlap Dirac fermion and classification of axial symmetries via Onsager algebra structure.

Result: Overlap Weyl fermion violates reflection positivity in 2D and 4D; in 2D, its action becomes nonlocal with a boundary due to gravitational anomaly; overlap Dirac fermion is reflection positive but hosts mutually exclusive compact/reflection-invariant axial symmetries, unified in an Onsager algebra.

Conclusion: The overlap Weyl fermion cannot serve as a reflection-positive, local lattice regulator for unitary chiral fermionic theories in 2D/4D; this highlights intrinsic obstructions to lattice chiral symmetry beyond the Nielsen–Ninomiya theorem, rooted in gravitational anomalies and symmetry algebra constraints.

Abstract: We point out that the overlap Weyl fermion, the spacetime lattice realization of a Weyl fermion underlying Lüscher's construction of lattice chiral gauge theories, is not reflection positive in two and four dimensions at finite lattice spacing. Consequently, the standard Osterwalder--Schrader reconstruction does not yield a Hilbert space with a self-adjoint Hamiltonian. In two dimensions, we further show that the overlap Weyl fermion cannot be cut open: the lattice action becomes nonlocal in the presence of a boundary, as expected from the gravitational anomaly of the continuum field theory. By contrast, the overlap Dirac fermion is known to be reflection positive. However, its compact axial symmetry is not invariant under the reflection, whereas a reflection-invariant axial symmetry is noncompact, and both are part of an Onsager algebra.

</details>


### [98] [Thermal deep inelastic scattering, universality and complex geodesics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21127)
*Andrei Parnachev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies deep inelastic scattering (DIS) in strongly coupled quantum field theories at finite temperature using holography, revealing universal behavior of current two-point functions in the high-virtuality, near-lightcone thermal limit—linking absorption exponents, three-point couplings, conformal collider bounds, and geodesic propagation in the dual geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermal effects modify vacuum-dominated DIS kinematics in strongly coupled QFTs, particularly the crossover from exponential to power-law response at large photon virtuality, and to uncover universality in thermal correlators via holography.

Method: Holographic computation of finite-temperature two-point functions of conserved currents in the deep inelastic scattering regime; analysis of the near-lightcone limit of thermal CFT correlators; identification of universal dependence on stress-tensor three-point couplings; connection to conformal collider bounds and complex geodesics in the dual black hole geometry.

Result: Thermal response functions exhibit universal scaling controlled by a channel-dependent coefficient tied to current–stress-tensor OPE coefficients; saturation of conformal collider bounds implies vacuum-like behavior in the near-lightcone limit; low-temperature absorption exponent is determined by a complex geodesic in the near-lightcone AdS-black hole geometry.

Conclusion: The DIS regime at finite temperature in holographic CFTs reveals robust universality governed by conformal data and geometry, unifying thermal dynamics, collider bounds, and bulk geodesic structure—providing a geometric origin for absorption exponents and sharpening the link between boundary correlators and bulk physics.

Abstract: In deep inelastic scattering (DIS), a lepton scatters off a target hadron whose structure is probed by an exchanged photon with large spacelike virtuality. We consider two-point functions of currents in strongly interacting quantum field theories at finite temperature in this kinematic regime and use a holographic description to compute them. At large virtuality, the crossover from vacuum-dominated to thermally dominated physics corresponds to a transition from exponentially suppressed absorption to power-law behavior of the response functions. This regime is related to a particular near-lightcone limit of thermal CFT correlators. We show that the response functions exhibit a large degree of universality: the dependence on the three-point couplings of currents to the stress tensor enters through a channel-dependent coefficient multiplying the kinematic parameter that controls the relative size of vacuum and thermal effects. The same coefficient controls conformal collider bounds; when either bound is saturated, the corresponding response function takes its vacuum form in the near-lightcone limit. Furthermore, the absorption exponent in the low-temperature regime is related to a complex geodesic propagating in the near-lightcone geometry.

</details>


### [99] [Non-Topological Chiral Anomaly: The Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18841)
*Rémy Larue,Amaury Marchon,Jérémie Quevillon,Diego Saviot*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a novel non-topological contribution to the chiral anomaly in thermal media, leading to new transport effects—including the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect in Weyl semimetals—and establishes a generalized hydrodynamic framework for anomaly-induced phenomena.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the incompleteness of the conventional chiral anomaly description in thermal media, particularly for non-gauged axial symmetries, and to uncover new anomaly-driven transport beyond topological contributions.

Method: Derivation of the chiral anomaly in a thermal medium using quantum field-theoretic and hydrodynamic methods, including analysis of the axial current continuity equation and identification of non-topological terms independent of electromagnetic fields.

Result: Discovery of a non-topological, temperature-dependent contribution to the chiral anomaly; prediction of the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect—a magnetic-field-induced electric field in Weyl semimetals without external electric field; formulation of a broader relativistic hydrodynamic framework incorporating non-topological anomalies.

Conclusion: Non-topological chiral anomalies are physically significant and generically modify hydrodynamic constitutive relations, enabling new transport phenomena in both condensed-matter (e.g., Weyl semimetals) and high-energy systems.

Abstract: We derive the chiral anomaly in a thermal medium and show that, in addition to the well-established topological piece, the anomaly acquires a previously overlooked non-topological contribution, relevant for non-gauged axial symmetries. Remarkably, this modification of the continuity equation is independent of the electromagnetic fields, thereby giving rise to novel transport effects in relativistic hydrodynamics. We further propose an experimentally accessible new effect in Weyl semimetals which we call the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect: an electric field proportional to the magnetic field is generated by the non-topological anomaly, even in the absence of an externally applied electric field. Beyond this concrete prediction, our results establish a broader hydrodynamic framework for non-topological chiral anomalies, opening the door to a range of previously unexplored anomaly-induced transport phenomena across condensed-matter and high-energy systems.

</details>


### [100] [Triple Exchange at the Cosmological Collider in Closed Form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20356)
*Taushif Ahmed,Sumit Banik,Syed Mehedi Hasan,Andreas Rapakoulias*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives closed-form expressions for the quasi-single-field inflation bispectrum using Feynman-integral methods and multiple polylogarithms, improving upon the Planck template by correcting amplitude and mass biases; it also computes the collapsed trispectrum and shows tighter constraints on self-coupling at weak mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, closed-form expressions for the quasi-single-field triple exchange bispectrum—previously unavailable for light fields—and to improve observational templates used in CMB and large-scale structure analyses.

Method: Feynman-integral techniques applied to inflationary correlation functions, yielding exact momentum-dependent results expressed as expansions in multiple polylogarithms near the massless and conformally coupled limits.

Result: First closed-form bispectrum templates for quasi-single-field inflation; quantified up to 1.8× overestimation of amplitude and systematic mass underestimation in the Planck template; new template recovers true amplitude and mass; computed collapsed trispectrum and combined bispectrum–trispectrum analysis yields tighter bounds on field self-coupling at weak mixing.

Conclusion: Accurate closed-form bispectrum templates are essential for unbiased inference of particle mass and couplings from primordial non-Gaussianity; inclusion of trispectrum information enhances sensitivity to weakly coupled physics in future surveys.

Abstract: Particles with masses near the Hubble scale leave a characteristic pattern in the primordial bispectrum, turning inflation into a cosmological collider. For light fields, where this signal is largest, no closed form is known for the full shape of the quasi-single-field triple exchange. Using Feynman-integral methods, we obtain the first closed-form results, exact in the momenta, as expansions in terms of multiple polylogarithms around the massless and conformally coupled limits. We show that the template used in Planck analyses overestimates the amplitude by up to a factor of 1.8 and underestimates the mass, whereas our new template recovers both, as needed for current and upcoming CMB and galaxy surveys. We also compute the collapsed trispectrum and, combining it with the bispectrum, find that at weak mixing it bounds the self-coupling more tightly.

</details>


### [101] [Soft momentum expansion about the Stokes topological limit of Bogoliubov coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21271)
*Daniel J. H. Chung,Nidhi Sudhir,Junqi Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the soft-momentum expansion of particle production amplitude near the Stokes topological limit in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds, using exponentially improved asymptotics and discrete symmetries of a power-law dispersion relation; yields nonperturbative leading-order momentum dependence and a semi-analytic estimate of particle number density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analytically characterize particle production in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds near the recently discovered Stokes topological limit, where standard perturbative or WKB methods break down.

Method: Applies exponentially improved asymptotic expansions incorporating angular step functions intrinsic to Stokes sectors; exploits discrete symmetries of the dispersion relation ω_k²(z) = C(k) + A zⁿ (n even) to isolate the leading nonperturbative momentum dependence.

Result: Derives the leading soft-momentum behavior of the particle production amplitude—nonperturbative in the coupling A—and provides a semi-analytic expression for the resulting particle number density.

Conclusion: Stokes topology governs nonperturbative particle production in power-law time-dependent backgrounds; the method enables precise, analytic access to otherwise inaccessible regimes of quantum field dynamics in expanding spacetimes.

Abstract: It is known that particle production in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds can be computed using the Stokes phenomena occurring in Bogoliubov transformations with an analytically continued time coordinate. We analytically compute the soft momentum expansion of the particle production amplitude about the Stokes topological limit that was recently discovered in this context. The computational technique relies on an exponentially improved asymptotic expansion that involves automorphisms in the space of functions defined partly by the angular step function intrinsic to the Stokes topological sectors. We combine this with special discrete symmetries tied to the time dependence of the dispersion relation $\omega_k^2(z) = C(k) + A z^n$ for even $n$ to explicitly compute the leading momentum dependence of the amplitude which is nonperturbative in $A$. We also use this result to give a semi-analytic estimate of the number density of particles in this class of scenarios.

</details>


### [102] [Schwarzschild scattering from CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18826)
*Paolo Arnaudo,Benjamin Withers*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between retarded CFT correlators on ℝ×S² (dual to a small AdS₄ black hole) and the flat-space Schwarzschild S-matrix, including quasinormal modes (QNMs), via high-frequency boundary plane-wave behavior of bulk fields; it also presents a toy model illustrating how dissipative QNMs emerge transiently in Hermitian systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge holographic CFT descriptions of black holes with observable flat-space scattering data—specifically, to derive the asymptotically flat Schwarzschild S-matrix and its quasinormal mode spectrum from a controlled AdS/CFT setup.

Method: Identifies a class of CFT operators whose dual bulk fields approximate plane waves near the AdS boundary at high frequencies; exploits analytic continuation and the large-AdS-radius (L → ∞) limit to map retarded CFT correlators to reflection coefficients and hence to flat-space partial-wave S-matrix elements; includes analysis for spin-0 (conformally coupled), spin-1, and spin-2 fields; constructs a minimal quantum-mechanical toy model exhibiting transient QNM-like decay in a unitary system.

Result: Derives exact asymptotically flat Schwarzschild partial-wave S-matrix elements—including poles corresponding to quasinormal mode frequencies—from AdS₄/CFT₃ correlators; establishes an analytic, well-behaved map between CFT retarded correlators and flat-space reflection coefficients in the L → ∞ limit; demonstrates that QNMs can arise as short-time approximations in Hermitian systems, validating their effective interpretation in holography.

Conclusion: Retarded CFT correlators in the small black hole background encode full flat-space scattering data for Schwarzschild, confirming a concrete realization of flat-space holography from AdS/CFT; the appearance of QNMs is understood as an emergent, transient phenomenon tied to spectral deformation under boundary conditions—not fundamental non-Hermiticity.

Abstract: We show that retarded CFT correlators on $\mathbb{R}\times \mathbb{S}^2$ in a state described by the small black hole in AdS$_4$ can be used to derive asymptotically flat Schwarzschild partial-wave S-matrix elements, including its spectrum of quasinormal modes. This is achieved by identifying a class of operators for which the dual bulk fields behave as plane waves near the AdS boundary for sufficiently high frequencies. For such operators there is a simple analytic relation between CFT correlators and reflection coefficients which behaves gracefully in the flat space limit, $L\to \infty$. These include spin-1, spin-2, and conformally coupled scalars. We provide a simple toy model that captures how dissipative QNMs can appear for short times inside Hermitian systems, closely resembling the small black hole in AdS.

</details>


### [103] [Gravitational Memory Correlators and BMS Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18827)
*Enrico Herrmann,Ian Moult,Brett Oertel,Julio Parra-Martinez,Biswajit Sahoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar decay into gravitons using the in-in formalism in perturbative quantum gravity, and verifies the supertranslation flux balance relation linking memory, energy, and Bondi mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize asymptotic symmetry constraints—specifically supertranslation invariance—on infrared-finite observables in quantum gravity, by studying memory and energy correlators in gravitational radiation from scalar decay.

Method: In-in formalism at leading order in perturbative quantum gravity; computation of n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar-to-graviton decay.

Result: Compact analytic expressions for arbitrary-n correlators; explicit verification of the supertranslation flux balance relation among memory, energy flux, and Bondi mass detectors.

Conclusion: The supertranslation symmetry is realized as a constraint on infrared-finite observables, confirming consistency between soft theorems, memory effects, and asymptotic symmetries in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We compute the n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar decay into gravitons using the in-in formalism at leading order in perturbative quantum gravity. We obtain compact results for arbitrary n and explicitly verify the supertranslation flux balance relation between memory, energy, and Bondi mass detectors, realizing asymptotic symmetry constraints on infrared-finite observables.

</details>


### [104] [Rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models from a tetrality web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18831)
*António Antunes,Jin Chen,Junchen Rong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising (TIM) models using chiral symmetry folding, $S_3$-based 'moving up/down' procedures, revealing a tetrality web of four CFTs; it identifies 24 distinct boundary states (18 factorized + 6 transmitting), computes their energy transmission ($35/48$), boundary entropies, bulk correlations, operator spectra, and full annulus amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models—two paradigmatic minimal CFTs—with controlled symmetry structure and physical interpretability (e.g., RG flows, duality walls).
