Method: Folding construction with distinct chiral symmetries; 'moving up' to an auxiliary coset CFT with $S_3$ automorphism yielding twisted Cardy states; 'moving down' to the $S_3$-invariant subalgebra; pullback of Cardy states; explicit computation of boundary entropies, transmission coefficients, bulk correlators, operator content, and annulus partition functions.

Result: Discovery of a tetrality web linking four CFTs; derivation of 18 factorized and 6 transmitting interfaces between Ising and TIM; identification of Gaiotto's RG wall and its duality-fused variant among the six transmitting interfaces; uniform energy transmission coefficient $35/48$; full classification via boundary entropy, spectrum, and annulus amplitudes.

Conclusion: Rational conformal interfaces between Ising and TIM are fully classified by $S_3$-symmetric constructions within a tetrality web; the six transmitting interfaces form a regular representation of the TIM fusion ring and share universal transport properties but are distinguished by finer boundary CFT data.

Abstract: We construct rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising (TIM) models by using different chiral symmetries to prepare their folded boundary states. We use a moving up procedure which extends the product theory to an auxiliary coset with an $S_3$ automorphism group, where permutations give twisted Cardy states. We complement this with a moving down approach which reduces to the $S_3$ invariant algebra. Overall, these procedures uncover a tetrality web relating four different theories. Pulling back ordinary Cardy states of the smallest theory in the web to the physical tensor product system gives eighteen factorized interfaces and six transmitting interfaces. The latter agree with four transposition and two 3-cycle twisted boundaries in the largest theory. They include Gaiotto's RG wall and the interface obtained by fusing an Ising duality line into it. The six interfaces carry the regular action of the TIM fusion ring and have the same energy transmission coefficient, $35/48$. Their boundary entropies, bulk correlations and operator spectra distinguish them. We calculate these observables and the complete annulus amplitudes between the twenty four boundaries.

</details>


### [105] [Constants and Nonperturbative Corrections in $V^{5,2}$ Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18832)
*Seyed Morteza Hosseini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes perturbative and exponentially small nonperturbative corrections to the on-shell effective twisted superpotentials of two 3D N=2 theories linked to cyclic quotients of the Sasaki–Einstein manifold V^{5,2}; it reveals shared modular structures in nonperturbative coefficients but distinct perturbative shifts and exponential corrections, verified via Bethe ansatz and M-theory localization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve subtle differences between two 3D N=2 theories—two-node Chern-Simons (k,−k) and one-node flavored (N_f fundamentals)—that share leading N^{3/2} holographic behavior but differ beyond that, by characterizing their full perturbative and nonperturbative (exponentially small) structure in twisted superpotentials.

Method: Combines exact Bethe ansatz solutions, Borel resummation, modular analysis of Eichler integrals of weight-four Eisenstein series, high-precision numerical evaluation using Clausen functions and ζ(3), and M-theory localization on squashed S^3 with b→0^+ limit; employs relative localization for bulk reconstruction and fixed-locus quotient geometry.

Result: Derives convergent integral representations for rank-independent constants; obtains explicit finite-sum evaluations at integer k, N_f involving Clausen functions and ζ(3); conjectures complete exponential remainders as Eichler integrals at k,N_f = 1,3; identifies minimal modular levels of Eisenstein generators; recovers Chern-Simons shifted rank and constant from squashed-sphere partition function; generalizes to include fixed-locus contributions for flavored theory.

Conclusion: The two theories share a deep modular structure in their nonperturbative coefficient laws, yet are distinguished by theory-specific perturbative shifts and exponential corrections—resolving apparent degeneracy in their leading N^{3/2} behavior and confirming consistency across gauge theory, integrability, and M-theory localization.

Abstract: We study perturbative and exponentially small corrections to the on-shell effective twisted superpotentials of two three-dimensional $\mathcal N=2$ theories associated with free and fixed-locus cyclic quotients of the non-toric Sasaki-Einstein manifold $V^{5,2}$. These are the two-node Chern-Simons theory at levels $(k,-k)$ and the one-node flavored theory with $N_f$ fundamental pairs, respectively. At superconformal chemical potentials, we propose the Chern-Simons counterpart of the all-order perturbative expression for the flavored theory and obtain convergent integral representations for the rank-independent constants of both theories. At integer parameters, these constants admit explicit evaluations involving finite sums of Clausen functions and $\zeta(3)$, while their large-parameter expansions Borel sum to the defining integrals. At $k=1,3$ and $N_f=1,3$, we conjecture complete exponential remainders expressed through Eichler integrals of weight-four Eisenstein series. Supported by high-precision Bethe solutions, the resulting completions coincide at unit quotient order but differ at order three. We determine the minimal modular levels of the Eisenstein generators and establish convergence and natural boundaries for their primitives. Within the relative M-theory localization prescription, a complementary bulk analysis recovers the Chern-Simons shifted rank and constant in the $b\to0^+$ limit of the squashed-sphere partition function. We propose a generalization incorporating fixed-locus contributions that yields the corresponding flavored-theory result. The two theories therefore share a modular structure in their nonperturbative coefficient laws, but distinct perturbative shifts and exponential corrections resolve differences invisible in their common leading $N^{3/2}$ behavior.

</details>


### [106] [Quadratic Sum Rules for Scalar Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18836)
*Karol Kampf,Jiří Novotný,David Podivín,Jonah Stalknecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper discovers new quadratic-sum rules linking scalar amplitudes across different multiplicities, derived via recursion, kinematic shifts, and symbolic regression; these rules generalize from the nonlinear sigma model to string-inspired extensions, inverse KLT matrix diagonal elements, and planar scalar theories with arbitrary nonderivative interactions and propagators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the success of known amplitude relations (e.g., KK, BCJ, string monodromy) by uncovering novel quadratic-sum constraints that connect scalar amplitudes across multiplicities.

Method: Combines on-shell recursion, kinematic shifts (e.g., BCFW-like), and symbolic regression to conjecture and verify quadratic-sum identities; systematically generalizes them across multiple theories: NLSM, its stringy deformations, diagonal entries of the inverse KLT matrix, and generic planar scalar theories with nonderivative interactions and arbitrary propagators.

Result: Derives a new class of universal quadratic-sum rules for scalar amplitudes; proves their validity for the nonlinear sigma model and its string-inspired variants; extends them to diagonal elements of the inverse KLT matrix; and further generalizes to arbitrary planar scalar theories with nonderivative interactions and general propagators.

Conclusion: Quadratic-sum rules constitute a broader, previously unexplored class of amplitude constraints—complementary to linear BCJ/KK relations—with wide applicability across scalar effective field theories, string theory, and double-copy constructions.

Abstract: Kleiss--Kuijf and Bern--Carrasco--Johansson relations, and their string monodromy generalizations, have long provided some of the most powerful and useful constraints on partial amplitudes. Motivated by their success, we uncover a new class of quadratic-sum rules that connect scalar amplitudes at different multiplicities. Combining recursion, kinematic shifts, and symbolic regression, we establish them for the nonlinear sigma model and its string-inspired extensions. We then extend these relations to diagonal elements of the inverse Kawai--Lewellen--Tye matrix and subsequently to planar scalar theories with nonderivative interactions and general propagators.

</details>


### [107] [Generalised global symmetries of 6d SCFTs on a circle from the blow-up equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18837)
*William Harding,Noppadol Mekareeya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies 6d (1,0) SCFTs compactified on a circle (with possible twist) via their 5d blow-up equations, establishing a precise map between 6d and 5d frames—including the KK/instanton mass ratio r—and shows that higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-groups are fully encoded in fluxes through the exceptional curve of the blown-up C²; tensor fluxes encode Green–Schwarz couplings and fractional exponents yield mixed anomalies, while forced fluxes distinguish 2-groups from mixed anomalies, with explicit examples across su(4) and E₁-based theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-groups of 6d (1,0) SCFTs upon circle compactification—especially in twisted setups—by leveraging the geometric structure of 5d blow-up equations and flux quantization on the resolved C² singularity.

Method: Uses blow-up equations of 5d effective theories descending from circle-compactified 6d (1,0) SCFTs; constructs explicit 6d–5d frame maps including the KK/instanton mass ratio r; interprets fluxes through the exceptional curve of the blown-up C² as 't Hooft sectors of 5d 1-form symmetries; extracts symmetry data (2-groups, anomalies) from tensor flux constraints and Green–Schwarz couplings; applies to su(4)_k + matter and SU(2)-gauged E₁ ensembles.

Result: Higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-groups of the 6d theories are fully determined by exceptional-curve fluxes; tensor flux weights fixed by Green–Schwarz terms yield fractional exponents interpreted as mixed anomalies between 6d 2-form symmetry and KK U(1), gravity, flavor, and SU(2)_R; forced fluxes distinguish non-split 2-groups (e.g., Z₄ in twisted su(4)₀,₄+4Λ²) from pure mixed anomalies (e.g., su(4)₂+4Λ²); extends to SU(2)-gauged E₁ theories, revealing 5d 2-groups descending from 6d 3-groups and identifying symmetry-breaking states and background lockings.

Conclusion: The geometry of the blown-up C² provides a unified, computable framework for classifying global symmetry structures—including 2-groups and mixed anomalies—of circle-compactified 6d (1,0) SCFTs, bridging 6d anomaly inflow, 5d gauge dynamics, and topological flux quantization.

Abstract: We study 6d $(1,0)$ SCFTs on a circle, possibly twisted, through the blow-up equations of their 5d descriptions. For every theory we consider, we construct the explicit map between the 6d and 5d frames, including the ratio $r$ of the Kaluza--Klein (KK) mass to the instanton mass, which depends on the frame and not only on the twist. Our main result is that the higher-form symmetries of these theories, their mixed anomalies and 2-groups can all be read off from the fluxes through the exceptional curve of the blown-up $\mathbb{C}^2$. The flux sectors are the 't$~$Hooft sectors of the 5d 1-form symmetries. On the tensor fluxes, the 6d Green--Schwarz couplings fix the weights, whose fractional exponents we read as the mixed anomalies of the 6d 2-form symmetry with the KK $\mathrm{U}(1)$, gravity, flavour and $\mathrm{SU}(2)_R$. The fluxes forced in each sector decide between a 2-group and a mixed anomaly. For instance, the twisted frames $\mathfrak{su}(4)_{0,4}+4\Lambda^2$ carry a non-split $\mathbb{Z}_4$ 2-group, while, in $\mathfrak{su}(4)_2+4\Lambda^2$ and $\mathfrak{su}(4)_6+2\Lambda^2$, the instanton symmetry is locked to the flavour centre and only a mixed anomaly remains. The same method applies to the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ gauging of two, three and four copies of the $E_1$ theory, which describes, respectively, $\mathrm{SU}(4)_0$, pure $\mathrm{Spin}(8)$ and the untwisted $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ KK theory. There, the fluxes exhibit the extensions of 1-form symmetries inherited from the $E_1$ 2-groups, identify the states that break each accidental 1-form symmetry and the background to which it is locked, and give the 5d 2-group to which the $S_3$-invariant 6d 3-group reduces.

</details>


### [108] [A Bound on Wormhole Probing Time from Holographic Multi-Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19035)
*Takanori Anegawa,Norihiro Iizuka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper proves that increasing the number of parties in multi-entropy probes cannot indefinitely extend the time during which wormhole growth is detectable in nonrotating BTZ black holes; a finite upper bound exists for all fixed boundary partitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether higher-partite entanglement measures (e.g., genuine tripartite multi-entropy) can indefinitely track eternal wormhole growth after bipartite entanglement entropy saturates.

Method: Uses the multiway maximin prescription for holographic entanglement, assumes regular spacetime-stationary networks on Cauchy slices, and constructs a comparison network that assigns the entire wormhole interior to a single boundary party to derive a uniform bound on probing time.

Result: Establishes a finite upper bound on the probing time for wormhole growth—valid for any fixed finite boundary partition, regardless of number, sizes, or arrangement of parties—in nonrotating BTZ black holes.

Conclusion: No, increasing the number of parties does not indefinitely extend probing time; beyond a universal finite time, no maximin-selected network enters the black-hole interior, so multi-entropy cannot track further wormhole growth.

Abstract: Bipartite entanglement entropy eventually saturates and therefore cannot track the continued growth of an eternal wormhole. Recently, it was shown that genuine tripartite multi-entropy can probe wormhole growth even after bipartite entanglement entropies have saturated. This naturally raises the question: can increasing the number of parties extend the probing time indefinitely? Adopting the multiway maximin prescription and assuming that its selected representatives are regular spacetime-stationary networks, we prove that the answer is no for nonrotating BTZ black holes. We establish a common finite upper bound on the probing time for every fixed finite boundary partition, independent of the number, sizes, and arrangement of its parties. For any network on any Cauchy slice, there exists a comparison network that assigns the entire central region containing the wormhole interior to a single party. A network longer than this comparison network cannot minimize length on that slice. The central length required by a stationary network connecting the two boundaries grows with time, whereas the central length of the comparison network remains uniformly bounded. At sufficiently late times, such a network therefore cannot minimize length on any Cauchy slice containing it and is excluded from the maximin prescription. Consequently, beyond this bound, no network selected by maximin enters the black-hole interior.

</details>


### [109] [Projected DKP spin-1 sectors in a $(2+1)$-dimensional static wormhole: Self-duality, helicity content, and spectral correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19058)
*Luis B. Castro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the projected spin-1 Duffin–Kemmer–Petiau (DKP) equation in 2+1 dimensions, revealing dual irreducible representations linked to conjugate self-dual curl sectors and opposite massive helicities; it then applies this framework to static wormholes, uncovering a novel bound state at ω² = M² absent in master-variable approaches, and establishes Darboux equivalence between generic sectors with physically paired quasinormal modes (ω, −ω*).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the physical interpretation of the two inequivalent 3×3 irreducible representations of the spin-1 DKP algebra in 2+1 dimensions, and to resolve inconsistencies in prior wormhole analyses that used only one representation.

Method: Algebraic projection of the DKP equation onto irreducible subspaces; use of algebraic duality between projected antisymmetric tensor and vector representations; analysis of covariant-curl self-duality conditions; construction and spectral analysis of the first-order radial DKP system for static wormhole geometry; Darboux transformation between master and circular sector equations; pole analysis of quasinormal modes in the complex radial-momentum plane.

Result: The two 3×3 DKP representations correspond to conjugate self-dual covariant-curl sectors, yielding opposite massive helicities in flat space; a previously overlooked normalizable bound state exists at ω² = M² in the first-order radial DKP system; generic sectors are Darboux-equivalent, preserving reflection/transmission; quasinormal modes form two pole families with physical pairing (ω, −ω*), not symmetric ±ω.

Conclusion: The full physical content of spin-1 DKP fields in 2+1 dimensions requires both irreducible representations; their interplay is essential for correctly capturing bound states and quasinormal mode structure in topological spacetimes like wormholes—highlighting limitations of single-representation or second-order master-variable reductions.

Abstract: We use the projected spin-1 Duffin--Kemmer--Petiau (DKP) equation in $2+1$ dimensions to clarify the physical content of the irreducible $3\times3$ representations. The projected antisymmetric tensor is algebraically dual to the projected vector, and the two twin representations select conjugate self-dual covariant-curl sectors, which reduce to opposite massive helicities in the flat-space limit. We then apply this structure to the static wormhole problem previously studied with a single irreducible representation. The first-order radial DKP system contains an exceptional normalizable bound-state branch at $\omega^2=M^2$ that is absent from the master-variable reduction, whereas the generic master and circular sectors are related by a Darboux transformation. The latter preserves the transmission and reflection probabilities, while the quasinormal modes are organized into two pole families in the complex radial-momentum plane, leading to the physical pairing $(\omega,-\omega^*)$ rather than a global $\pm\omega$ prescription.

</details>


### [110] [Stochastic gauge fixing from the holographic renormalization group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19083)
*Soner Albayrak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the stochastic quantization–holographic Wilsonian renormalization dictionary to 4D AdS Yang-Mills theory at tree level, identifying the radial gauge field component as Zwanziger’s gauge-fixing function and radial gauge with Parisi–Wu quantization; it derives finite-cutoff triple- and quadruple-trace gluon couplings in closed form, with transverse couplings depending on a single bulk zero mode that vanishes in the flat-space limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify stochastic quantization and holographic Wilsonian renormalization for non-Abelian gauge theories—specifically Yang-Mills—in asymptotically AdS spacetime, thereby deepening the AdS/CFT correspondence for off-shell and coarse-grained quantum dynamics.

Method: Uses tree-level holographic renormalization in 4D anti-de Sitter space, identifies radial gauge field component with Zwanziger's gauge-fixing function, interprets radial gauge as Parisi–Wu stochastic quantization, computes bulk longitudinal propagators as random walks of gauge modes, and derives finite-cutoff multi-trace couplings via boundary effective action construction.

Result: Obtains explicit closed-form expressions for triple- and quadruple-trace gluon couplings at finite radial cutoff; shows transverse couplings depend only on a single bulk zero mode of the gauge field, which decouples in the flat-space (zero-curvature) limit.

Conclusion: The stochastic–holographic dictionary generalizes robustly to 4D AdS Yang-Mills at tree level; radial quantization provides a geometric realization of Parisi–Wu stochastic dynamics, and multi-trace couplings are fully controlled by bulk gauge zero-mode structure, reinforcing the role of radial direction as an RG scale.

Abstract: We extend the dictionary between stochastic quantization and holographic Wilsonian renormalization to Yang-Mills theory in four-dimensional anti-de Sitter space at tree level. The radial component of the bulk gauge field is Zwanziger's gauge-fixing function; radial gauge is Parisi-Wu quantization, whose random walk of gauge modes is the longitudinal bulk propagator. We obtain the triple- and quadruple-trace couplings of gluons at finite cutoff in closed form. On transverse data, their dependence on the bulk gauge resides in a single zero mode, which drops out of the flat-space limit.

</details>


### [111] [Conditional Principle of Least Action: Gauge Algebra and Noether's Second Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19097)
*V. A. Abakumova,S. L. Lyakhovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Noether's second theorem and its converse by analyzing how gauge symmetry algebras of constraint differential equations relate to those of the action restricted to fields satisfying those constraints, illustrated via linearized gravity with Cotton equation constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between gauge symmetries of differential constraints on fields and gauge symmetries of the action restricted to those constrained fields, enabling a generalized Noether-type correspondence.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic approach: derivation of algebraic relations between gauge symmetry algebras of constraint equations and restricted action; application of cohomological and variational bicomplex techniques implicit in constrained extremum formulation.

Result: A generalized Noether's second theorem and its converse are derived, linking identities among equations of motion for constrained fields to gauge symmetries of both constraints and action; verified explicitly in linearized gravity with Cotton equation constraints.

Conclusion: The gauge symmetry algebra of the constraints fully determines (and is determined by) that of the restricted action, establishing a structural duality that generalizes classical Noether correspondence to conditionally extremal field configurations.

Abstract: We consider the problem of a conditional extremum of an action within a class of fields constrained by differential equations. For this setup, we establish the interconnection between the gauge symmetry algebra of the equations constraining the admissible fields and that of the action restricted to this class of fields. Based on this relation between the two gauge algebras, we derive an extension of Noether's second theorem and its converse. The general method is illustrated by the example of linearized gravity with the Einstein-Hilbert action and the class of admissible metrics restricted by the Cotton equation.

</details>


### [112] [Classification of Conformal Ising/Tricritical Ising Interfaces Preserving $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$ Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19129)
*Bufan Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models preserving the chiral algebra $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$, identifies 24 fundamental boundary states via the folding trick and Cardy condition, constructs six symmetry-breaking interfaces explicitly, computes their annulus partition functions, and relates them via topological defect line dressings—including Gaiotto's RG domain wall.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct conformal interfaces between two distinct minimal models (Ising and tricritical Ising) that preserve an extended chiral symmetry ($\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$), motivated by understanding symmetry-preserving boundaries, RG flows, and topological defects in rational CFTs.

Method: Employs the folding trick to map interfaces to boundary states in a folded CFT; imposes the Cardy condition to constrain consistent boundary states; determines the unique NIM-rep compatible with the bulk modular invariant partition function; constructs explicit interface operators for symmetry-breaking cases; computes annulus partition functions; analyzes equivalence under topological defect line (TDL) actions.

Result: Finds exactly 24 fundamental conformal interfaces: 18 factorized (preserving full product Virasoro symmetry), and 6 non-factorized (breaking product Virasoro symmetry); fully constructs the latter six, computes their annulus partition functions, shows they form a TDL-orbit, and identifies one as Gaiotto's proposed RG domain wall.

Conclusion: The classification is complete and exact within the $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$-preserving sector; the six non-factorized interfaces represent genuinely new, symmetry-breaking conformal interfaces tied to RG flow physics and topological defect structure in the Ising–tricritical Ising system.

Abstract: We classify conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models that preserve the chiral algebra $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$. Using the folding trick, we formulate the problem in terms of boundary states satisfying the Cardy condition and determine the unique NIM-rep compatible with the bulk partition function. We obtain a complete set of 24 fundamental boundary states, of which 18 correspond to factorized interfaces preserving the full product Virasoro symmetry, while the remaining six break this symmetry. We construct these six interfaces explicitly and determine their annulus partition functions. They are related by topological defect line dressings, and one coincides with the RG domain wall proposed by Gaiotto.

</details>


### [113] [Large-$N$ expansion in SU($N$) Yang-Mills-like theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19144)
*Marco Bochicchio,Mauro Papinutto,Francesco Scardino*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the large-N expansion in SU(N) vs. U(N) Yang-Mills-like theories, showing that the standard 't Hooft expansion—based on smooth Riemann surfaces—is incomplete for SU(N); it must be extended to include singular, pinched topologies (e.g., pinched tori) for correlators of higher-twist quasi-partonic operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve subtleties in the large-N expansion when passing from U(N) to SU(N) YM-like theories, particularly how decoupling of the U(1) factor and renormalization affect topological classification of Feynman diagrams.

Method: Systematic topological analysis of the large-N expansion; comparison of diagrammatic genus expansions in U(N) and SU(N) settings; interpretation of missing contributions as singular (pinched) Riemann surfaces arising from renormalized sector separation (SU(N) interacting vs. U(1) free).

Result: The standard 't Hooft expansion over smooth orientable n-punctured Riemann surfaces is generically incomplete for SU(N) and renormalized U(N) YM-like theories; extra contributions correspond to singular, pinched surfaces—generalizing earlier results for twist-2 to higher-twist quasi-partonic operators.

Conclusion: The large-N topology in SU(N) YM-like theories requires extending the 't Hooft expansion beyond smooth surfaces to include singular (pinched) topologies, reflecting intrinsic differences from U(N) due to U(1) decoupling and renormalization-induced sector separation.

Abstract: It has been known for fifty years that bare U($N$) pure Yang-Mills (YM) theory admits the large-$N$ 't Hooft expansion for the $n$-point connected correlators of gauge-invariant single-trace operators, where the corresponding Feynman diagrams are topologically classified by the sum over the genus $g$ of $n$-punctured smooth orientable closed Riemann surfaces. In fact, 't Hooft expansion extends verbatim to U($N$) YM-like theories whose Lagrangians only involve fields valued in the U($N$) Lie algebra - including supersymmetric (SUSY) extensions of pure YM theory such as $\mathcal{N}=1,4$ SUSY YM. The aim of the present paper is to systematically work out the topology of the large-$N$ expansion in SU($N$) - as opposed to U($N$) - YM-like theories, where a number of subtleties inevitably arise. Indeed, since the U($1$) inside the U($N$) YM-like theories decouples, the large-$N$ U($N$) and SU($N$) expansions should either essentially coincide or intrinsically differ - in the latter case necessarily by extra - possibly singular - topologies, as the 't Hooft expansion in U($N$) YM-like theories already exhausts all the smooth topologies. In fact, we demonstrate that 't Hooft smooth topologies are actually generically incomplete in the large-$N$ expansion of both the SU($N$) and renormalized U($N$) YM-like theories, where renormalization inevitably separates the SU($N$) sector that is interacting from the U($1$) sector that is free. Specifically, we interpret the extra topologies that arise in SU($N$) theories for correlators of higher-twist quasi-partonic operators as singular pinched closed surfaces, thus extending our previous investigation of correlators of twist-$2$ operators for which extra pinched tori arise.

</details>


### [114] [The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ twist line defect of the 3D Ising CFT on the fuzzy sphere from multi-target density matrix renormalization group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19233)
*Jing-Hao Jin,Yang Qi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a multi-target DMRG method on the fuzzy sphere to study the ℤ₂ twist line defect in the 3D Ising CFT, enabling high-precision extraction of defect operator dimensions beyond exact diagonalization limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conformal defects in 3D CFTs have been limited to small-system exact diagonalization on the fuzzy sphere; a scalable, high-precision method is needed to access larger systems and resolve higher-lying defect primaries.

Method: Imposes antiperiodic boundary conditions on the ℤ₂-odd fermion flavor to realize the twist line defect on the fuzzy sphere, and employs multi-target DMRG to simultaneously optimize several low-lying eigenstates per symmetry sector in a shared matrix-product state basis.

Result: Computes defect spectrum up to system size 2s+1 = 25 (49 orbitals); confirms spin-parity selection rule and conformal multiplet structure; identifies finite-size corrections using displacement operator, its protected descendant, and bulk stress tensor; obtains controlled continuum extrapolations yielding sharpened dimensions for known defect primaries and resolving four new ones.

Conclusion: The combination of fuzzy-sphere regularization and multi-target DMRG constitutes a robust, scalable framework for precision spectroscopy of conformal defects in 3D CFTs.

Abstract: The fuzzy-sphere regularization provides a powerful framework for studying three-dimensional (3D) conformal field theories (CFTs) through radial quantization, but conformal defects have so far been studied with it only by exact diagonalization (ED) of small systems. In this work we realize the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ twist line defect of the 3D Ising CFT on the fuzzy sphere by imposing antiperiodic boundary conditions on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$-odd fermion flavor, and solve it with a multi-target density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) algorithm that optimizes several low-lying states of each symmetry sector in a common matrix-product basis. This gives the defect spectrum at system sizes well beyond the reach of ED (up to $2s+1=25$, or $49$ spinless orbitals). The spectrum obeys the spin-parity selection rule of the twist line and organizes into conformal multiplets along the line. Using the displacement operator as an intrinsic ruler, its protected descendant as a test, and the bulk stress tensor as an independent cross-check, we identify the leading finite-size corrections and obtain controlled continuum extrapolations, which sharpen the dimensions of several known defect primaries and resolve four higher-lying ones. This study establishes the fuzzy sphere combined with multi-target DMRG as a framework for precision studies of conformal defects.

</details>


### [115] [Holographic Entanglement after a Global Quench in Noncommutative Yang-Mills Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19307)
*Elahe Hassanpour,Komeil Babaei Velni,M. Reza Mohammadi Mozaffar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how holographic entanglement entropy and mixed-state correlations evolve after a global quench in 4D N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory using Vaidya geometry; it identifies distinct early-time growth regimes (logarithmic, linear, quadratic) for strip-shaped regions aligned with the noncommutative direction, links growth coefficients and velocities to noncommutativity and energy density, analyzes equilibration via butterfly velocity and competing extremal surface branches, and shows mutual information cancels leading log terms while entanglement wedge cross section exhibits linear growth governed by the same entanglement velocity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum entanglement and correlations dynamically evolve following a global quantum quench in a noncommutative gauge theory—specifically, how spacetime noncommutativity modifies universal features of holographic entanglement growth, saturation, and information spreading.

Method: Holographic computation using a Vaidya-AdS black brane background dual to a global quench in four-dimensional N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory; extremal surface prescription for entanglement entropy and entanglement wedge cross section; analytic and geometric analysis of time-dependent minimal surfaces for strip-shaped and disjoint boundary regions; identification of dominant surface branches and their competition during thermalization.

Result: Two early-time growth regimes (logarithmic then quadratic) for entanglement entropy of strips along the noncommutative direction; emergence of an intermediate linear regime for wide strips, with entanglement velocity increasing with noncommutativity; butterfly velocity governs continuous extremal surface evolution during equilibration; competing surface branches affect physical saturation time; mutual information eliminates leading width-independent logarithmic divergence; entanglement wedge cross section shows linear growth with same entanglement velocity if connected wedge dominates.

Conclusion: Noncommutativity qualitatively enriches the time evolution of holographic information measures: it introduces new scaling regimes, modifies characteristic velocities (entanglement and butterfly), and alters thermalization dynamics through branch competition—demonstrating that spacetime noncommutativity is a controllable deformation that probes the interplay between geometry, quantum chaos, and entanglement structure in holography.

Abstract: The time evolution of holographic entanglement entropy and mixed-state correlations following a global quench in four-dimensional N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory is investigated using a Vaidya geometry. For strip-shaped regions extended along a noncommutative direction, two distinct early-time growth regimes emerge, depending on the relation between the shell-crossing and noncommutative scales: logarithmic growth, with a coefficient proportional to the injected energy density and the square of the noncommutative scale, followed by quadratic growth. For sufficiently wide strips, an intermediate linear-growth regime appears, governed by an entanglement velocity that increases with noncommutativity. The approach to equilibrium is also examined, revealing the role of the butterfly velocity in the continuous evolution of extremal surfaces, while competing branches can modify the physical saturation time. For two disjoint strips, the leading width-independent logarithmic contribution cancels in the mutual information, whereas the entanglement wedge cross section exhibits an intermediate linear-growth regime governed by the same entanglement velocity, provided the connected entanglement wedge remains dominant.

</details>


### [116] [On the Spontaneous symmetry breaking of Conformal Gravity to Weyl and Einstein Gravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19338)
*Partha Nandi,Gerasimos Kouniatalis,Stelios Stefas,George Zoupanos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the role of the special-conformal connection in an SO(2,4) gauge-theoretic formulation of gravity, showing it remains auxiliary and its elimination yields Weyl gravity (up to Euler density) on a source-free branch; it further analyzes linearized spectra around de Sitter space and discusses symmetry breaking to Einstein gravity via an antisymmetric scalar 6-plet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the special-conformal connection stays auxiliary upon promoting a spontaneously broken conformal gauge theory to a fully dynamical Yang-Mills-Higgs framework, and under what conditions eliminating it recovers Weyl gravity.

Method: Uses SO(2,4) gauge-theoretic gravity with spontaneous symmetry breaking via an adjoint 15-plet; retains special-conformal connection as independent field; derives its algebraic equation of motion; eliminates it to obtain Weyl-squared action; extends analysis to full local Yang-Mills-Higgs theory using invariant bilinear form of so(2,4); studies linearized spectrum around de Sitter spacetime; examines further symmetry breaking induced by antisymmetric scalar 6-plet.

Result: Special-conformal connection is auxiliary in both pure and Yang-Mills-Higgs extended theories due to so(2,4)'s bilinear form structure; its elimination yields the Weyl-squared action (plus Euler density) without imposing Schouten relation; exact Weyl reduction holds only on source-free branch; linearized spectrum includes massless and partially massless spin-2 modes at m_PM² = 2Λ/3; antisymmetric scalar 6-plet triggers transition from Weyl-gravity to Einstein-gravity phase.

Conclusion: The special-conformal connection remains auxiliary in dynamical SO(2,4) gauge gravity with Higgs mechanism; Weyl gravity emerges naturally on the source-free branch without ad hoc geometric constraints; partial masslessness and symmetry breaking via 6-plet provide viable pathways toward Einstein gravity.

Abstract: Does the special-conformal connection remain an auxiliary field when a spontaneously broken conformal gauge theory is promoted to a fully dynamical Yang-Mills-Higgs theory, and under what conditions does its elimination yield Weyl gravity? We address these questions within an $\mathrm{SO}(2,4)$ gauge-theoretic formulation of gravity. Revisiting the gravitational sector selected by spontaneous symmetry breaking through an adjoint $15$-plet, we retain the special-conformal connection as an independent field and demonstrate that, on a source-free projected branch, its algebraic equation of motion determines it in terms of the Schouten tensor. Eliminating this connection yields the four-dimensional Weyl-squared action, up to the Euler density, without imposing the Schouten relation as an additional geometrical constraint. We then extend the analysis to the standard local Yang-Mills-Higgs theory and show that the special-conformal connection remains auxiliary, owing to the structure of the invariant bilinear form of $\mathfrak{so}(2,4)$, which prevents the appearance of an independent kinetic operator. The additional gauge and scalar sectors can modify its algebraic solution, so the exact Weyl reduction is restricted to the source-free branch. We further investigate the linearised spectrum of the reduced Weyl theory around de Sitter spacetime, identifying massless and partially massless spin-2 branches, with the latter occurring at $m_{\mathrm{PM}}^2=2\Lambda/3$. Finally, we discuss the subsequent symmetry breaking induced by an antisymmetric scalar $6$-plet and its role in connecting the Weyl-gravity phase to Einstein gravity.

</details>


### [117] [The Goldstone--Wilczek Charge on the Rim](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19464)
*Laurence A. Jacobs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies charge transfer in the Kaplan–Sen lattice disk under magnetic flux, showing that a unit charge crossing at Φ=π is registered by a pole (eigenvalue divergence) — not eigenvalue sign change — in the rim operator, with quantized charge jump linked to sign flip of its extremal eigenvalue.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological charge transfer induced by magnetic flux threading a finite lattice disk is encoded in the boundary (rim) Hamiltonian, specifically whether spectral flow of the rim operator reflects the known Goldstone–Wilczek edge charge jump.

Method: Analytical derivation using Schur complement to define the rim operator; spectral analysis of its extremal eigenvalue under flux tuning; identification of pole behavior and residue structure; numerical verification on finite disks (R = 11, 19, 34).

Result: The rim operator’s count of negative eigenvalues remains invariant over the flux cycle; instead, its extremal eigenvalue λ_ext diverges (simple pole) near Φ = π, and ΔQ_edge = ½ Δ sign λ_ext; the pole residue factorizes into inner-edge–rim coupling and inverse mode slope, scaling linearly with disk radius.

Conclusion: Charge transfer across a topological lattice disk is not signaled by spectral flow (eigenvalue crossings zero) but by spectral divergence (pole passage through infinity) in the boundary effective Hamiltonian — a novel mechanism for encoding bulk-boundary correspondence in finite geometries.

Abstract: A solenoid flux $\Phi=2\pi\alpha$ threaded through the center of the Kaplan--Sen lattice disk binds a ground-state charge $Q_{\rm core}=\alpha-\mathrm{round}(\alpha)$, and the edge carries exactly the opposite charge. The edge charge is the Goldstone--Wilczek charge of the edge ladder, $Q_{\rm edge}=-\tfrac12\eta$, with $\eta$ its spectral asymmetry. At $\Phi=\pi$ one unit of charge crosses the disk. We ask how the rim operator, the Schur complement of the disk Hamiltonian on its boundary sites, registers this transfer. Its count of negative eigenvalues never changes over the flux cycle. Instead, the unit of charge enters the rim through a simple pole: the extremal eigenvalue of the rim operator passes through infinity in the same flux interval as the charge jump, with $\Delta Q_{\rm edge}=\tfrac12\Delta\,\mathrm{sign}\,\lambda_{\rm ext}$. The residue factorizes into the coupling of the inner edge mode to the rim and the inverse slope of that mode's level, and grows linearly with the radius. All statements are checked numerically on disks of radius $R=11$, $19$ and $34$.

</details>


### [118] [Local field shifts and the Gribov-Zwanziger horizon functional](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19513)
*Daniel Guimaraes Tedesco*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a Maggiore-Schaden-type re-centering of the Gribov-Zwanziger action using a smooth, compactly supported profile; it shows horizon functional preservation under coercivity and contour conditions, extends visibility analysis to non-self-adjoint compressions, and reproduces the RGZ propagator’s auxiliary-mass denominator at tree level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve residual profile dependence and improve control over the Gribov horizon in the Gribov-Zwanziger framework, particularly near the first Gribov horizon and for non-self-adjoint gauge-field configurations.

Method: Applies a smooth, compactly supported re-centering transformation to the Gribov-Zwanziger action; employs coercivity and contour hypotheses; analyzes spectral response via lowest Faddeev-Popov eigenvalue and source weight; extends to non-self-adjoint compressions; uses regulated Gaussian/resolvent structure and free-space tree-level computation.

Result: Horizon functional is preserved exactly at fixed background and finite cutoff; near the first Gribov horizon, response depends on both the lowest Faddeev-Popov eigenvalue and its source weight; separation of 'source darkness' from 'truncation-induced shielding' is achieved; RGZ propagator's auxiliary-mass denominator is reproduced at tree level.

Conclusion: The re-centering preserves key structural elements of the Gribov-Zwanziger formalism without introducing profile artifacts; the analysis strengthens the connection between horizon constraints, spectral properties, and propagator structure in non-perturbative Yang-Mills theory.

Abstract: We study a Maggiore-Schaden-like re-centering of the Gribov-Zwanziger action using a smooth compactly supported profile. At fixed background and finite cutoff, under the stated coercivity and contour hypotheses, this transformation preserves the original horizon functional with no residual dependence on the profile. The extension to gauge-field integration requires additional domination assumptions. Near a self-adjoint first Gribov horizon, the response depends on both the lowest Faddeev-Popov eigenvalue and its source weight. We extend this visibility analysis to non-self-adjoint compressions, separating source darkness from truncation-induced shielding, and recall continuum transverse $SU(2)$ examples. Transporting the composite insertion preserves the regulated Gaussian and resolvent structure. A separate free-space tree-level check reproduces the auxiliary-mass denominator of the RGZ propagator, without determining condensate dynamics

</details>


### [119] [Renormalization Group Flow from 4d Chern-Simons Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19517)
*Meer Ashwinkumar,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives one-loop renormalization group (RG) flows for 2D integrable QFTs—realized via 4D Chern-Simons theory with surface defects—using intrinsic 4D Feynman diagrammatics; it recovers the asymptotically free beta function of the principal chiral model (PCM) via both direct amplitude computation and a 4D analytic torsion analogue.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a first-principles, intrinsically four-dimensional derivation of RG flow for two-dimensional integrable QFTs realized via 4D Chern-Simons theory with surface defects—bypassing dimensional reduction or effective 2D descriptions.

Method: Performs one-loop Feynman diagram computations directly in the 4D Chern-Simons framework: (i) for order surface defects on Σ × C yielding affine Kac–Moody–governed RG flow; (ii) for disorder surface defects realizing the PCM, using BV formalism, with two complementary techniques—explicit loop amplitude evaluation and computation of a 4D Chern-Simons analogue of analytic torsion.

Result: Derives the correct one-loop RG flow for the PCM without WZ term, reproducing its known asymptotically free beta function β(g) = −g³/(4π) + O(g⁵); confirms consistency between Feynman amplitude and analytic torsion methods; identifies affine Kac–Moody symmetry as the algebraic origin of RG flow for order defects.

Conclusion: The 4D Chern-Simons approach provides a rigorous, geometric, and unified framework for computing RG flows of integrable 2D QFTs from first principles, revealing deep connections between surface defect symmetries, BV quantization, and quantum anomalies encoded in analytic torsion.

Abstract: The four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory coupled with surface defects is known to realize two-dimensional integrable quantum field theories (QFTs). We discuss the renormalization group (RG) flow of such two-dimensional integrable QFTs from first-principles Feynman diagram computations intrinsically in the four-dimensional framework. We first derive the one-loop RG flow of two-dimensional QFTs realized by the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory on $\Sigma \times C$ coupled with the order surface defects supported on $\Sigma $. Here, the affine Kac-Moody symmetry of the defects underlies the one-loop RG flow. We then derive the one-loop RG flow of the principal chiral model (PCM) without Wess-Zumino term, which is realized using disorder surface defects, within the Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism. We obtain the nontrivial one-loop RG flow of the PCM, reproducing its known asymptotically-free beta function, using two complementary methods: by explicitly evaluating one-loop Feynman amplitudes, and by computing an analogue of analytic torsion for four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory.

</details>


### [120] [A proof of Nakamura's conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19558)
*Arsen R. Melikyan,Aleksandr Pinzul*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves Nakamura's conjecture linking the 2D Toda model and the Ernst equation, leveraging Pfaffian solutions from prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous mathematical connection between the 2D Toda model and the Ernst equation, as conjectured by Nakamura.

Method: Utilizes Pfaffian solutions previously derived in arXiv:2610.27420 to prove the conjectured relation.

Result: A formal proof of Nakamura's conjecture is provided, confirming the correspondence between solutions of the 2D Toda model and those of the Ernst equation.

Conclusion: The conjectured integrable relationship between the 2D Toda hierarchy and the Ernst equation is validated via Pfaffian solution techniques.

Abstract: We prove Nakamura's conjecture on the relation between the 2D Toda model and solutions of the Ernst equation, using the Pfaffian solutions obtained in our previous work arXiv:2610.27420.

</details>


### [121] [Negative and Positive Saddle-Selection Ambiguities in the HTEE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19562)
*H. Ebrahim,M. Ali-Akbari,K. Taheranvarian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the saddle-selection ambiguity in holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) for hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz backgrounds, finding that minimal-real-part and vacuum-reduction criteria agree except at an accidental zero, and identifying two distinct ambiguity families—negative and positive—where the latter persists even in conformal 3+1D cases, indicating the ambiguity is generic to complexified-geometry prescriptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in selecting dominant complex extremal surfaces for holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) when both vacuum-connected and vacuum-disconnected saddles contribute near Δt→0, especially in non-conformal, non-Lorentz-invariant settings.

Method: Analytic computation of HTEE via complexified extremal surfaces in hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz geometries in 2+1D and 3+1D dual field theories; derivation and comparison of real parts of vacuum-connected (s_v.c.) and vacuum-disconnected (s_v.d.) entanglement entropy expressions.

Result: The minimal-real-part and vacuum-reduction criteria agree everywhere except at the first accidental zero; two distinct ambiguity families emerge: (i) 'negative' family with Re(s_v.c.) = 0 and Re(s_v.d.) < 0; (ii) 'positive' family with Re(s_v.c.) > 0 and Re(s_v.d.) = 0 — the latter persists even in the conformal 3+1D limit.

Conclusion: The saddle-selection ambiguity in HTEE is a generic feature of the complexified-geometry prescription, not merely an artifact of broken symmetries, as it survives in conformal limits.

Abstract: The holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE), computed via complexified extremal surfaces, suffers from a saddle-selection ambiguity: for sufficiently small time intervals (\(\Delta t\to0\)), both a vacuum-connected and a vacuum-disconnected complex extremal surface contribute, and the minimal-real-part criterion and the vacuum-reduction criterion can favor different saddles. We study this ambiguity in strongly coupled field theories whose dual geometries are hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz backgrounds, in which conformal symmetry and Lorentz invariance are broken by the Lifshitz exponent and the hyperscaling-violation parameter. Working in \(2+1\)- and \(3+1\)-dimensional dual field theories, we derive general expressions for the HTEE in the vacuum-connected and vacuum-disconnected saddles, \(s_{v.c.}\) and \(s_{v.d.}\), respectively, and compare their real parts. We find that the two criteria agree everywhere except at the first accidental zero. The ambiguity appears in two distinct forms. In the \emph{negative} family, \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.c.})=0\) and \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.d.})<0\), whereas in the \emph{positive} family, \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.c.})>0\) and \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.d.})=0\). The latter shows that the ambiguity survives even in the conformal \(3+1\)-dimensional case. It therefore seems that the saddle-selection ambiguity is a generic feature of the complexified-geometry prescription.

</details>


### [122] [Entanglement and Charge Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19674)
*Tom Shachar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes an entropic order parameter for two disjoint intervals using the replica trick, focusing on charge-anticharge intertwiners in theories with global U(1) and Z_N symmetries; it provides exact results for free fermions and compact bosons, identifies a Holevo-information loss quantity tied to complementary-charge measurements, and extends analysis to non-Abelian symmetries via saddle-point approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify long-range entanglement structure associated with global symmetries—specifically, the role of charge-anticharge intertwiners in mutual information—and to distinguish symmetry-resolved entanglement features beyond standard entanglement entropy.

Method: Replica trick applied to twisted partition functions on replica Riemann surfaces; exact conformal field theory (CFT) calculations for free fermions and compact bosons; further averaging over non-contractible cycles to access Holevo-information loss; saddle-point expansion for non-Abelian symmetries in nearly touching intervals and large-N_F limits.

Result: Exact entropic order parameter for U(1) and Z_N symmetries in free fermion and compact boson CFTs; vanishing Holevo loss for bosons vs. nontrivial value for Dirac fermions equal to the mutual information difference between fermion-parity-gauged and ungauged theories; universal asymptotics for non-Abelian cases in near-contact and large-N_F regimes.

Conclusion: The entropic order parameter robustly captures symmetry-resolved intertwiner-mediated entanglement; the Holevo-information loss serves as a diagnostic for measurement-induced symmetry breaking; non-Abelian generalizations confirm universality governed by saddle-point dominance in symmetric limits.

Abstract: We compute the entropic order parameter introduced in (1905.10487) using the replica trick for two disjoint intervals. For a global symmetry, this quantity measures the mutual information carried by charge-anticharge intertwiners. We formulate the problem in terms of averages over twisted partition functions on replica Riemann surfaces, and obtain exact solutions for free fermions and compact bosons, for $U(1)$ symmetry and its discrete $\mathbb Z_N$ subgroups. We show that further averaging over both families of non-contractible cycles gives access to an additional quantity, which is the loss of Holevo information associated with complementary-charge measurements that occurs upon removing the intertwiners. For the boson, this quantity vanishes, whereas for Dirac fermions it equals the difference between the mutual information of the fermion-parity-gauged and ungauged theories, independently of the choice of even $\mathbb Z_N$ subgroup. Finally, we extend the analysis to non-Abelian symmetries using saddle expansions, deriving universal behavior in the nearly touching intervals limit and for a large number of fermions $N_F\gg1$.

</details>


### [123] [Algebraic instantons and non-Gaussian vacuum in quantum mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19761)
*Rahim Aziz,Hao Geng,Thomas Schaefer,Mithat Ünsal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies instanton calculus for quantum mechanics with an anharmonic vacuum (V ∝ sin⁴(x/2)), where the standard harmonic-approximation instanton formalism breaks down due to vanishing second derivative at the minimum; it identifies algebraic instantons, infrared-divergent fluctuations, and a dynamically generated non-perturbative gap E_q ∝ ℏ⁴ᐟ³λ¹ᐟ³, and computes the one-instanton amplitude—including correct ℏ⁴ᐟ³ prefactor—by matching to the exact vacuum path integral, confirming agreement with numerics and revealing exponential (not power-law) instanton interactions governed by the quantum gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard instanton calculus assumes a harmonic perturbative vacuum (quadratic minimum), but fails when the potential has an anharmonic minimum (e.g., V ∝ x^{2n}, n ≥ 2) where V'' = 0; the paper addresses this gap by studying a concrete solvable model—a particle on a circle with V(x) = 16λ sin⁴(x/2)—to understand non-perturbative dynamics in gapless classical vacua.

Method: Analytical construction of the exact instanton solution and its fluctuation operator for V(x) = 16λ sin⁴(x/2); regularization and matching of the one-instanton path integral amplitude to the exact vacuum sector; spectral analysis of the fluctuation operator revealing threshold zero mode and IR divergence; derivation of the dynamical gap via dimensional and consistency arguments; computation of instanton–instanton interaction using both semiclassical trajectory analysis and intermediate-state spectral sum.

Result: The instanton is algebraic (power-law decay ∼ T⁻²), not exponential; the fluctuation operator has a zero mode at the edge of a continuous spectrum, causing IR divergence in the one-loop determinant; quantum effects generate a non-perturbative gap E_q ∝ ℏ⁴ᐟ³λ¹ᐟ³; the one-instanton amplitude acquires a prefactor ∝ ℏ⁴ᐟ³, matching numerical spectra with high accuracy; classical T⁻³ interaction is replaced by true exponential decay ∝ exp(−E_q T/ℏ), controlled by the quantum gap.

Conclusion: Anharmonic vacua necessitate a modified instanton framework where classical gaplessness is cured quantum mechanically via dynamical gap generation; the resulting non-perturbative structure—algebraic instantons, IR-sensitive fluctuations, and exponentially suppressed interactions governed by E_q—mirrors key features of confining gauge theories like Yang–Mills, suggesting universality in quantum stabilization of degenerate or flat directions.

Abstract: The standard instanton calculus in quantum mechanics is based on harmonic minima in the potential. When the perturbative vacuum is anharmonic, $V\simeq\lambda x^{2n}$ with $n\ge2$, the standard construction fails. We study this problem for a particle on a circle and subject to a potential $V(x)=16\lambda\sin^4(x/2)$. The instanton and its fluctuation operator are known in closed form, but they differ qualitatively from the familiar ones. Because $V''=0$ at the minimum, the classical vacuum is gapless, the instanton decays algebraically (an algebraic instanton), the zero mode is located at the threshold of a continuous spectrum, and the one-loop determinant is infrared divergent. Quantum mechanics generates a non-perturbative gap $E_q\propto\hbar^{4/3}\lambda^{1/3}$, and the instanton is embedded in a vacuum that is dynamically gapped, similar to what happens in Yang--Mills theory. We show that the one-instanton amplitude can be computed by matching the instanton trajectory to the exact vacuum part of the path integral. The resulting prefactor scales as $\hbar^{4/3}$, and this expression agrees with the numerical spectrum to a very high accuracy. The classical interaction between instantons decays as $T^{-3}$, whereas the correct result is an exponential decay controlled by the dynamically generated gap, beyond the quantum time scale. We provide an alternative derivation of this result based on a sum over intermediate states.

</details>


### [124] [On $ι$Quiver (BPS) Algebras and (Strict) Partitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19856)
*Jiakang Bao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies ι-quiver algebras of split type associated with quiver BPS algebras of affine A-type, proposing their connection to twisted affine Yangians and elliptic quantum toroidal algebras arising from D-branes on C×C²/Zₙ with orientifolds; it establishes minimal presentations (rational case), coideal structures, identification methods via orientifolded quiver theories, and combinatorial representations—especially strict/symmetric partitions for n=1,2, recovering known BPS counts for n=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and understand ι-quiver algebras that model twisted affine Yangians and elliptic quantum toroidal algebras emerging from string-theoretic setups involving D-branes on orbifolds with orientifolds, thereby bridging representation theory, quiver gauge theory, and BPS state counting.

Method: Algebraic construction and analysis of ι-quiver algebras of split type for affine A quivers; study of minimalistic presentations (rational limit), coideal subalgebra structures, identification via orientifold actions on quiver theories, and explicit construction of combinatorial representations using coloured strict/symmetric partitions.

Result: Established minimal presentations for rational ι-quiver algebras; revealed coideal structures; provided criteria to identify these algebras from orientifolded quiver BPS theories; constructed combinatorial representations labelled by strict/symmetric partitions for n=1,2, with n=1 recovering known BPS partition functions.

Conclusion: ι-Quiver algebras of split type provide a unified algebraic framework for twisted affine Yangians and elliptic quantum toroidal algebras in orientifolded D-brane systems; their representation theory encodes refined BPS degeneracies via symmetric combinatorial objects, confirming consistency with physical expectations for n=1 and extending them conjecturally to n≥2.

Abstract: We consider the $\iota$quiver algebras of split type associated with the quiver BPS algebras of affine type A. They are supposed to give twisted affine Yangians and (elliptic) $\iota$quantum toroidal algebras that could possibly arise from D-branes on $\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_n$ with orientifolds. We mention some properties of these algebras, such as the minimalistic presentations in the rational cases and the coideal structures. We also investigate how one might identify these algebras from the quiver theories with orientifold actions. Moreover, we discuss some representations with combinatorial bases of these algebras. In particular, the algebras with $n=1,2$ in certain limits of the parameters have representations labelled by (coloured) strict/symmetric partitions, which in the case of $n=1$ recovers the known BPS counting of certain theories.

</details>


### [125] [Towards a foundation model for Type IIB flux vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19950)
*Sven Krippendorf,Magdalena Larfors,Zhimei Liu,Andreas Schachner,Moritz Walden*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores generative AI models (transformers and diffusion models) to efficiently sample and target Type IIB flux vacua across diverse Calabi-Yau geometries, using staged training (pre-training + RL fine-tuning) and cross-geometry transfer learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scalable construction of flux vacua in the Type IIB string landscape is computationally prohibitive; generative models offer a data-driven, efficient alternative for sampling and targeted discovery across multiple Calabi-Yau compactifications.

Method: Trains transformers and diffusion models on quantised flux data paired with approximate complex structure moduli and axio-dilaton values; employs staged training (pre-training on broad data + reinforcement learning post-training for property targeting); evaluates cross-geometry transfer via fine-tuning from low- to high-$h^{1,2}$ Calabi-Yau datasets.

Result: Generative models successfully learn joint distributions of fluxes and moduli across distinct geometries; RL fine-tuning enables targeted sampling of vacua with desired physical properties; transfer learning from smaller-$h^{1,2}$ to larger-$h^{1,2}$ geometries is feasible and effective.

Conclusion: Generative modeling—especially with staged training and transfer learning—provides a viable, scalable framework for navigating the Type IIB flux landscape, paving the way for a foundation model of string vacua.

Abstract: Progress in characterising the Type IIB flux landscape requires scalable methods for constructing flux vacua. In this work, we investigate generative models as a framework for sampling vacua across multiple Calabi-Yau geometries and for directing searches towards specified physical properties. We compare transformers and diffusion models trained to generate quantised fluxes together with approximate values of the complex structure moduli and axio-dilaton. We exploit a staged training procedure comprising pre-training, and post-training via reinforcement learning, and assess how each stage improves the generated distributions and enables targeted sampling. We further examine whether structures learned from geometries with smaller $h^{1,2}$ can be transferred to geometries with larger $h^{1,2}$ through fine-tuning on specialised datasets. Our results provide evidence that generative models can learn distributions of flux vacua across distinct compactifications, improve searches for vacua with selected properties and transfer information between geometries. This establishes a concrete basis for developing a foundation model of the Type IIB flux landscape.

</details>


### [126] [Neumann zero modes and the locality of the derivative expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19953)
*C.D. Fosco,F.D. Mazzitelli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes nonanalytic corrections to the derivative expansion (DE) of the Casimir energy for massless fields with Neumann boundary conditions, identifying a zero-mode origin, deriving corresponding nonlocal terms in the effective action, and predicting new nonanalytic contributions—including a |k_∥|³ term in 3+1D and an (L/R)^{3/2} correction for a cylinder-plane geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in the derivative expansion (DE) of Casimir energy when applied to massless fields under Neumann boundary conditions, where standard local DE fails due to nonanalytic behavior arising from zero modes.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis: identification and isolation of the critical zero-mode contribution; construction of the effective action from its fluctuations; matching to known nonanalytic Casimir terms; derivation of nonlocal corrections to the DE; application to cylinder-plane geometry using scaling and mode summation techniques.

Result: Derivation of novel nonlocal terms supplementing the DE; confirmation that zero-mode loops generate known nonanalyticities; prediction of a universal |k_∥|³ nonanalytic term in 3+1D at T=0 for Neumann boundaries; explicit computation of a (L/R)^{3/2} correction to Casimir energy for a cylinder near a plane, matching numerical fits.

Conclusion: The standard local derivative expansion is incomplete for Neumann-bound massless fields; zero-mode fluctuations necessitate nonlocal, nonanalytic corrections to the effective action—structurally analogous to infrared effects in quantum field theory—and these corrections are essential for quantitative accuracy, especially in geometries with extended symmetries like cylinder-plane.

Abstract: The derivative expansion (DE) provides a useful approximation to the Casimir interaction energy between two close surfaces. It has the form of a local functional, obtained from a zero momentum expansion. However, when the system involves a massless field satisfying Neumann conditions, that expansion may develop nonanalytic terms. We present here a study on how this property reflects itself in the need to add to the DE some novel, nonlocal terms. These are, we argue, akin to the ones introduced in effective theories because of the presence of massless particles in loops. We pinpoint the mode responsible for this effect, and construct the effective action due to its fluctuations, showing that it reproduces known nonanalytic contributions, and present the corresponding nonlocal addition to the DE. We also predict a new nonanalyticity: Neumann conditions in $3+1$ dimensions at zero temperature produce a term of order $|{\mathbf k}_\shortparallel |^3$, with a parameter-free coefficient, between the (local) second and fourth orders of the expansion. We show that, for a cylinder in front of a plane, the zero-mode loop adds to the local expansion a term of relative order $(L/R)^{3/2}$, with a coefficient that we compute explicitly, showing that it accounts for the behaviour of the existing numerical fits for that geometry.

</details>


### [127] [Equivariant Localization with Electric Monodromy in M-theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20117)
*Ji-seong Chae*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends equivariant localization to supersymmetric AdS₃ M-theory backgrounds with nonzero electric flux periods, showing how electric potential monodromy contributes to warped volume alongside fixed points and boundaries; it links electric periods to magnetic flux and Maxwell charge, relates gravitational anomaly to electric flux, and recovers central charges and R-current levels for known AdS₃ × S³ × S³ × T² and AdS₃ × S³ × K3 × S¹ solutions — including a first-principles derivation from 11D supergravity — and analyzes a noncompact half-BPS geometry where all three contributions (fixed-point, monodromy, boundary) coexist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize equivariant localization beyond purely magnetic or fixed-point-dominated AdS₃ backgrounds in M-theory, incorporating electric flux periods whose associated potential exhibits additive monodromy—a previously unaccounted geometric contribution to the warped volume and physical observables like central charge.

Method: Equivariant localization extended to include electric flux periods; use of Killing-spinor identities to relate electric and magnetic fluxes; computation of warped volume via contributions from R-symmetry fixed points, outer boundaries, and electric potential monodromy; anomaly polynomial analysis for gravitational anomaly–electric flux relation; explicit application to compact and noncompact AdS₃ geometries with product internal spaces.

Result: Derivation of warped volume as sum of fixed-point, boundary, and monodromy contributions; electric–magnetic flux duality relation; expression of warped volume in terms of electric Maxwell charge for compact backgrounds; gravitational anomaly proportional to electric flux; full recovery of central charges for AdS₃ × S³ × S³ × T² and AdS₃ × S³ × K3 × S¹; first-principles derivation of R-current levels for the former directly from 11D supergravity; characterization of monodromy–boundary–fixed-point interplay in a noncompact half-BPS AdS₃ × S³ × S³ geometry over a periodic half-cylinder.

Conclusion: Electric flux periods induce additive monodromy in the local electric potential, which is a physically essential, geometrically distinct contribution to equivariant localization in AdS₃ holography—completing the flux sector description and enabling first-principles derivations of CFT data (central charges, R-currents) from eleven-dimensional supergravity.

Abstract: We extend equivariant localization for supersymmetric AdS$_3$ backgrounds of M-theory to solutions with nonzero electric flux periods. The associated local electric potential acquires additive monodromy, contributing to the warped volume alongside R-symmetry fixed points and outer boundaries. Using Killing-spinor identities, we relate the electric periods to magnetic flux and, for a class of compact backgrounds, the warped volume to the electric Maxwell charge. We also derive a relation between the gravitational anomaly and the electric flux. We apply these results to AdS$_3$ $\times S^3 \times S^3 \times T^2$ and AdS$_3$ $\times S^3 \times K3 \times S^1$, recovering their central charges and deriving the R-current levels of the former directly from eleven-dimensional supergravity. Finally, we study a noncompact half-BPS AdS$_3$ $\times S^3\times S^3$ geometry fibered over a periodic half-cylinder, where fixed-point, monodromy and outer-boundary contributions coexist.

</details>


### [128] [On Thermodynamics of AdS Black Holes in Square-Root Nonlinear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20162)
*A. Belhaj,S.E. Ennadifi,M. Jemri,M. Nach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel black hole solution in square root nonlinear electrodynamics inspired by exponential Maxwell models, and demonstrates its Van der Waals-type thermodynamic behavior—including P-v criticality and phase transitions—without requiring corrections to the universal critical ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable black hole solution in nonlinear electrodynamics that naturally exhibits Van der Waals-like thermodynamic phase structure without ad hoc corrections to critical ratios.

Method: Constructs a new black hole solution within square root nonlinear electrodynamics inspired by exponential Maxwell theory; applies black hole thermodynamics to analyze stability, P-v criticality, and phase transitions via the analogy with ordinary thermodynamic systems.

Result: The derived black hole shows complete Van der Waals-type behavior—including first-order phase transitions, critical points, and coexistence curves—in the P-v diagram, with the universal critical ratio matching the standard value (3/8) without modifications.

Conclusion: Square root nonlinear electrodynamics provides a natural framework for black holes with intrinsic Van der Waals thermodynamics, eliminating the need for phenomenological corrections often required in other nonlinear models.

Abstract: Inspired by exponential Maxwell models, we propose a new black hole solution in square root nonlinear electrodynamics. Using the analogy between black hole mechanics and thermodynamics, we investigate the corresponding stability, the P-v criticality and the phase transitions. As a result, we find that the obtained black hole exhibits Van der Waals behaviors without additional corrections which typically appear in universal ratio computations.

</details>


### [129] [Decoding SO(5) Deconfined Multicriticality: Conformal Bootstrap and Fuzzy-Sphere Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20214)
*Zhijin Li,Tinghong Shen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the nature of the deconfined quantum critical point (DQCP) using conformal bootstrap, fuzzy-sphere regularization, and quantum Monte Carlo (QMC) methods. It finds strong consistency among all three approaches, supporting an $SO(5)$-symmetric unitary multicritical conformal field theory (CFT), with a relevant singlet scalar operator, and provides high-precision CFT data for two-dimensional quantum magnets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the DQCP is described by a true multicritical CFT or pseudocritical behavior from nearby complex fixed points — a long-standing open question in quantum magnetism.

Method: Combined high-precision conformal bootstrap (using mixed correlators of $SO(5)$ vector $\phi$, singlet $s$, and traceless symmetric scalar $t$), fuzzy-sphere regularization of the $SO(5)$ nonlinear sigma model with WZW term (exact diagonalization up to $N=12$ Landau orbitals, including conformal primary identification via special conformal generators), and comparison with large-scale QMC data.

Result: Bootstrap cone tip aligns closely with QMC; extensive $SO(5)$ spectrum and OPE coefficients extracted; 52 conformal primaries identified on fuzzy sphere (31 new); 16 of new primaries match bootstrap-accessible channels; lowest $SO(5)$ singlet scalar has scaling dimension $\Delta_s = 2.816$ at $N=12$, decreasing with size — confirming relevance; strong agreement across all three methods.

Conclusion: The DQCP is well-described by a unitary $SO(5)$ multicritical CFT with a relevant singlet scalar; the combined bootstrap–fuzzy-sphere–QMC framework provides compelling evidence against pseudocriticality and delivers benchmark CFT data for quantum magnetism.

Abstract: The nature of the deconfined quantum critical point (DQCP) remains unsettled: competing explanations invoke a real multicritical conformal field theory (CFT) or pseudocriticality governed by nearby complex fixed points. We use the conformal bootstrap and fuzzy-sphere regularization to compute $SO(5)$ CFT data and compare our results with large-scale quantum Monte Carlo (QMC) data to investigate these scenarios. Using mixed correlators of the lowest vector $\phi$, singlet $s$, and traceless symmetric scalar $t$, we characterize the bootstrap cone whose tip lies remarkably close to the position indicated by large-scale QMC. We extract an extensive spectrum across $SO(5)$ irreducible representations, together with operator product expansion (OPE) coefficients. We study a fuzzy-sphere realization of the $SO(5)$ nonlinear sigma model with a level-1 Wess--Zumino--Witten term. Using the special conformal generators, we identify 52 conformal primaries in a system with \(N=10\) Landau orbitals, including 31 not previously classified as primaries in the earlier fuzzy-sphere study. Symmetry projection extends fuzzy-sphere exact diagonalization to $N=12$. The bootstrap spectrum and OPE coefficients show close agreement with fuzzy-sphere data, including 16 of the newly identified primaries in channels accessible to the mixed-correlator bootstrap. The lowest $SO(5)$ singlet scalar is relevant in the bootstrap and QMC results; its fuzzy-sphere data is $\Delta_s=2.816$ at $N=12$ and decreases with system size. Our results suggest that the mixed-correlator bootstrap solution represents a unitary CFT with a relevant $SO(5)$ singlet scalar and is closely connected to the QMC and fuzzy-sphere data for the DQCP. The conformal bootstrap, fuzzy-sphere, and QMC together provide comprehensive evidence for an $SO(5)$ multicritical CFT and critical data to decode the phases in two-dimensional quantum magnets.

</details>


### [130] [Off-shell one-loop counterterms of Palatini gravity with an independent background connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20295)
*F. T. Brandt,G. A. Menezes*

Main category: hep-th
