TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop divergences of pure gravity in the first-order (Palatini) formulation using the background field method in Goldberg variables, revealing novel counterterms involving nonmetricity invariants—beyond standard curvature-squared terms—and confirms their on-shell vanishing and off-shell renormalizability via field redefinitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study quantum corrections in the Palatini formulation of gravity, where connection and metric are independent fields, and to clarify the role of nonmetricity in UV divergences—especially since the Gauss-Bonnet term loses its topological nature for nonmetric connections.

Method: Background field method in Goldberg variables; independent background connection and metric; computation of one-loop divergences for two-, three-, and four-point graviton functions in arbitrary dimension and general gauge; verification of background Ward-Takahashi identities; analysis of counterterm structure up to fourth order in nonmetricity.

Result: One-loop divergences require new curvature–nonmetricity invariants—not reducible to the Gauss-Bonnet combination—appearing in two-point functions; three- and four-point functions in Feynman–de Donder gauge fully determine the counterterm Lagrangian up to O(nonmetricity⁴); all counterterms vanish on shell and are removable off shell by field redefinitions.

Conclusion: The Palatini formulation of pure gravity is one-loop renormalizable in the same sense as the metric formulation: divergences are local, on-shell trivial, and absorbable via field redefinitions; however, the counterterm structure is enriched by nonmetricity-dependent invariants, reflecting the enlarged geometric content of the first-order formalism.

Abstract: We compute the one-loop divergences of pure gravity in the first-order (Palatini) formulation, using the background field method in Goldberg variables, with a background connection independent of the background metric. Background Ward-Takahashi identities are verified at one loop. The divergences of the two-point functions, obtained in arbitrary dimension and gauge, require, besides the curvature-squared terms, invariants built from the nonmetricity of the background connection; in particular, the Gauss-Bonnet combination, which is not topological for a nonmetric connection, does not suffice. Three- and four-point functions in the Feynman-de Donder gauge complete the counterterm Lagrangian up to fourth order in the nonmetricity and provide parameter-free tests of it. All the counterterms vanish on shell, as in the metric formulation, and off shell they can be removed by field redefinitions.

</details>


### [131] [Phase Structure and Thermodynamic Geometry of STU Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20328)
*Fil Simovic,Kenan Scavennec,David Kubiznak,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the thermodynamics and geometric structure of 4D STU black holes in gauged N=8 supergravity, deriving exact thermodynamic quantities in terms of physical charges, identifying a multi-critical phase structure, and using Ruppeiner geometry (with T and V as fluctuation variables) to infer attractive/repulsive microscopic interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the microstructure and phase behavior of STU black holes via geometric thermodynamics—specifically, by constructing and analyzing the Ruppeiner metric with physically motivated fluctuation variables (temperature and thermodynamic volume).

Method: Derives closed-form thermodynamic quantities (mass, entropy, temperature, volume, etc.) for non-rotating, pairwise-equal-charge STU black holes in 4D gauged N=8 SO(8) supergravity; computes the Ruppeiner metric using T and V as independent fluctuation variables; analyzes its renormalized curvature scalar to diagnose intermolecular (microscopic) interactions and locates critical points via the equation of state.

Result: Finds a rich phase structure with up to two distinct critical points when the charge ratio exceeds a threshold; identifies regions in parameter space where the renormalized Ruppeiner curvature is positive (repulsive interactions) or negative (attractive interactions); confirms consistency between geometric thermodynamics and conventional thermodynamic stability analysis.

Conclusion: Ruppeiner geometry with (T,V) coordinates successfully captures the microscopic interaction nature and phase structure of STU black holes, revealing that their microstructure is governed by both attractive and repulsive effective interactions depending on charge configuration—supporting the interpretation of black hole thermodynamics as an emergent statistical phenomenon.

Abstract: We study the thermodynamics of four-dimensional STU black holes, a family of charged solutions of gauged N = 8, SO(8) supergravity, from the perspective of geometric thermodynamics. We focus on non-rotating solutions carrying pairwise-equal charges, obtaining closed-form expressions for the thermodynamic quantities directly in terms of the physical charges, and use them to determine the equation of state and associated phase structure. We also construct the Ruppeiner geometry for the STU black hole, taking the temperature and thermodynamic volume as fluctuation variables, and study its renormalized curvature as a probe of the black hole microstructure. We find a rich phase structure with two critical points present whenever the charge ratio is sufficiently large, and identify regions in parameter space associated with attractive and repulsive microscopic interactions.

</details>


### [132] [Holographic Homogeneous Black Branes from Anisotropic Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20673)
*Markus A. G. Amano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs homogeneous anisotropic black brane solutions in Einstein and Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton gravity across arbitrary dimensions, using radial scaling compatible with spatial Lie algebra; it derives thermodynamic quantities (mass, entropy, Euclidean action) and quasinormal spectra for scalar probes, with applications to Bianchi and nilsoliton geometries beyond AdS asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and analyze black brane solutions with homogeneous anisotropic scaling symmetries—particularly those not asymptotically locally anti-de Sitter—which arise in non-relativistic holography and condensed matter applications but lack a general geometric classification framework.

Method: Imposes radial scaling symmetry compatible with the spatial Lie algebra structure; reduces full field equations to coupled radial ODEs and algebraic constraints on scaling exponents, curvature, and matter fields; constructs explicit neutral/electric/magnetic/dyonic solutions; computes Euclidean action and thermodynamic variables via counterterm-free methods; solves Klein–Gordon equation for massive scalars to obtain exact quasinormal modes and asymptotic mass bounds; performs numerical analysis of scalar relaxation in Nil, Solv, and Bianchi VI backgrounds.

Result: Explicit families of anisotropic black branes in arbitrary D, including 5D Bianchi-type and higher-D Ricci nilsoliton-supported solutions; complete thermodynamic characterization (scaling of mass, entropy, action); exact purely damped quasinormal spectrum for a class of scaling geometries; asymptotic scalar mass bound; numerical evidence for momentum- and geometry-dependent relaxation dynamics.

Conclusion: A unified geometric framework is established for constructing and analyzing homogeneous anisotropic scaling black branes beyond AdS; the results enable systematic study of thermodynamics and linear response in strongly coupled anisotropic systems, with implications for holographic non-Fermi liquids and Lifshitz-like critical phenomena.

Abstract: We construct homogeneous anisotropic black branes in Einstein gravity and Einstein--Maxwell--dilaton theory in arbitrary spacetime dimensions. By imposing compatibility between radial scaling and the spatial Lie algebra, we reduce the field equations to radial equations and algebraic constraints on the scaling exponents, curvature, and matter fields. We construct neutral, electric, magnetic, and dyonic solutions, including explicit five-dimensional Bianchi geometries and higher-dimensional families supported by Ricci nilsolitons. We investigate the thermodynamics of these solutions, obtaining their Euclidean action, mass, and entropy scaling. We also study the dynamics of a massive probe scalar, deriving an asymptotic mass bound and an exact purely damped quasinormal spectrum for a particular class of scaling geometries. Numerical calculations further explore the mass, dynamical exponent, and momentum dependence of scalar relaxation in Nil, Solv, and Bianchi type VI geometries. Our results establish a framework for studying thermodynamic and dynamical properties of homogeneous anisotropic scaling geometries that are generally not asymptotically locally anti-de Sitter.

</details>


### [133] [Doubly-spinning Black Saturns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20701)
*Jay Armas*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a six-parameter family of stationary, asymptotically flat 5D vacuum black hole solutions — 'black Saturns' — consisting of a central spherical black hole surrounded by a rotating black ring; it identifies the first regular asymptotically flat vacuum solution with two *extremal*, disconnected horizons, analyzes thermodynamic equilibrium (possible for non-extremal but not doubly extremal cases), and confirms consistency with known limits and blackfold predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize a new class of exact multi-horizon black hole solutions in five-dimensional vacuum Einstein gravity that realize physically interesting configurations — specifically, coexisting spherical and ring-shaped horizons — and to explore extremality, thermodynamic stability, and connections to effective-theory predictions.

Method: Exact solution construction within 5D vacuum general relativity using integrability techniques (e.g., inverse scattering method or Bäcklund transformations) adapted to stationary, axisymmetric spacetimes with U(1)^2 isometry; thermodynamic analysis via horizon geometries, surface gravities, angular velocities, and Smarr relations; comparison with limiting cases and blackfold effective theory.

Result: A six-parameter family of asymptotically flat, stationary black Saturn solutions is obtained; existence of a regime where both spherical and ring horizons simultaneously become extremal (zero surface gravity) is demonstrated; thermodynamic equilibrium (vanishing net angular momentum transfer and equal horizon temperatures) is shown possible for non-extremal Saturns but impossible for the doubly extremal case; all known related solutions (Myers-Perry, Pomeransky–Sen'kov ring, singly spinning Saturn) emerge as regular limits; phase structure matches blackfold predictions.

Conclusion: The black Saturn solution provides the first explicit, regular, asymptotically flat vacuum Einstein metric with two disconnected extremal horizons, enriching the taxonomy of higher-dimensional black holes; its thermodynamic behavior reveals a qualitative distinction between extremal and non-extremal multi-horizon systems, and its agreement with blackfold theory validates the effective description for such composite objects.

Abstract: We construct a six-parameter family of stationary, asymptotically flat solutions of five-dimensional vacuum Einstein gravity describing a spherical black hole surrounded by a black ring, with rotation in both independent planes. In a specific regime both horizons can become extremal, thereby providing the first example of a regular asymptotically flat vacuum Einstein solution with two extremal disconnected horizons. We also show that while non-extremal black Saturns can be in thermodynamic equilibrium, the doubly extremal Saturn cannot. The solution admits the doubly spinning Myers-Perry, the Pomeransky-Sen'kov black ring, and the singly spinning black Saturn as regular limits. We also show agreement with earlier blackfold predictions for the phase diagram of these solutions.

</details>


### [134] [A note on Padmanabhan's path-integral duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20731)
*P. Fernandez de Cordoba,J.M. Isidro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Padmanabhan's length-action duality to quantum field theory by constructing a field-theoretic framework where the duality S → L₀²/S is implemented via a nonlocal wave operator involving modified Bessel functions; it recovers the Klein-Gordon equation in the IR and exhibits exponential UV suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Padmanabhan's classical duality principle—relating proper length (action) S and a fundamental length scale L₀—to quantum field theory, bridging particle-based duality with field-theoretic dynamics.

Method: Starts from the particle propagator and its Schwinger proper-time representation; derives a scalar-field wave operator invariant under S ↔ L₀²/S; constructs a nonlocal differential operator using modified Bessel functions; analyzes spectral behavior in IR and UV limits.

Result: A well-defined nonlocal scalar field theory emerges; standard Klein-Gordon dynamics is recovered in the infrared limit; exponential suppression of high-momentum modes appears in the ultraviolet, indicating natural UV regularization.

Conclusion: Padmanabhan’s duality admits a consistent field-theoretic realization, yielding a predictive, UV-regularized QFT that preserves low-energy locality while modifying short-distance physics through intrinsic nonlocality.

Abstract: We develop a field-theoretic implementation of Padmanabhan's principle of invariance under the duality $\mathcal S \rightarrow {L_0^2}/{\mathcal S}$, where ${\mathcal S}$ is the proper length (the action integral along a particle's trajectory) and $L_0$ is a quantum of length. Starting from the particle propagator and its Schwinger proper-time representation, we derive the wave operator associated with the duality transformation in terms of a scalar field, rather than a point particle. The resulting theory is governed by a nonlocal operator involving modified Bessel functions. We analyse the infrared and ultraviolet regimes, demonstrate the recovery of standard Klein-Gordon theory at low energies, and show the appearance of exponential ultraviolet suppression. Our analysis provides a natural extension, to quantum field theory, of Padmanabhan's duality for point particles.

</details>


### [135] [Quantum chaos from time-dependent CFT states at large central charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20817)
*Shaun D. Hampton,Pantelis Panopoulos,Gideon Vos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies OTOCs in heavy, time-dependent CFT backgrounds at large central charge, using the monodromy method with subleading identity block corrections; it shows maximal chaos (Lyapunov exponent saturating the MSS bound) via analytic continuation from Euclidean to Lorentzian signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum chaos and scrambling in time-dependent heavy states of large-c conformal field theories, specifically whether such backgrounds support maximal Lyapunov growth in OTOCs.

Method: Monodromy method applied to four-point correlators involving two light and two middle-heavy operators on a heavy time-dependent background; includes subleading corrections to the identity Virasoro block; computes discontinuity contributions and performs analytic continuation to Lorentzian signature.

Result: The out-of-time-ordered correlator exhibits exponential decay with Lyapunov exponent λ_L = 2π/β, saturating the Maldacena–Shenker–Stanford (MSS) bound — i.e., maximal chaos.

Conclusion: Heavy, time-dependent background states in large-c CFTs can support maximal quantum chaos, as evidenced by saturation of the MSS bound in the OTOC, extending previous results beyond static or thermal states.

Abstract: We provide a first study of the behavior of four probes on heavy, time-dependent background states of conformal field theories at large central charge. Employing the monodromy method and including subleading corrections to the identity block, we compute the correlation function of two light and two middle-heavy operators on such a heavy background, by switching on a small interaction between the light and middle-heavy sectors. Summing the discontinuity contributions and analytically continuing from Euclidean to Lorentzian signature, we show that the out-of-time-ordered correlator (OTOC) exhibits maximal chaos, that is, the Lyapunov exponent saturates the MSS bound.

</details>


### [136] [From Quasinormal Mode Poles to Branch Cuts at Finite $N$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20912)
*Navid Abbasi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that the first quantum (1/N²) correction to holographic thermal correlators in AdS₅ black brane spacetime introduces branch cuts—rather than isolated poles—due to integration over QNM-pair poles, leading to a nonuniform large-N expansion at late times and a new exponentially slower decay regime ~ t^α e^{-C t^{3/4}}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum (bulk-loop) corrections modify the analytic structure and late-time dynamics of holographic thermal correlators beyond the classical (large-N) approximation, where quasinormal modes (QNMs) dominate via isolated poles.

Method: Computes the one-loop (1/N²) correction to scalar retarded correlators in planar AdS₅ black brane background; analyzes analytic structure by identifying QNM-pair poles at fixed loop momentum q, then integrates over continuous spatial momentum to reveal branch cuts; studies asymptotics near zero frequency and late-time behavior via saddle-point analysis.

Result: Loop integration converts QNM-pair poles into branch cuts accumulating at ω = 0; for QNM pairs with opposite real parts, residual frequency scales as q^{-1/3}, yielding infinite cuts at zero frequency; late-time correlator acquires a finite-N contribution G(t) ∼ N^{-2} t^α e^{-C t^{3/4}} with C > 0, slower than any classical QNM decay.

Conclusion: The strict large-N limit is nonuniform at late times: bulk quantum fluctuations qualitatively alter the analytic structure (poles → cuts) and introduce a distinct, slower-decaying late-time regime not captured by classical QNMs.

Abstract: In the strict large-$N$ limit, holographic thermal retarded correlators are typigcally meromorphic, with their late-time behavior determined by isolated quasinormal mode (QNM) poles. We show that the first bulk-loop correction, of order $1/N^2$, qualitatively changes this structure. In a scalar theory on the planar AdS$_5$ black brane, a one-loop bulk diagram generates poles at sums of two QNM frequencies at fixed loop momentum $q$; integration over the continuous momentum turns these QNM-pair poles into branch cuts. At vanishing external spatial momentum, for pairs built from QNM branches with opposite real parts, the leading light-cone contributions cancel at large momentum, and the residual QNM-pair frequency scales as $q^{-1/3}$. Consequently, an infinite family of cuts accumulates at zero frequency, while all tree-level QNM poles remain at finite nonzero frequencies. The first finite-$N$ correction therefore closes the gap set by the classical QNM spectrum. Competition between the decreasing QNM-pair damping and thermal Boltzmann suppression produces a moving saddle and a finite-$N$ contribution $G(t)\sim N^{-2}t^\alpha e^{-Ct^{3/4}},\, C>0,$ which decays more slowly than the leading classical QNM contribution. Thus the large-$N$ expansion is nonuniform at late times, and bulk quantum fluctuations generate a distinct late-time regime beyond the classical QNM decay.

</details>


### [137] [Higher-point amplitudes and recursion relations from the worldsheet in complex Liouville string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21091)
*Aleksandr Artemev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes multipoint string amplitudes in genus-zero complex Liouville string theory using worldsheet methods and analytic bootstrap, resolves prior inconsistencies in tachyon correlators with one degenerate field via higher equations of motion, derives a recursion formula matching the matrix model result, and proposes a geometric interpretation of ground ring correlators via tautological classes on moduli space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in prior calculations of tachyon correlators in complex Liouville string theory—specifically those involving one degenerate field—and to unify worldsheet and matrix model approaches via a consistent recursion relation and geometric interpretation.

Method: Explicit worldsheet computation of correlators with ground ring operators (including integration-cycle dependence), analytic bootstrap, application of higher equations of motion in Liouville theory, derivation of a recursion formula, and interpretation via tautological classes on the moduli space of curves.

Result: A corrected computation of higher-point tachyon correlators with one degenerate field; a recursion formula for amplitudes that matches the matrix model prediction; an expression for ground ring correlators in terms of tautological classes; and conceptual groundwork for extension to rational minimal strings.

Conclusion: The worldsheet approach to complex Liouville string theory, when augmented with analytic bootstrap and higher equations of motion, yields results fully consistent with the matrix model, and admits a natural geometric formulation on the moduli space—suggesting deeper structural unity across dual formulations of 2D string theory.

Abstract: We consider multipoint perturbative string amplitudes in genus zero in complex Liouville string theory (CLS) in the worldsheet approach. Based on explicit worldsheet calculations and analytic bootstrap, we first compute correlators involving ground ring operators, which additionally depend on a choice of integration cycle on the moduli space. Using this result and higher equations of motion in Liouville theory, we calculate higher-point tachyon correlators with one degenerate field and explain what contributions were missed in previous attempts to apply this method in the literature. This allows us to derive a recursion formula, which agrees with the general recursion for amplitudes known from "matrix model" approach to CLS. We also propose an expression for correlators with ground ring insertions in terms of tautological classes on the moduli space of curves and comment on generalization of these results to the case of rational minimal strings.

</details>


### [138] [Lifting asymptotic symmetries from metric to tetrad formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21296)
*Antoine Vincenti*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper addresses how residual symmetries in tetrad-based gravity formulations relate to those in metric formulations, showing that a reference tetrad provides a canonical, cocycle-free lift of metric residual symmetries into the tetrad symmetry algebra — yielding a semi-direct sum structure — and demonstrates this for a Weyl-BMS-type solution space with U(1) Lorentz freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity and field-dependent cocycle arising when lifting metric residual diffeomorphisms to tetrad symmetries under partial Lorentz gauge fixing, ensuring consistent algebraic realization of symmetries in first-order (tetrad) formulations of gravity.

Method: Uses principal bundle geometry: treats tetrad solution space as a principal Lorentz bundle over metric solution space; constructs a reference tetrad section to define a horizontal lift; proves the lifted symmetry algebra is a semi-direct sum; computes and cancels the cocycle explicitly in a Weyl-BMS example with time-dependent boundary metric and U(1) Lorentz freedom.

Result: A reference tetrad guarantees a cocycle-free lift of metric residual symmetries into tetrad symmetries, resulting in a well-defined semi-direct sum algebra structure; the naive lift fails due to a field-dependent 2-cocycle, which is explicitly computed and removed in the Weyl-BMS example.

Conclusion: The reference tetrad construction universally resolves the algebraic obstruction in tetrad formulations, generalizing the Kosmann lift to arbitrary residual symmetries and boundary conditions, and ensures compatibility between metric and tetrad symmetry algebras independent of gauge, Lagrangian, or group specifics.

Abstract: Metric solution spaces defined by gauge and boundary conditions are associated with a residual symmetry algebra, consisting of the diffeomorphism generators whose action preserves these conditions. Describing a metric solution space in terms of tetrad variables adds a local Lorentz freedom. When part of this freedom is fixed by additional tetrad gauge and boundary conditions, metric residual symmetries must be supplemented by compensating Lorentz transformations. If a residual Lorentz freedom remains, there is an ambiguity in these compensating transformations. The most direct choices generally fail to realize the metric residual symmetry algebra as a subalgebra of the tetrad one, because of a field-dependent cocycle. We show that a reference tetrad, that is a section of the tetrad solution space seen as a principal bundle over the metric one, always provides a lift free of this cocycle, so that the tetrad residual symmetry algebra is the semi-direct sum of the metric one and the ideal of field-dependent residual Lorentz transformations. The construction is independent of the gauge conditions, of the residual group, of the first-order Lagrangian, and reduces to the Kosmann lift on metric isometry generators. We illustrate it on a Weyl BMS-type solution space with time-dependent boundary metric, described in tetrad variables with a residual U(1) Lorentz freedom, for which the cocycle of a naive lift is computed and then explicitly removed.

</details>


### [139] [Carroll Double Copy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21388)
*Sucheta Majumdar,Silvia Nagy,Giorgio Pizzolo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a double copy for Carroll gravity from Carroll Yang-Mills theory at the linearised level, adapting the convolutional double copy to the Carrollian limit (c → 0), handling electric/magnetic sectors, introducing regularisation for magnetic on-shell configurations, and incorporating a Goldstone-type field to ensure Carroll boost covariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the double copy paradigm — a powerful correspondence between gauge and gravity theories — to ultra-relativistic (Carrollian) symmetry limits, where standard Lorentzian double copy constructions break down due to singularities and symmetry reduction.

Method: Starts from the convolutional double copy in Lorentzian spacetime, takes the strict Carroll limit c → 0, distinguishes electric and magnetic Carroll sectors, introduces a regularisation scheme for time-independent magnetic fields, imposes Carroll boost covariance via field dressing with a Goldstone-type scalar, and validates the construction first with scalar toy models then with linearised Carroll Yang-Mills and Carroll gravity.

Result: A consistent, symmetry-respecting double copy dictionary for Carroll gravity is established; it successfully maps solutions of linearised Carroll Yang-Mills (in all electric/magnetic combinations) to linearised Carroll gravity, including proper treatment of magnetic sector on-shell configurations and boost-covariant field redefinitions.

Conclusion: The double copy survives the Carroll limit when appropriately adapted: the electric–electric combination reproduces standard linearised Carroll gravity, while mixed and magnetic–magnetic cases yield novel gravitational sectors; the necessity of Goldstone dressing and magnetic regularisation highlights structural differences from the relativistic case and enriches the scope of double copy beyond Lorentzian settings.

Abstract: We construct a double copy for Carroll gravity as the ``square'' of Carroll Yang-Mills theory at the linearised level. Starting from the convolutional double copy prescription in Lorentzian spacetime, we take the Carrollian limit $c \rightarrow 0$ to establish a double copy dictionary compatible both with symmetries and the corresponding equations of motion. Since this limit yields two distinct sectors, namely the electric and magnetic Carroll theories, we systematically investigate double copy constructions involving all possible combinations of these parent theories. To accommodate the time-independent on-shell configurations of the magnetic Carroll fields, we introduce a suitable regularisation procedure for the convolutional double copy. In addition, the covariance of the double copy under Carroll boosts requires an appropriate dressing of the fields, leading to the inclusion of a Goldstone-type object in the construction. We first develop the formalism using toy models involving scalar fields and then apply it to the double copy between linearised Carroll Yang-Mills theory and Carroll gravity.

</details>


### [140] [Instant Folded Strings: Fractal Decay and ANEC Saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21424)
*Nissan Itzhaki,Uri Peleg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Instant Folded Strings (IFSs) appear to violate the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), challenging causality and unitarity; this paper shows that finite string coupling resolves the tension via a self-similar decay cascade governed by κ_ICS = 2√2 − 2, yielding a zero-energy, highly excited state with persistent negative integrated pressure—demonstrating consistent stringy low-energy dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent conflict between Instant Folded Strings (IFSs)—which arise naturally in time-dependent string backgrounds and black-hole interiors—and their violation of the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), which threatens standard causality and unitarity.

Method: Analytical semiclassical analysis of IFS decay at finite string coupling, including computation of the Malthusian branching exponent, energy-momentum evolution, ANEC saturation, and self-similarity of the fragmentation cascade in Minkowski spacetime.

Result: Finite string coupling triggers an infinite, self-similar fragmentation cascade with Malthusian exponent κ_ICS = 2√2 − 2; the total energy-momentum vanishes globally, yet every finite time slice carries negative integrated pressure; the ANEC is saturated, restoring consistency without violating null-energy constraints microscopically.

Conclusion: IFSs do not violate fundamental principles when string coupling is finite: their apparent ANEC violation is cured dynamically via cascade decay, yielding a stable, zero-energy, highly excited state that realizes macroscopic negative pressure while preserving microscopic null-energy consistency—a genuinely stringy low-energy phenomenon.

Abstract: Instant Folded Strings (IFSs) present a puzzle. On the one hand, they appear to be an intrinsic part of string theory: they are fundamental closed strings nucleated classically in backgrounds in which the string coupling grows toward the future, and they have been argued to play an important role in black-hole interiors and cosmology. On the other hand, their energy-momentum tensor violates the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), placing IFSs in tension with standard causality and unitarity arguments. We show that finite string coupling resolves this tension by triggering a decay cascade that saturates the ANEC in Minkowski spacetime. The remnant of the original IFS persists indefinitely, continuously seeding a self-similar fragmentation cascade that produces infinitely many strings over its full history. In the semiclassical regime, the cascade is governed by a single Malthusian exponent, $\kappa_{\mathrm{IFS}}=2\sqrt{2}-2$, which controls the branching growth, pressure decay, cutoff-ANEC convergence, and anomalous non-integer dependence on the string coupling. The complete state has vanishing total energy and momentum while remaining highly excited and carrying negative integrated pressure on every finite time slice. This provides a concrete example of genuinely stringy low-energy dynamics in which microscopic null-energy consistency coexists with macroscopic negative pressure.

</details>


### [141] [Learning from Duality in Neural Network Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21503)
*Pietro Capuozzo,Brandon Robinson,Benjamin Suzzoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs neural realizations of positive-level WZW models using bosonic Wakimoto and fermionic Fourier-Berezin fields; leverages level-rank duality in the fermionic construction to recover conformal blocks and transfer correlator information; and demonstrates that neural approximations of the same CFT current algebra exhibit architecture- and regularization-dependent finite-width behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge exact conformal field theory (CFT) structures—specifically Wess-Zumino-Witten models—with neural field theories by building explicit, interpretable neural representations that preserve algebraic and symmetry properties.

Method: Neural realization via bosonic Wakimoto and fermionic Fourier-Berezin field representations; use of level-rank duality to relate charged sectors; extraction of neural observables encoding conformal blocks; comparative analysis of finite-width approximation errors across architectures, regulators, and parameter distributions.

Result: Explicit neural observables successfully reproduce conformal block data; level-rank duality enables cross-theory transfer of unknown correlator information; distinct neural presentations of the same current algebra show quantifiably different finite-width approximation accuracies.

Conclusion: The expressive and computational properties of neural field theories are deeply tied to the underlying exact mathematical structure of CFTs; neural representations are not merely approximators but can encode and leverage algebraic dualities and symmetry data in a principled way.

Abstract: We develop neural realizations of positive-level Wess-Zumino-Witten models using bosonic Wakimoto and fermionic Fourier-Berezin fields. Level-rank duality connects the charged sectors of the fermionic construction, where explicit neural observables recover conformal block data and enable the transfer of information about unknown correlators between paired theories. We also show that distinct neural presentations of the same continuum current algebra exhibit different finite-width behavior, with approximation accuracy depending on architecture, regulator, and parameter distribution. These results connect the exact mathematical structure of conformal field theory with the expressive and computational properties of neural field theories.

</details>


### [142] [The Shape of the Strongly Coupled MQM Ground State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21606)
*Alexander Frenkel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a highly efficient family of variational ansatze—based on matrix-integral probability distributions with single-trace actions—for bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large N; achieves high-precision ground-state energy and correlators, matches bootstrap bounds, and captures 1/N corrections via connected two-point functions validated against resummed perturbation theory and Monte Carlo.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop computationally efficient and analytically tractable variational ansatze for the ground state of bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large N, bridging non-perturbative accuracy with scalability beyond mean-field or planar limits.

Method: Constructs variational wavefunctions whose norm-squared is a matrix integral with single-trace action (hence admitting its own 't Hooft expansion); optimizes few parameters to minimize energy and compute observables (single-trace moments, connected correlators); computes 1/N corrections via connected two-point functions; validates against conformal bootstrap bounds, resummed quantum perturbation theory, and Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: Achieves many-digit accuracy for ground-state energy, single-trace expectation values, and connected correlators; converges rapidly to published conformal bootstrap intervals at infinite N; stabilizes beyond them to several digits; accurately reproduces 1/N corrections in connected two-point functions, consistent with both resummed perturbation theory and Monte Carlo data.

Conclusion: Matrix-integral-based variational ansatze provide an unexpectedly efficient and systematic framework for non-perturbative calculations in large-N bosonic matrix quantum mechanics—capable of high-precision planar results and controlled inclusion of 1/N corrections.

Abstract: We study a surprisingly efficient family of variational ansatze for the ground state of bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large $N$. These are ansatze whose norm-squared probability distributions are themselves matrix integrals with single-trace actions, and so have their own independent `t Hooft expansions. They are efficient in the sense that optimizing over a small number of parameters produces many digits of accuracy for the energy, single-trace moments, and connected correlators. For infinite-$N$ planar data our results rapidly converge to within published bootstrap intervals and stabilize to a few digits of precision beyond them. We also demonstrate that we can efficiently capture $1/N$ corrections by computing connected two-point functions. These are matched against resummed quantum perturbation theory and Monte Carlo simulations.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [143] [$Q$-operators, cluster mutations, and flavored Toda chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19436)
*Saebyeok Jeong,Taro Kimura,Norton Lee*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs Baxter Q-operators for flavored Toda chains—quantum cluster integrable systems associated with quivers Q_N[F]—proves their commutativity, identifies shifted quotients as transfer matrices encoding commuting Hamiltonians, links them to rank-two Lax matrices, describes flavor mobility under cluster transformations, derives integral kernels and scalar Q-functions via Fourier transform, and connects the construction to 5D N=1 gauge theory BPS quivers and codimension-two defects on S^3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum integrability tools—particularly Baxter Q-operators—to flavored deformations of q-deformed Toda chains using quantum cluster algebras, and to establish connections with supersymmetric gauge theories and defect partition functions.

Method: Quantum cluster mutation sequences on quivers Q_N[F]; construction of Q-operators as ordered products of Faddeev’s quantum dilogarithms; proof of commutativity via quantum cluster algebra identities; identification of transfer matrices as shifted Q-quotients; derivation of integral kernels and scalar Q-functions via explicit Fourier analysis; mapping to Lax matrices and BPS quivers in 5D N=1 gauge theory.

Result: Baxter Q-operators for flavored Toda chains are explicitly constructed and shown to commute; their shifted quotients yield transfer matrices whose coefficients are commuting Hamiltonians; these transfer matrices match matrix elements of rank-two local Lax operators; flavor insertions are mobile under local cluster transformations while preserving spectral data; integral kernels and a factorized scalar Q-function are derived; the scalar Q-function is identified with the perturbative partition function of a codimension-two defect on a squashed S^3 in 5D N=1 theory.

Conclusion: The flavored Toda chains admit a full quantum integrable structure governed by commuting Q-operators built from quantum cluster mutations, unifying quantum integrability, cluster algebras, and supersymmetric gauge theory—specifically linking Q-functions to defect observables in five dimensions.

Abstract: We construct Baxter $Q$-operators for a family of quantum cluster integrable systems, that we call the flavored Toda chains, associated with quivers $\mathcal{Q}_N[F]$, where $F\subseteq\{2,\dots,N\}$ specifies data of $\vert F \vert$ additional nodes compared to $\mathcal{Q}_N[\varnothing]$ for the ordinary $N$-particle open $q$-deformed Toda chain. A distinguished sequence of quantum cluster mutations yields an ordered product of Faddeev's quantum dilogarithms. We prove that these operators form a commuting family and that their shifted quotients are transfer matrices whose coefficients are commuting Hamiltonians. We identify these transfer matrices with matrix elements of products of explicit local Lax matrices of rank two. Local cluster transformations move flavor insertions along the chain while preserving their ordered flavor parameters and the transfer matrix together with the canonical transformation. We also derive integral kernels for the $Q$-operators and evaluate an associated Fourier transform as a factorized scalar $Q$-function. Finally, we relate the quivers to the BPS quivers of five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories in the decoupling limit and identify the normalized scalar $Q$-function with the perturbative contribution of a canonical codimension-two defect wrapping a squashed three-sphere.

</details>


### [144] [Non-factorisable Yangian Lax matrices through oscillators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21333)
*Rouven Frassek,Henrik Jürgens*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators for type A, focusing on non-factorisable cases linked to prime snake modules; it proves generation of finite-dimensional skew representations by tensor vacua, derives vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials, provides a determinant dimension formula in terms of highest loop weights, and illustrates with a 21-dimensional example including basis and transfer-matrix identity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators—especially non-factorisable ones associated with prime snake modules—and to systematically describe finite-dimensional skew representations of $Y(\mathfrak{gl}_n)$ via tensor products of oscillator representations.

Method: Constructs Lax operators from ordered tensor products of linear oscillator representations; restricts them to submodules generated by the tensor vacuum; computes vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials using highest loop weight data; derives a determinant formula for dimensions; verifies the framework via an explicit 21-dimensional case with basis construction and transfer-matrix identity.

Result: Full oscillator realisation of Yangian Lax operators for skew representations; proof that such representations are vacuum-generated; explicit expressions for vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials; determinant-based dimension formula tied to highest loop weights; concrete 21D example with basis and transfer-matrix identity confirming consistency.

Conclusion: Oscillator realisations provide a powerful and computationally tractable framework for studying non-factorisable Yangian Lax operators and prime snake modules; the vacuum-generation property and determinant dimension formula unify and simplify the representation-theoretic description of finite-dimensional skew modules of $Y(\mathfrak{gl}_n)$.

Abstract: We construct oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators in type $A$, with particular interest in non-factorisable operators associated with prime snake modules. Our construction applies to finite-dimensional skew representations of $Y(\mathfrak{gl}_n)$. We prove that these representations are generated by the tensor vacuum in suitably ordered tensor products of oscillator representations. Their Lax operators are obtained by restricting the corresponding products of linear oscillator Lax operators to these submodules. We determine the vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials, and give a determinant formula for the dimensions directly in terms of the highest loop weights. A $21$-dimensional example illustrates the construction, including an explicit basis and a transfer-matrix identity.

</details>


### [145] [Observables and Symmetries in Causal Fermion System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18821)
*Patrick Fischer,Claudio F. Paganini,Sahil Yadav*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how observables and symmetries interact in causal fermion systems (CFS), showing covariance of position observables under unitary symmetries, deriving Heisenberg-type evolution for static CFS, and linking spacetime isometries to CFS symmetries via the Dirac equation—identifying the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian as the generator of induced unitary symmetry in static spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how physical observables (e.g., position) and spacetime symmetries are consistently realized within the background-independent, quantum-gravitational framework of causal fermion systems.

Method: Mathematical analysis of symmetry transformations in CFS: (i) derivation of covariance properties of position observables under unitary symmetries; (ii) Heisenberg-picture evolution for static CFS; (iii) construction of CFS from Dirac solutions; (iv) identification of conditions under which spacetime isometries induce CFS symmetries via infinitesimal generators tied to the Dirac equation.

Result: Position observables transform covariantly under unitary CFS symmetries; in static CFS, they obey Heisenberg-type evolution; spacetime isometries induce CFS symmetries iff the Dirac equation links their infinitesimal generators—specifically, the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian generates the induced unitary symmetry in static spacetimes.

Conclusion: The Dirac equation serves as the fundamental bridge between geometric spacetime symmetries and quantum-mechanical symmetries in CFS, enabling a consistent, operator-algebraic formulation of observables and dynamics without reference to a classical background metric.

Abstract: We investigate the interplay of observables and symmetries in the framework of causal fermion systems (CFS). We show that position observables transform covariantly under unitary symmetries and for static CFS we derive their Heisenberg-type evolution. To give these results a geometric interpretation, we establish conditions under which spacetime isometries induce symmetries of CFS constructed from solutions of the Dirac equation. The crucial link is provided by the Dirac equation itself, which relates the infinitesimal generators of the geometric and CFS symmetries. In static spacetimes, this allows us to identify the generator of the induced unitary symmetry with the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [146] [Energy-flow bubbles at the Schwarzschild horizon and their Regge-pole clock](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20200)
*Maciej Janowicz,Arkadiusz Orłowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies energy flow patterns (recirculation cells) formed by circularly polarized electromagnetic waves scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole, linking them to interference between background fields and photon-sphere surface waves; predicts cell births/deaths via a phase condition with π/4, validated experimentally; reveals topological structure—linearly polarized cores and torus-knotted flow lines—enabled by electromagnetic duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the fine structure of near-horizon electromagnetic energy flow in black hole scattering, particularly the origin, dynamics, and topology of recirculation cells arising from wave interference and geometry.

Method: Numerical mapping of time-averaged Poynting vector for κ = ωrₛ/c ≤ 10; analytical decomposition into background integral and Regge pole (photon-sphere surface wave) contributions; parabolic-cylinder approximation at the peak of the effective potential barrier to derive a phase-based death condition; use of electromagnetic duality to infer polarization and flow topology.

Result: Identified periodic birth of zero-circle pairs on the symmetry axis sliding into the horizon; discovered off-axis cells at higher κ; confirmed all 16 observed cell deaths occur precisely at zeros of the circularly polarized field on the horizon; verified π/4 phase condition predicted 8 deaths in 6 ≤ κ ≤ 10 within ±0.012; established linear-polarization cores and torus-knotted Poynting flow inside main-family cells.

Conclusion: The recirculation cells are universal interference phenomena governed by photon-sphere surface waves and horizon boundary conditions; their birth/death is quantized by a fundamental phase rule; electromagnetic duality dictates a topologically nontrivial flow structure—torus knots with fixed chirality—revealing deep connections between black hole scattering, wave asymptotics, and polarization geometry.

Abstract: For a circularly polarized plane wave scattered by a Schwarzschild black hole, the time-averaged energy flow on the side of the horizon facing the wave contains small, sealed recirculation cells. In the main family, pairs of zero circles are born on the axis and slide into the horizon in periodic frequency windows; at higher frequencies further cells appear off the axis. We map the cells for $\kappa=\omega r_s/c\le10$ and show that each is the interference of a background integral with the leading photon-sphere Regge surface wave. A cell can die only at a zero of the circularly polarized field on the horizon, and all sixteen measured deaths are such zeros. A parabolic-cylinder analysis at the top of the potential barrier reduces this to a phase condition with a constant $\pi/4$ and no fitted parameter; recorded before the measurement, it predicted eight deaths in $6\le\kappa\le10$ to within 0.012 in $\kappa$. Electromagnetic duality makes the core of each main-family cell a circle of linear polarization; inside such a cell, flow lines lie on nested tori and close into torus knots of one chirality.

</details>


### [147] [A treatment of d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians with noninteger quantum numbers and their connection to the radial momentum operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18823)
*Ali Bağcı,Zühra Güneş*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a detailed mathematical analysis of Bagci-Hoggan exponential-type orbitals—identified as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians with real (noninteger) quantum numbers—deriving their radial differential equation, verifying solutions via power series and Green’s function methods, establishing new transformations to noninteger Slater-type orbitals, and computing key quantum information measures (uncertainty products, Shannon entropy, Fisher information) and expectation values in closed form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish and exploit the identification of Bagci-Hoggan orbitals as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians with real quantum numbers, enabling broader applicability in quantum chemistry and mathematical physics where noninteger quantum numbers arise.

Method: Derivation of the radial differential equation from the closed-form orbital expression; verification via power series expansion and first-order matrix representation using the Green's function of the radial Laplacian; derivation of transformation relations to noninteger Slater-type orbitals; operator-based formulation using the radial momentum operator; analytical computation of expectation values, uncertainty products, Shannon entropy, and Fisher information.

Result: A general radial differential equation for Coulomb-Sturmians with real quantum numbers is obtained and verified; new exact transformation formulas between Bagci-Hoggan and noninteger Slater-type orbitals are derived; closed-form expressions for ⟨r⟩, ⟨p_r⟩, radial/Heisenberg uncertainty products, Shannon entropy S_r, and Fisher information I_r are provided.

Conclusion: Bagci-Hoggan orbitals constitute a mathematically consistent and analytically tractable extension of Coulomb-Sturmians to real quantum numbers, supporting advanced quantum information analysis and offering a unified framework for noninteger principal quantum number treatments in d dimensions.

Abstract: The Bagci-Hoggan exponential-type orbitals, the generalization of Coulomb-Sturmians to noninteger quantum numbers, were recently identified as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians. In the present study, this identification is treated in detail. The radial differential equation for these orbitals is derived directly from their closed form. It represents the general Coulomb-Sturmian differential equation for quantum numbers with real values. The solution is verified through a power series representation and a first-order matrix form based on the Green's function of the radial Laplacian. New transformation relationships between these orbitals and Slater-type orbitals with noninteger principal quantum numbers are established. The radial differential equation is expressed in terms of the radial momentum operator. The expectation values of the position and radial momentum operators are obtained in closed form. The radial and Heisenberg uncertainty products, the Shannon entropy and the Fisher information are derived in general form.

</details>


### [148] [Gravitational and Maxwell--Proca-Type Structures in a Variational Metric-Affine Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19001)
*Oldřich Stolín*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Einstein's nonsymmetric metric-affine gravity, deriving gravitational equations with geometric source terms, a torsion-based constitutive relation, and emergent Maxwell–Proca- and Lorenz-type field equations—all from pure geometry without external matter or electromagnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether electromagnetic-like dynamics can emerge purely from geometric degrees of freedom in a nonsymmetric metric-affine variational framework, avoiding ad hoc matter or gauge field couplings.

Method: Variational principle applied to Einstein's nonsymmetric metric-affine Lagrangian density with independent nonsymmetric metric g_μν and affine connection Γ^λ_μν; irreducible decomposition of torsion; analysis of symmetric/antisymmetric metric field equations; contraction and trace operations on Bianchi identities and connection equations.

Result: Symmetric metric equation yields Einstein-type equations with geometric sources; antisymmetric part yields a constitutive relation for torsion; its contracted four-divergence gives a vector-field equation interpolating between source-free Maxwell (Q=0) and Proca (constant Q≠0); trace of generalized Bianchi identities yields homogeneous Maxwell-type equations; connection equation implies a Lorenz-type condition.

Conclusion: A unified geometric origin for gravitational, electromagnetic-like, and massive vector field structures arises naturally within the nonsymmetric metric-affine framework—no external fields are required.

Abstract: We study Einstein's nonsymmetric metric-affine Lagrangian density with a generally nonsymmetric metric and an affine connection treated as independent variational variables. The resulting connection equation belongs to the classical Einstein--Hlavatý--Tonnelat framework, while the unrestricted metric Euler--Lagrange equation is analyzed without a divergence constraint on the antisymmetric metric density. Its symmetric part takes the form of Einstein-type gravitational equations with geometrically induced source terms and reduces to the vacuum Einstein equations in the torsion-free limit. The antisymmetric part becomes a geometric constitutive relation based on the irreducible decomposition of torsion. Its contracted four-divergence yields a general vector-field equation with geometric source and response terms. Under a trace--axial reduction, $Q=0$ gives a source-free Maxwell-type equation, whereas constant nonzero $Q$ gives a Proca-type equation. Independently, a trace of the generalized Bianchi identities yields the homogeneous Maxwell-type pair associated with the torsion trace, while the connection equation implies a Lorenz-type condition. Thus gravitational, constitutive, Maxwell--Proca-type, and Lorenz-type structures arise as compatible consequences of one metric-affine variational system. The antisymmetric dynamical level is generated by the contracted four-divergence of its constitutive level. No independent electromagnetic or matter field is introduced.

</details>


### [149] [On the Power-Law Duality in Wigner-Yang Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19246)
*Georg Junker,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Hooke-Newton duality—linking harmonic and inverse-square potentials—to Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, a reflection-deformed quantum framework based on Dunkl operators; it shows the duality persists for eigenvalue problems of harmonic and Coulomb-like potentials, generalises to arbitrary power-law potentials, and reveals that while deformation-parameter rescaling (analogous to Langer modification) replaces angular-momentum rescaling, the energy–coupling-constant exchange remains universal across classical, standard quantum, and Wigner-Yang settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether the classical and quantum Hooke-Newton duality—relating harmonic and Newtonian (Coulomb-like) potentials—extends to deformed quantum frameworks, specifically Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, and to understand how symmetries and scaling transformations adapt in reflection-difference operator formalisms.

Method: Analytical derivation and eigenvalue problem mapping: revisiting classical and quantum Hooke-Newton duality; formulating Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics on ℝ via Dunkl operators; solving Schrödinger-type eigenvalue problems for harmonic and Coulomb-like potentials; establishing duality-preserving mappings; generalising to arbitrary power-law potentials; comparing rescaling prescriptions (deformation parameters vs. angular momenta) and verifying coupling-constant exchange invariance.

Result: The Hooke-Newton duality holds in Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics: eigenvalue problems for harmonic and Coulomb-like potentials are exactly related via parameter exchange and reflection-aware rescaling of deformation parameters (not angular momenta); this extends to all power-law potentials; the energy–coupling-constant exchange relation is identical across classical, standard quantum, and Wigner-Yang frameworks.

Conclusion: Hooke-Newton duality is robust across mechanical paradigms—including reflection-deformed quantum mechanics—and its persistence underscores deep structural links between symmetry, scaling, and spectral equivalence; the required rescaling shifts from angular momentum (Langer) in standard quantum mechanics to Dunkl deformation parameters in Wigner-Yang theory, revealing a unified coupling-constant exchange law transcending formalism.

Abstract: In this work we first revisit the Hooke-Newton duality and its generalisation to arbitrary power laws in classical and quantum mechanics. We then consider Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics on the real line, which is a deformation of standard Heisenberg-Schrödinger quantum mechanics closely related to Dunkl's differential-difference operator formalism. We consider the corresponding eigenvalue problem for the harmonic potential and a Coulomb-like potential. We observe that the Hooke-Newton duality is also applicable to these deformed quantum systems allowing us to solve the eigenvalue problem of one by mapping it onto the other system. This approach is then generalised to arbitrary power-law potentials in Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics. Whereas in standard quantum mechanics the classical re-scaling of angular momenta has to be applied to Langer-modified quantum angular momenta, Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics requires a rescaling of deformation parameters similar to the Langer-type modification but involving the reflection operator. The energy coupling-constant exchange, however, remains the same in all three cases, classical mechanics, quantum mechanics and Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [150] [Absence of binding for the helium anion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19370)
*Hao Liang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the helium anion (He⁻) with three electrons does not bind in the nonrelativistic Coulomb model with infinite nuclear mass—i.e., no stable three-electron ground state exists; the three-electron energy equals the two-electron (neutral helium) ground-state energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the fundamental question of whether the helium anion can bind a third electron in the idealized nonrelativistic Coulomb framework—a longstanding issue in quantum few-body theory and atomic physics.

Method: Rigorous spectral analysis using decomposition relative to the helium ground state and completion of the square, leading to a one-dimensional variational inequality; employs exact nonrelativistic quantum mechanics with infinitely massive nucleus and spin-1/2 electrons.

Result: Proof that E^F(3,2) = E^F(2,2), i.e., the three-electron ionization threshold coincides with the two-electron ground-state energy—hence no bound state for He⁻; absence of a three-electron ground state is established.

Conclusion: The helium anion does not exist as a bound system in the given model; binding fails due to insufficient electron correlation stabilization to overcome repulsion, confirming nonexistence at the level of mathematical proof.

Abstract: We study the helium anion in the nonrelativistic Coulomb model with an infinitely massive nucleus and spin-$\frac12$ electrons. We prove the absence of binding, with $E^{\mathrm{F}}(3,2)=E^{\mathrm{F}}(2,2)$ and no three-electron ground state. A decomposition relative to the helium ground state and completion of the square reduce the problem to a one-dimensional variational inequality.

</details>


### [151] [Integrability of the Schrödinger Equation for a Mie-type Potential via the NU Method and Appell-type Hypergeometric Polynomials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19622)
*Kikunga Kasenda Ivan,Omalohoso Tasa Joshua,Likwolo Katamba Stanislas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a formal correspondence between Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials and exact solutions of the radial Schrödinger equation for Mie-type potentials using the Nikiforov-Uvarov method, revealing a dual structure of bound states (via confluent hypergeometric $U$) and scattering states (via generalized Laguerre $L$), with $\widetilde{e}_n$ emerging as s-wave scattering solutions when $l=0$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the physical significance of recently introduced Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials $\\widetilde{e}_n$ by linking them to quantum mechanical solutions—specifically, bound and scattering states of Mie-type potentials—and to provide a unified algebraic framework for both regimes.

Method: Application of the Nikiforov-Uvarov (NU) method to reduce the radial Schrödinger equation for Mie-type potentials to a hypergeometric differential equation; asymptotic analysis of solutions to distinguish square-integrable bound states (involving $U(a,b,z)$) from non-normalizable scattering states (involving a specific class of generalized Laguerre functions $L^{(\\alpha)}_n(z)$); identification of $\\widetilde{e}_n$ as truncations of scattering solutions in the $l=0$ limit.

Result: A dual solution structure is uncovered: (i) bound states require $N \\in \\mathbb{N}$ (Diophantine quantization), yielding discrete energy spectra via $U$; (ii) scattering states are described by generalized Laguerre functions, which truncate to $\\widetilde{e}_n$ for $l=0$, thereby characterizing s-wave scattering. The Appell-type polynomials are thus physically interpreted as exact scattering eigenfunctions in the s-wave limit.

Conclusion: The Appell-type polynomials $\\widetilde{e}_n$ are not merely mathematical constructs but encode fundamental physical information—specifically, s-wave scattering behavior for Mie-type potentials—while the NU-based hypergeometric framework unifies treatment of both bound and scattering states under a single algebraic structure.

Abstract: We establish a formal correspondance between the Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials $\widetilde{e}_n$, rectently introduced in [1], and the exact solutions od the radial Schrödinger equation for a family of Mie-type potentials. Using the Nikiforov-Uvarov (NU) method, we show that the radial equation reduces to an hypergeometric differential equation with a parameter $N$, which plays the role of an effective principal quantum number. A careful analysis of th asymptotic behavior of the solutions reveals a dual structure: the confluent hypergeometric function of the second kind $U$ yields square-integrable bound states, for which the condition $N\in \mathbb{N}$ provides a quantization condition of Diophantine type, and a discrete energy spectrum. In contrast, the generalized Laguerre function $L$, of a certain type, yields non-normalizable scattering states. In the particular case of vanishing angular momentum $(l=0)$, these scattering stetes truncte into the Appell-type polynomial $\widetilde{e}_n$, which thus characterize the s-wave scattering of the Mie-type potential. This work therefore provides a unified algebraic framework for both bound and scattering states, and clarifies the physical scope of the Appell-type polynomials.

</details>


### [152] [High-energy asymptotics for the spectral actions of free particles in thermal equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19648)
*Fabio Ciolli,Francesco Fidaleo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives high-energy asymptotics of the spectral action for free particle gases in thermal equilibrium on compact manifolds (torus and 3-sphere), under Bose, Fermi, and Boltzmann statistics, with special attention to logarithmic corrections in odd dimensions for relativistic massive particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend spectral action analysis to thermal quantum statistical systems—specifically, to obtain rigorous high-energy asymptotics of the average particle number as a spectral invariant, bridging noncommutative geometry, statistical mechanics, and spectral theory.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of spectral zeta functions and heat kernel expansions applied to Laplace-type operators on compact Riemannian manifolds (d-torus and S³); explicit treatment of Bose–Einstein, Fermi–Dirac, and Maxwell–Boltzmann distributions via contour integrals and Mellin transforms; dimensional regularization and residue analysis to extract logarithmic terms.

Result: Exact leading-order and subleading asymptotics (including dimension-dependent logarithmic terms for odd d in the relativistic massive case) for the average particle number spectral action on T^d and S³; confirmation that massless cases lack logarithmic terms, while relativistic massive cases generically exhibit them in odd dimensions—even on S³.

Conclusion: The spectral action for thermal particle number exhibits universal asymptotic structure governed by manifold geometry and statistics, with logarithmic anomalies arising intrinsically in odd-dimensional relativistic massive settings—a feature robust across flat (T^d) and curved (S³) geometries.

Abstract: We provide the asymptotics for high energy regime of the spectral action associated with the extensive quantity describing the average number of particles of a gas of free particles in thermal equilibrium. We analyse the case of massless particles (the massive non relativistic case being easier and well known) living in a finite volume manifold of arbitrary dimension, and subjected to periodic boundary conditions (i.e. the d-dimensional torus), obeying Bose, Fermi and Boltzmann statistics. We also treat in full generality the case of relativistic massive particles, which is more difficult and presents the appearance of a logarithmic term when the dimension of the underlying manifold is odd. The situation relative to massless and relativistic massive particles living on the 3-sphere is also studied, showing that, even in this case the asymptotics relative to the massive relativistic particles presents an analogous logarithmic term.

</details>


### [153] [Thouless's bandwidth conjecture: a proof by elliptic periods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19723)
*Yongqiang Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves Thouless's bandwidth conjecture for the critical rational Harper operator, establishing a universal asymptotic value for the total bandwidth scaled by denominator, linked to Catalan's constant; it achieves this via coarea analysis, local law derivation, exact finite-matrix formulas, nodal-annulus geometry, and rational Fourier identities, covering all reduced numerators and both denominator parities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously prove Thouless's long-standing bandwidth conjecture for the critical rational Harper operator — a fundamental problem in spectral theory of quasiperiodic operators with implications for quantum Hall physics and fractal spectra (e.g., Hofstadter butterfly).

Method: Combines spectral analysis, coarea formula techniques, local law derivation via integrable endpoint bounds (using positivity and evenness), exact finite-dimensional reduction per odd numerator, algebraic-geometric analysis of a compact genus-one family defined by boundary polynomials and point blowups, nodal-annulus integration to fix the period equation, and rational Fourier identities with uniform closed-path estimates to extend results universally.

Result: Proof that denominator × total bandwidth → 32G/π uniformly in reduced numerator, where G is Catalan’s constant; identification of a universal limit for normalized even coarea density; full characterization of the singular matrix limit including subtraction and normalization.

Conclusion: Thouless’s bandwidth conjecture is confirmed for the critical rational Harper operator. The universality arises from geometric–analytic structure (genus-one family, nodal-annulus constraints) and exact arithmetic properties (rational Fourier identity, boundary polynomials), extending robustly across all rational fluxes.

Abstract: We prove Thouless's bandwidth conjecture for the critical rational Harper operator: the denominator times the total bandwidth tends to $32G/\pi$, uniformly in the reduced numerator, where $G$ is Catalan's constant. We identify a universal limit of the normalized even coarea density. Positive real part and evenness give an integrable endpoint bound, so the local law determines the bandwidth. An exact finite-matrix formula reduces the local law to one normalized period for each fixed odd numerator. Explicit boundary polynomials, point blowups, and intersections determine a compact genus-one family. Nodal-annulus calculations fix its period equation and hence the universal density. An exact rational Fourier identity and uniform closed-path bounds extend the result to all reduced numerators and both denominator parities. The singular matrix limit, including its subtraction and normalization, is proved separately in detail.

</details>


### [154] [Matrix Star-Triangle Relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20056)
*Rinat Kashaev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a matrix generalization of the star–triangle relation and identifies its solutions relevant to the sl_n chiral Potts models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the star–triangle relation—a key integrability condition in two-dimensional lattice models—to a matrix setting, enabling new insights into higher-rank quantum integrable systems such as the sl_n chiral Potts models.

Method: Algebraic construction of matrix-valued star–triangle relations using representation theory of sl_n and analysis of associated Yang–Baxter-type consistency conditions.

Result: Explicit matrix solutions to the generalized star–triangle relation are identified, directly linked to the sl_n chiral Potts model Boltzmann weights.

Conclusion: The matrix generalization provides a unifying framework for integrability in higher-rank chiral models and opens avenues for constructing new solvable lattice models beyond the Ising-type hierarchy.

Abstract: We propose a matrix generalization of the star--triangle relations and identify solutions underlying the $\mathfrak{sl}_n$ chiral Potts models.

</details>


### [155] [The Maxwell Bethe-Sommerfeld problem I: Thin-layer induction and results for planar TE modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20188)
*Habib Ammari,Yu Gao,Jiayu Qiu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a thin-layer induction framework to construct a single periodic medium supporting infinitely many complete spectral gaps for divergence-form operators (TE-polarized Maxwell), thereby producing dense counterexamples to the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture and enabling arbitrary prescribed gap Chern number configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disprove the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture (BSC) for periodic divergence-form operators—particularly as a stepping stone toward disproving it for full 3D Maxwell—and to achieve precise topological control (Chern numbers) over spectral gaps.

Method: Inductive thin-layer insertion: a seed medium with a prescribed spectral gap is repeatedly embedded into progressively thinner layers of the unit cell, preserving and accumulating gaps while controlling topological invariants via careful seed selection.

Result: Existence of a single periodic medium supporting infinitely many complete spectral gaps; densely many counterexamples to BSC for divergence-form operators; realization of any prescribed configuration of gap Chern numbers.

Conclusion: The thin-layer induction framework establishes a robust method for engineering spectral gap structure and topology in periodic media, providing foundational tools to refute BSC for divergence-form operators and paving the way for its refutation in full 3D Maxwell systems.

Abstract: We present a framework for constructing a single medium that supports infinitely many complete spectral gaps for periodic divergence-form operators, which arise from the transverse electric (TE) polarization of Maxwell's equations. The construction proceeds by inductively inserting a seed medium with a prescribed spectral gap into a thin layer of the unit cell. As a result, we obtain densely many counterexamples to the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture (BSC) for divergence-form operators. Moreover, we prove that the thin-layer construction controls the gap topology: by carefully selecting the seed medium, one can realize a single operator with any prescribed configuration of gap Chern numbers. This is the first of two papers that contains all the fundamental ingredients for the thin-layer induction. In the second paper, we will apply this framework to disprove the BSC for the full three-dimensional Maxwell operator.

</details>


### [156] [Energy and Radius Estimates for Molecules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20206)
*Ioannis Anapolitanos,Michael Hofacker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically proves that ionizing a well-separated mixture of hydrogen and helium atoms requires energy — resolving a long-standing open question — and leverages this result to rigorously derive the van der Waals law for such systems, establish an improved lower bound on the size of stable multi-nuclei molecules, and prove energetic cost of ionizing an H₂ molecule near a conducting half-plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the decades-old open question of whether ionization of well-separated atoms costs energy — physically intuitive but mathematically unproven — and extend insights to molecular systems where geometry may relax upon ionization, enabling rigorous justification of asymptotic interaction laws (e.g., van der Waals, plate-molecule interactions).

Method: Rigorous analytical methods from quantum many-body theory and spectral analysis; derivation of energy lower bounds using variational principles and stability-of-matter techniques; application of geometric and electrostatic constraints (e.g., perfect conductor boundary conditions) to model long-range interactions.

Result: 1) Proof that ionization of well-separated H/He atoms requires positive energy; 2) Van der Waals law for H/He mixtures now follows unconditionally (previously assumed this result); 3) New, assumption-light lower bound on the minimal size (diameter) of stable multi-nuclei molecules; 4) Proof that ionizing an H₂ molecule separated from a perfectly conducting half-plane costs energy.

Conclusion: The paper establishes foundational energetic positivity results for ionization in atomic and hybrid atomic–macroscopic systems, enabling assumption-free derivations of long-range interaction laws and improving geometric constraints on molecular stability via novel lower bounds.

Abstract: The question of whether ionizing a system of well-separated atoms requires energy has remained open for decades, even though the answer is, from a physical point of view, obviously positive. The analog question for a system of molecules is even more challenging, partly because the molecular geometry is not known a priori and may change when electrons are added or removed. In this work, we investigate and analytically solve the first problem in the special case of a mixture of hydrogen and helium atoms. As a consequence, the van der Waals law for a system of hydrogen and helium atoms, which was proven in [6] assuming our result, now follows as a corollary without assumptions. Moreover, we prove a new lower bound for the `size' of a stable molecule with several nuclei, which improves upon the lower bounds from [1] with even less assumptions. Using among others this lower bound, we prove that it costs energy to ionize a system consisting of an $H_2$ molecule and a perfectly conducting half-infinite plate, provided that they are well-separated. The long-range asymptotics of their interaction energy was analyzed in [3] assuming our result.

</details>


### [157] [Lieb-Robinson Bounds for Electric Quantum Walks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20516)
*Houssam Abdul-Rahman,Christopher Cedzich,Günter Stolz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal Lieb-Robinson bounds for quantum walks under rational electric fields, showing exponentially suppressed propagation velocity with field period and proving equivalence of Lieb-Robinson and asymptotic velocities for shift-coin and split-step walks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify information propagation limits (via Lieb-Robinson bounds) in quantum walks subject to rational electric fields, and to clarify the relationship between Lieb-Robinson velocity and asymptotic transport velocity.
