Method: Analytic continuation of the dispersion relation; bounding its imaginary part; spectral analysis of the electric walk's Floquet operator; comparison of infimal Lieb-Robinson velocity and asymptotic velocity; derivation of exponential tail estimates outside the effective light cone.

Result: Optimal Lieb-Robinson bounds with velocity decaying exponentially in the field period; equivalence of infimal Lieb-Robinson and asymptotic velocities for shift-coin and split-step walks; a counterexample showing inequivalence in nonhomogeneous cases; exponential decay estimate for probability outside the light cone.

Conclusion: The Lieb-Robinson velocity is physically meaningful and attainable as an asymptotic transport speed in these periodic quantum walks, but not universally—its agreement with asymptotic velocity depends on homogeneity and structure of the walk.

Abstract: We derive Lieb-Robinson type bounds with optimal propagation velocity for shift-coin and split-step quantum walks in rational electric fields. The velocity decays exponentially fast in the period of the field, showing long intervals of suppressed spreading. The proof rests on bounds for the imaginary part of the analytically continued dispersion relation of the electric walk. We show that the infimal Lieb-Robinson velocity coincides with the asymptotic velocity for these walks, while a nonhomogeneous example demonstrates that the two types of velocity need not agree in general. We also obtain an exponential tail estimate on the probability of a walk to lie outside the effective light cone.

</details>


### [158] [Resurgent Analysis of the Single-Orbit Term in the Selberg Trace Formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20892)
*Wen Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic, Borel-Laplace, and Stokes properties of the single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula on compact hyperbolic surfaces, showing it is 1-Gevrey divergent, recoverable via Laplace integration, and its resurgent structure is governed by the resonance spectrum of the associated hyperbolic cylinder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the divergent asymptotics and resurgence structure of individual geodesic contributions in the Selberg trace formula, linking them to spectral resonances.

Method: Asymptotic analysis, Borel–Laplace transform theory, study of singularities of the Borel transform on a two-sheeted Riemann surface, and computation of Stokes phenomena via resonance sums.

Result: The single-orbit term admits a uniform 1-Gevrey asymptotic expansion; its Borel transform is meromorphic on the Riemann surface of √ξ with poles at cylinder resonances; Laplace integrals along generic directions converge; Stokes jumps are expressed as convergent exponential sums over the resonance spectrum.

Conclusion: The resurgent structure of the single-orbit term is fully encoded in the resonance spectrum of the corresponding hyperbolic cylinder, establishing a precise link between dynamical orbit contributions and scattering resonances.

Abstract: We study the large-time asymptotic expansion, the Borel-Laplace transforms and the Stokes phenomenon of the single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula. By a single-orbit term we mean the contribution from a primitive closed geodesic orbit and its iterations on a compact hyperbolic surface, and its formal large-time expansion is divergent. We prove that the divergent formal series is uniformly $1$-Gevrey asymptotic to the convergent single-orbit term and recover the single-orbit term by a Laplace integral along the positive real axis. Other directions for the Laplace integration are also explored by analyzing the singularities of the Borel transform of this $1$-Gevrey formal series. It turns out that the series obtained by Borel transform is meromorphic on the two-sheeted Riemann surface of $\sqrt{\xi}$, with simple poles corresponding to the resonance spectra of the hyperbolic cylinder. The Laplace transform can be performed along almost every direction on the two-sheeted Riemann surface. Finally, the Stokes structure as the limit of the difference between Laplace integrals along different directions can be computed by a convergent sum of the exponential of resonance spectrum. These results demonstrate the phenomenon that the resurgent structure of a single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula is reflected by its associated cylinder resonance spectra.

</details>


### [159] [Two-Dimensional Kowalevski Top Problem as the Genus Two Full Jacobi Inversion Problem -Formulation Using Kolossoff Variables-](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21012)
*Kazuyasu Shigemoto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the Kowalevski top using Kolossoff variables, yielding two explicit equations of motion—contrasting the classical Kolossoff method that yields only one explicit equation and relies on Hamilton-Jacobi theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a more transparent formulation of the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two-variable (time + auxiliary) Kowalevski top, leveraging known results in reverse via Kolossoff variables.

Method: Reverse utilization of known solutions to the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two-dimensional Kowalevski top; introduction and systematic use of Kolossoff variables to derive explicit equations of motion.

Result: Two explicit equations of motion for the Kowalevski top in genus two, derived directly from Kolossoff variables—bypassing the need for Hamilton-Jacobi theory and improving structural transparency.

Conclusion: The Kolossoff-variable-based reformulation provides a clearer, more direct solution to the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two-variable Kowalevski top, distinguishing itself from prior approaches by yielding two explicit motion equations.

Abstract: For the Kowalevski top, we introduce another variable in addition to time variable, and formulate as the two-dimensional model. In this paper, since the results for the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two variables Kowalevski top are already known, we utilize these results in reverse, introducing Kolossoff variables, to derive a more transparent formulation of the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem. Since there are two implicit equations of motion using Kowalevski variables, we derived two explicit equations of motion using Kolossoff variables, which is different from the Kolossoff method to derive only one explicit equation and to use Hamiltonian-Jacobi theory.

</details>


### [160] [The $N$-channel white-noise Schrödinger operator in all three Dyson classes. I. Explicit stationary admittance distribution, density of states and outgoing Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21067)
*Yan V. Fyodorov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the exact stationary distribution of the matrix Riccati (boundary admittance) variable for an N-channel Schrödinger operator with Gaussian white-noise disorder across all three Dyson classes (β=1,2,4), linking it to Kontsevich-type Airy matrix integrals; it yields exact finite-N results connecting Lifshitz tails and large-N spectral edges, and identifies universal edge scaling laws governed by Painlevé-I tritronquée and linear auxiliary equations—conjectured to apply to random band matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Halperin’s scalar solution for disordered 1D systems to multichannel (N > 1) settings across all symmetry classes, and to uncover universal spectral edge behavior in disordered quantum wires and random band matrices.

Method: Analysis of Riccati dynamics regularized by uniform absorption; derivation of stationary distribution via stochastic differential equations and symmetry-adapted Itô calculus; reduction to unitary orthogonal polynomials and auxiliary recursions for β=4 and even-N β=1; double-scaling asymptotics at the spectral edge; identification of Painlevé-I tritronquée structure via critical limit.

Result: Explicit stationary distribution of boundary admittance factorizing into Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals; exact finite-N formulas for density of states and sum of Lyapunov exponents; symmetry-dependent Lifshitz tail prefactors; N^{−4/5} edge scaling; edge profiles governed by complex tritronquée solution of Painlevé-I and a linear auxiliary ODE.

Conclusion: The matrix Riccati approach provides a unified framework for spectral statistics in disordered multichannel wires across all Dyson classes; the derived edge universality class—characterized by Painlevé-I tritronquée—likely extends to random band matrices, suggesting a new universality class beyond standard random matrix theory.

Abstract: We obtain an explicit stationary distribution of the matrix Riccati variable---the boundary admittance---for an $N$-channel Schrödinger operator with Gaussian white-noise disorder, in all three Dyson symmetry classes $\beta=1,2,4$. Uniform absorption regularizes the Riccati dynamics, and the distribution's normalization factorizes into outgoing Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals. This gives a common route to the density of states and the sum of Lyapunov exponents, extending Halperin's scalar solution to coupled channels. Exact finite-$N$ formulas connect two spectral regimes: the Lifshitz tail, including its symmetry-dependent prefactor, and the collective large-$N$ edge. We reduce the $\beta=4$ and even-$N$ $\beta=1$ problems to unitary orthogonal polynomials and an auxiliary recursion. Their critical double-scaling limit yields edge profiles on the $N^{-4/5}$ energy scale, governed by a complex Painlevé-I tritronquée solution and a linear auxiliary equation. We conjecture that these profiles describe a universal edge regime of random band matrices.

</details>


### [161] [A hyperhamiltonian approach to symmetry of the Dirac equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21090)
*Giuseppe Gaeta,Miguel Angel Rodriguez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the symmetries of the Dirac equation—including its Poincaré and dilation invariance, plus additional 'special' symmetries—in the framework of hyperhamiltonian dynamics, building on prior work that cast the Dirac equation itself in hyperhamiltonian form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize and unify the full symmetry structure of the Dirac equation—including both fundamental (Poincaré, dilation) and 'special' symmetries—within a geometric dynamical framework beyond standard Hamiltonian mechanics.

Method: Mathematical reformulation using hyperhamiltonian dynamics: leveraging quaternionic or hypercomplex structures to represent the Dirac equation and its symmetry group actions, extending earlier work where the equation itself was recast in hyperhamiltonian terms.

Result: Demonstrates that all known symmetries of the Dirac equation—including Poincaré invariance, wavefunction dilation invariance, and additional special symmetries—admit a coherent description within the hyperhamiltonian formalism.

Conclusion: The hyperhamiltonian framework provides a unifying geometric language for both the dynamics and the full symmetry algebra of the Dirac equation, suggesting deeper structural insights into relativistic quantum mechanics.

Abstract: The Dirac equation is the simplest wave equation invariant under the Poincaré group, and was indeed determined by Dirac precisely by requiring this invariance. Moreover it is invariant under dilations of the wave function, as follows by its linear character. It is known that the Dirac equation possesses, besides these, other symmetries, which in this sense are the special part of the Dirac equation's full symmetry. In previous work we have shown that the Dirac equation can be cast in hyperhamiltonian terms. Here we show that the symmetries can also be described in hyperhamiltonian terms.

</details>


### [162] [Group classification and equivalence of two classes of evolution equations of arbitrary order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21124)
*Vyacheslav M. Boyko,Oleksandra V. Lokaziuk*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits group classification of two classes of (1+1)-dimensional evolution equations, determines their point and contact equivalence groups, extends hodograph transformations to singular points, uses contact symmetry dimensions to distinguish classes for n > 2, introduces reciprocal transformations linking them at order three, poses a nonlocal classification problem, and classifies Lie symmetry extensions of the Burgers equation within Galilei-invariant equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify symmetry and equivalence properties of higher-order evolution equations, resolve ambiguities in existing classifications, extend classical transformations (e.g., hodograph) to degenerate cases (ux = 0), and uncover structural links (e.g., via reciprocal transformations) between seemingly distinct classes.

Method: Lie group analysis, classification of point and contact equivalence groups, hodograph and reciprocal transformations, symmetry algebra dimension analysis, and extension of Lie invariance algebras under Galilei invariance constraints.

Result: Complete description of usual point equivalence groups; recovery and extension of contact equivalences among second-order equations with 7D maximal Lie algebras; hodograph extension to ux = 0; dimensional distinction of contact symmetry algebras for n > 2; explicit reciprocal transformations linking the two classes at n = 3; formulation of a nonlocal classification problem; full classification of Lie symmetry extensions of the Burgers equation in Galilei-invariant generalizations.

Conclusion: The two equation classes are fundamentally distinguished by contact symmetry dimension for n > 2, yet linked nonlocally at n = 3; the analysis unifies and extends prior results, revealing deeper geometric and algebraic structures underlying evolution PDEs.

Abstract: We revisit the group classification of the $(1{+}1)$-dimensional evolution equations $u_t=F(u_n)$ and $u_t+uu_x=F(u_n)$ of arbitrary order $n\geq2$, where $u_n=\partial_x^n u$, and describe the usual point equivalence groups of the two classes. We recover the known contact equivalences between the three second-order equations with seven-dimensional maximal Lie invariance algebras and extend the hodograph construction to points where $u_x=0$. For $n>2$, the dimensions of the contact symmetry algebras distinguish the two classes. We give reciprocal transformations linking the two classes at order three and formulate a nonlocal classification problem. We also classify the extensions of the Lie invariance algebra of the Burgers equation in a wider class of Galilei-invariant equations.

</details>


### [163] [Precise Analytic Boundaries and Positive Symplectic Paths in the Stability Diagrams of Elliptic Relative Equilibria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21172)
*Jian Wang,Qinglong Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically characterizes spectral stability boundaries of elliptic relative equilibria in several N-body configurations (Lagrangian, Euler–Robe, (2+2)-body) by employing periodic and Hermitian Riccati decompositions; it derives explicit curves separating hyperbolic–hyperbolic from complex–saddle spectra, establishes an eccentricity-independent sufficient condition for monodromy hyperbolicity, and constructs a sharp lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve matching its circular-limit behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how linear stability of elliptic relative equilibria depends on mass parameters and orbital eccentricity—particularly across classical and generalized N-body systems—and to obtain rigorous, analytic descriptions of spectral transition boundaries rather than relying solely on numerical exploration.

Method: Uses periodic matrix Riccati decomposition to derive explicit analytic stability boundary curves; applies Hermitian Riccati factorization to obtain eccentricity-independent sufficient conditions for operator positivity under unit-circle boundary conditions; constructs a finite trigonometric test function to obtain an explicit lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve.

Result: Derives explicit analytic curves separating hyperbolic–hyperbolic and complex–saddle spectral types; obtains an eccentricity-independent sufficient condition guaranteeing monodromy hyperbolicity; constructs a sharp lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve that matches both value and tangent at the circular limit (eccentricity e = 0).

Conclusion: The combined analytical tools yield precise, closed-form descriptions and estimates of key spectral boundaries in stability diagrams for multiple families of relative equilibria, enabling rigorous classification of linear stability without numerical approximation.

Abstract: We investigate the dependence of the linear stability of elliptic relative equilibria on the mass parameter and eccentricity. Using a periodic matrix Riccati decomposition, we derive explicit analytic curves separating hyperbolic--hyperbolic and complex--saddle spectra. The construction applies to the Lagrangian, Euler--Robe, and $(2+2)$-body families. A Hermitian Riccati factorization also yields an eccentricity-independent sufficient condition for positivity of the associated operators under every unit-circle boundary condition, and hence for hyperbolicity of the monodromy matrix. Finally, we construct an explicit lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve by a finite trigonometric test function. This bound agrees with the value and tangent of the degeneracy curve at the circular limit. Together, these results provide analytic descriptions and estimates for distinct spectral boundaries in the stability diagrams.

</details>


### [164] [Coalescing and annihilating random walks on the ring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21186)
*A.M. Povolotsky,R. Tribe,O.V. Zaboronski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bi-Pfaffian structure for n-point correlation functions of coalescing-annihilating random walks on the 1D periodic lattice Z_N, generalizing the known single-Pfaffian structure on Z; it derives and solves kernel equations and identifies inhomogeneous Bernoulli initial conditions as yielding this structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Pfaffian point process characterization—previously established for coalescing-annihilating random walks on the infinite line Z—to the finite periodic lattice Z_N, where spatial compactness and topology necessitate new algebraic structure.

Method: Exact combinatorial and algebraic analysis: derivation of closed evolution equations for correlation functions; representation via matrix kernels; reduction to scalar kernels; explicit construction of solutions to kernel equations; identification of initial conditions preserving bi-Pfaffian form.

Result: n-point correlation functions on Z_N admit a bi-Pfaffian representation—i.e., a linear combination of two Pfaffians of 2n×2n matrices built from 2×2 matrix kernels expressible via two scalar kernels; the inhomogeneous Bernoulli initial condition (including deterministic limits) realizes this structure.

Conclusion: The coalescing-annihilating walk on Z_N does not yield a Pfaffian point process in general, but exhibits a controlled generalization—bi-Pfaffian correlations—under special initial data, reflecting the interplay between particle interactions, discreteness, and periodic boundary conditions.

Abstract: We study correlation functions in the model of coalescing and annihilating random walks on the one-dimensional periodic lattice $\mathbb{Z}_N$. We prove that, for a special class of initial conditions, the $n$-point correlation function can be represented as a linear combination of two Pfaffians of $2n\times 2n$ matrices constructed from $2\times2$ matrix kernels, which in turn can be expressed in terms of two scalar kernels. This is an analogue of the single-Pfaffian form exhibited by correlation functions of the same model on $\mathbb{Z}$, where it serves as a signature of a Pfaffian point process. We derive equations for the kernels and construct their solutions. We also show that a particular example of an initial condition leading to bi-Pfaffian correlation functions is the inhomogeneous Bernoulli point process, which includes a fixed initial configuration as a particular limiting case.

</details>


### [165] [Conditioned Reactive-Power Tuning Manifolds in Constrained Inverse Source Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21428)
*Mohamed R. Khodja*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a constrained inverse source problem for time-harmonic Maxwell fields in a spherical region with metamaterial loading, focusing on the 'tuning manifold'—the set of material parameters yielding zero reactive power for the minimum-energy source—and establishes its differentiability, local manifold structure, robustness bounds, and a dipole-based asymptotic reduction in the electrically small regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize material parameter sets (ε, μ) that enable self-tuned (zero reactive power) minimum-energy sources for prescribed exterior electromagnetic fields—crucial for designing passive or near-passive metamaterial-driven radiation systems.

Method: Functional-analytic treatment of the constrained inverse source problem in vector spherical harmonics; implicit-function-theorem-based manifold analysis; derivation of uniform asymptotic expansions for the imaginary part of complex power in the k₀a ≪ 1 regime using static electric/magnetic dipole approximations; numerical continuation to validate theoretical predictions.

Result: Proof of differentiability of optimal-source map and complex power P(θ) away from a singular set; generic local manifold structure of the tuning manifold ℳ = {θ : ℑP(θ) = 0}; quantitative conditioning bounds linking parameter perturbations to reactive-power error and distance to ℳ; controlled low-order dipole approximation of ℑP(θ) with uniform remainder estimates for k₀a ≪ 1; numerical confirmation of tuning curves and robustness classification.

Conclusion: The tuning manifold ℳ is generically a smooth submanifold with well-conditioned regions amenable to robust design; the dipole reduction provides an accurate, closed-form-free tool for predicting and engineering self-tuned behavior in electrically small metamaterial-loaded systems.

Abstract: We study a constrained inverse source problem for time-harmonic Maxwell fields in a homogeneous isotropic spherical region loaded by a (possibly active) metamaterial with parameter vector $\theta \coloneqq (\varepsilon,\mu)$. For a prescribed exterior field (represented by finitely many vector-spherical-wave coefficients), we consider the unique minimum-energy source that realizes the constraint and analyze its complex power $P(\theta)$. Our focus is the \emph{tuning manifold} $\mathcal{M}=\{\theta:\Im P(\theta)=0\}$, i.e., the set of material parameters for which the minimum-energy source is \emph{self-tuned} in the sense of vanishing reactive power. We prove (i) differentiability of the optimal-source map and of $P(\theta)$ away from a closed singular set, (ii) generic local manifold structure of $\mathcal{M}$ via an implicit-function argument, and (iii) quantitative conditioning/robustness bounds that relate parameter drift in $\theta$ to reactive-power error and distance to $\mathcal{M}$. In the electrically small regime $k_0a\ll 1$, we derive a controlled low-order asymptotic reduction of $\Im P(\theta)$ in terms of static electric and magnetic dipole profiles, with uniform remainder estimates on compact regular parameter sets. This yields local approximations of tuning curves and their conditioning without requiring a closed-form scalar profile. Numerical continuation experiments validate the one-parameter dipole reduction, the mixed-dipole tuning curves, and the identification of robust versus fragile segments of $\mathcal{M}$

</details>


### [166] [Normal ordering of Riemann-Liouville operators and a probabilistic formula for generalized Stirling numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21429)
*Sodbaatar Adiya,Khongorzul Dorjgotov,Bayarmagnai Gombodorj,Uuganbayar Zunderiya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies commutators between multiplication by x and Riemann-Liouville fractional integrals/derivatives, links them to generalized Stirling numbers S_{s;h}(n,k), constructs faithful operator representations, derives probabilistic interpretations via Dirichlet expectations and Pólya-type urn models, and extends results to arbitrary words in x and fractional operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize algebraic, analytic, and probabilistic structures arising from fractional calculus operators—specifically, to understand the normal ordering of noncommuting operators x and I^α/D^β, connect their combinatorics to known number sequences, and reveal underlying probabilistic models (e.g., Dirichlet and urn processes).

Method: Analytic functional analysis on locally integrable functions; explicit computation of integral kernels for (xI^α)^n, (I^αx)^n, (xD^β)^n, (D^βx)^n; derivation of recurrence relations for generalized Stirling numbers; representation-theoretic construction of a faithful algebra representation where v^s is defined for real s; probabilistic interpretation via Dirichlet expectation and de Finetti’s theorem applied to exchangeable sequences.

Result: A faithful representation of a deformation of the Weyl algebra parameterized by real s = 1 + 1/α; kernel formulas for (xI^α)^n expressed as Dirichlet expectations; probabilistic formula for generalized Stirling numbers S_{s;1}(n,k) (s > 1); identification of classical cases: s = 1 → Stirling numbers of first kind, s = 0 → second kind, s = 2 → Bessel numbers; extension to all words in x and fractional operators via product formulas from recurrences.

Conclusion: The interplay between fractional calculus operators and multiplication by x induces a rich algebraic-combinatorial-probabilistic structure: generalized Stirling numbers arise naturally as normal-ordering coefficients, admit Dirichlet-urn probabilistic interpretations, and unify classical combinatorial sequences under a single analytic framework.

Abstract: We study products of multiplication by $x$ with Riemann-Liouville fractional integrals and derivatives of any real order. For the integral $I^\alpha$ of order $\alpha$, the commutator with $x$ gives the relation $uv-vu=hv^s$ with $s=1+1/\alpha$. The coefficients of the normal ordering for this relation are the generalized Stirling numbers $S_{s;h}(n,k)$. We show that these operators give a faithful representation of the algebra in which the real powers of $v$ are defined. We expand $(XI^\alpha)^n$ and $(I^\alpha X)^n$ on locally integrable functions for every $\alpha>0$, and $(XD^\beta)^n$ and $(D^\beta X)^n$ on the range of $I^{n\beta}$ for every $\beta>0$. The kernel of $(XI^\alpha)^n$ is an expectation over the Dirichlet distribution. This gives a probabilistic formula for $S_{s;1}(n,k)$, $s>1$. By de Finetti's theorem, the formula describes an urn with $n$ colours: fractional integrals correspond to Pólya urns, and derivatives of integer order correspond to drawing without replacement. The urn probabilities are the terms of a product formula that follows from the recurrence. The cases $s=1,0,2$ give the Stirling numbers of both kinds and the Bessel numbers. The same method gives the kernels of all words in $x$ and fractional integrals.

</details>
